# Ask Hava architecture

Written in kit step K4 (`BUILD_BRIEF.md:598`). This file finalizes the module boundaries that `BUILD_BRIEF.md:124`–`BUILD_BRIEF.md:152` fixes, and pins the stack versions (`BUILD_BRIEF.md:121`). Since D-453 the server is Rust (`BUILD_BRIEF.md:105`); sections 2, 4, 5, 7 and 11 describe the Rust layout. It restates and arranges rules; where it had to choose, the choice is logged in `docs/decisions.md` (D-090 to D-100) and named here. If this file and a cited source disagree, the source wins and this file is the bug. Every PR that changes a boundary, table owner, state, timeout or pinned version updates this file in the same PR (`BUILD_BRIEF.md:489`).

Citations are `path:LINE`. "Changes file" means `sources/01_spec/Changes_Since_v9.3_AUTHORITATIVE.md`; "v9.3" means `sources/01_spec/Havasu_Home_Quotes_Technical_Blueprint_v9.3.md`. Requirement IDs (for example `PRIV-005`) are in `docs/spec/`.

---

## 1. Shape at a glance

One web app and one PostgreSQL database do all the work; every outside service sits behind an adapter (`sources/01_spec/Havasu_Home_Quotes_Technical_Blueprint_v9.3.md:44`; `BUILD_BRIEF.md:146`).

```
 Homeowner (text, web chat,      Business (email, portal  Casey (owner portal,
 quote page, homeowner portal)   links; no calls; text    alert emails, HELP
                                 only if chosen)          notifications, calls)
            \                         |                        /
             +------------ web process (Rust: Axum + Askama) -+
                                      |
          worker process (Rust: scheduler + task runner, same crates)
                                      |
                PostgreSQL 16: one schema per module, contact vault encrypted
                                      |
                         adapters (real | sandbox | stand-in)
   Twilio texts (homeowner number; a business number only if approved, TXT-035)
   Twilio calls | Twilio Verify | Stripe | Claude API (zero retention)
   Resend email | Lob letters | license sources | object storage
   Kept outside the database: Twilio and Stripe records, web request log,
   audit stream, AI structured readings (OPS-011)
```

## 2. Processes and code layout

| Process | Runs | Notes |
|---|---|---|
| `web` | The `server` binary (Axum on Tokio): pages, portals, web chat, webhooks, `/healthz` | Stateless; any number of replicas; server-rendered Askama pages that work without JS (`BUILD_BRIEF.md:106`) |
| `worker` | The `worker` binary: the scheduler and the task runner, polling `core.task` with `SELECT … FOR UPDATE SKIP LOCKED` | Timers, outbox sends, retries, nightly jobs (`BUILD_BRIEF.md:108`; JOB-006). Same image, started with a different command |

Both binaries are built from one Cargo workspace into one Docker image (`BUILD_BRIEF.md:119`), pinned by digest (SEC-021).

```
Cargo.toml           the workspace: members "app/*" and the Rust test-layer crates; every approved crate and its exact
                     version under [workspace.dependencies]; lints under [workspace.lints] (unsafe_code forbid; clippy
                     too_many_lines and excessive_nesting denied with their thresholds in clippy.toml; missing_docs;
                     every member sets [lints] workspace = true, which the architecture test checks, D-481)
Cargo.lock           committed
Dockerfile           multi-stage: cargo-chef planner, a builder that cooks the dependencies then builds both binaries
                     (`--release --locked`), and a `debian:trixie-slim` runtime pinned by digest with `ca-certificates`
                     and `libssl3t64` (Debian 13's name), running as user 10001; `RUSTUP_TOOLCHAIN` is set in the
                     builder so rustup does not fetch clippy and rustfmt; no `# syntax=` line; `Ci` logs in to Docker
                     Hub or uses a mirror because anonymous pulls are rate-limited (T019, D-491)
.sqlx/               offline query data from `cargo sqlx prepare --workspace -- --all-targets --all-features` (and `--check` placed before the `--`),
                     committed; every build and `Ci` run with SQLX_OFFLINE=true, and `Ci` fails on a stale entry
                     through `cargo sqlx prepare --check` after `sqlx migrate run` (D-481)
rust-toolchain.toml  the pinned Rust release (section 11)
clippy.toml          too-many-lines-threshold = 50, excessive-nesting-threshold = 4 (an unset or 0 threshold turns the
                     lint off, D-488, D-585)
deny.toml            cargo-deny policy: license allow-list, crates.io only, banned crates, yanked and unknown
                     sources denied, every ignore with a reason and expiry (SEC-021, SEC-045)
app/
  server/            composition root (a crate that depends on every module)
    src/lib.rs       `build_router()` and `register_handlers()`: the one composition both binaries call, and the
                     place that creates `privacy`'s `ReleaseMint` and hands it to `payments` (section 4, rule 4);
                     the shared composition file (D-481)
    src/main.rs      the `web` binary: installs the rustls crypto provider, builds the router, binds
                     `0.0.0.0:$PORT`, serves with graceful shutdown on SIGINT and SIGTERM (a container stop sends
                     SIGTERM; without the handler the exit is 137, D-491); `server migrate` runs `sqlx::migrate!` as
                     the release step (section 9); a missing or bad `PORT` exits 1 without echoing the value, and
                     `/healthz` is left to T106 (D-588)
    src/bin/worker.rs        the worker binary: same provider install, one Tokio runtime; it runs `core`'s
                             `Worker::run` (the claim loop, section 5: 4 tasks at once in a `JoinSet`, polling
                             200 ms to 5 s while idle) over `register_handlers()`; on SIGINT or SIGTERM it stops
                             claiming, drains for 20 s, gives back its leases and exits 0 (T013, D-708, D-710)
    src/bin/rules_bridge.rs  JSON-in, JSON-out door to each crate's `api::rules` for the twin (T074): one
                     long-lived process, one JSON object per line each way, requests `{id, call, args}`, answers
                     `{"ok": {id, result}}` or `{"error": {id, code}}` (about 0.05 ms a call measured, D-491); calls
                     `fee` (payments), `quiet_hours` (messaging), `transition` (jobs), `card` and `job_types`
                     (quotes over the license map and catalog) (D-1592); plain binary, no feature, linted with the
                     rest; its input and output follow a hand-written JSON schema
                     (`app/server/rules_bridge.schema.json`) that a contract test checks on the Python side
                     (`tests/contract/test_rules_bridge.py`) and the Rust side
                     (`app/server/tests/rules_bridge_contract.rs`), both with a small subset validator, since no
                     JSON-schema crate is allowed and Ci has no jsonschema package (D-481, D-1592); building it
                     compiles every crate's query macros, so `twin.yml` sets `SQLX_OFFLINE=true`
  core/              shared kernel (section 5); not a business module; `build.rs` is `sqlx migrate build-script --source ../../migrations` with a `//!` line (D-488)
                     so a new migration file is seen without a clean build (T012); `src/db.rs` holds `MIGRATOR`, `app_pool`,
                     `migrate` (run by `server migrate` over `ASKHAVA_MIGRATION_DATABASE_URL`) and `Guarded<T>` (D-698, D-700);
                     `sqlx.toml` maps `'TIMESTAMPTZ'` to `jiff_sqlx::Timestamp` (D-488)
                     `src/test_control.rs` is the test-control router (`/__test/clock`, `/__test/clock/advance`,
                     `/__test/messages`, proxied to the Twilio stand-in's sent log) and the clock both binaries run on;
                     the routes compile only under core's `test-control` feature, the one Cargo feature allowed, which
                     no manifest and no Docker build enables (e2e builds pass `--features askhava-core/test-control`),
                     and need a per-run bearer secret on every `/__test/` request (SEC-035, D-739, D-740, D-742)
  <module>/          one crate per module in section 3, package name `askhava-<module>` (lib `askhava_<module>`), so
                     no first-party crate shadows Rust's built-in `core` or another standard name (D-484)
    Cargo.toml       depends only on the lower crates section 4 allows (the compiler refuses anything else)
    src/lib.rs       hand-written: `pub mod api;` plus private `mod models; mod rules; mod service; mod handlers;
                     mod routes;` and `#[cfg(test)] mod tests;` (no automod, D-481)
    src/api.rs       the module's public functions and types; `pub mod rules` inside it re-exports the pure
                     decision functions (no I/O, no tables), which is how the rules_bridge and the property tests
                     of other crates reach them without a feature; everything else in the crate is private
    src/models.rs    row types and SQLx queries, all in the module's own Postgres schema
    src/rules.rs     pure decision logic (no I/O), the main target of unit and property tests
    src/status.rs    the crate's status enums and every `match` on them, with clippy
                     `wildcard_enum_match_arm` and `match_wildcard_for_single_variants` denied on this module only (SEC-031, D-488); nothing outside it compares a
                     status with `==` or glob-imports one (`scripts/check_rust_rules.py`; D-590 lists the shapes it catches)
    src/service.rs   orchestration: transactions, calls to lower crates' api, audit rows; errors are a
                     `thiserror` enum per crate (no anyhow), mapped once in `server` to responses, never carrying a
                     `Redacted` value in `Display`
    src/handlers.rs  task handlers registered in server/src/lib.rs (timers and events)
    src/routes.rs    HTTP routes, only in crates that own a web surface (portals, site, webhooks)
    src/tests/       the crate's unit and property tests, one file per task, each declared by one `mod` line in
                     src/tests/mod.rs (a shared path); they see the crate's private items under `#[cfg(test)]`
                     and need no feature (D-481)
    templates/       Askama templates (checked when the crate compiles), served through askama_web; all
                     customer-facing words live in templates and message files, never in `.rs` string literals,
                     so the AI-smell scan sees them. A scan bans `|safe`, `escape = "none"` and a user value in an
                     href, src, style or script context. The `.vcf` contact card is not Askama: one function
                     escapes each field per RFC 6350 (backslash, comma, semicolon, newline) before it is written.
                     The shared layout of `docs/design/DESIGN_SPEC.md` lives in `app/site/templates/` and the
                     other crates reach it through `askama.toml` `dirs` (D-481)
    static/          CSS, fonts, images and `.ts` sources; `npm run build` (tsc) writes the `.js` beside each
                     source in the Docker build stage and before local and CI e2e runs (T173); the generated
                     `.js` is not committed (`npm run build` is `tsc --ignoreConfig --strict` over
                     `app/*/static/*.ts`); the image keeps only the `.js`, at `/srv/askhava/static/<crate>/`,
                     the `/static/<crate>/` paths templates load (D-1281); to be served by tower-http `ServeDir`
                     with content-hash cache busting (no route serves `/static/` yet, D-1235)
migrations/          SQL files `0NNN_<slug>.sql`, NNN the owning task's serial position in the runner's order (D-485; gaps are
                     allowed, numbers only rise, and a number is frozen once applied to the test project, D-491), each
                     beginning `-- <module> (T###)`, `<module>` being the schema the migration touches (D-492) (line 2 when
                     `-- no-transaction` must be line 1), forward-only, each
                     tested; SQLx applies
                     any not yet applied in number order, including a lower number added later (checked, D-453).
                     On a fresh database the files run in number order, not task order; the architecture test
                     (`tests/integration/tests/architecture_migrations.rs`, T226) fails if a migration's number is
                     higher than that of a migration whose task depends on its own, whatever schemas they touch
                     (two tasks that may run in either order never fail), and each migration file names its
                     owning module in a leading comment (D-481, D-600); it also fails on dynamic SQL, which hides
                     a schema name from it: a `DO` statement, an `EXECUTE` that runs a string or a `format`/`quote_*`
                     call in a migration, or `DO`/`EXECUTE` in a query macro's SQL under `app/`, unless the file is
                     in the test's `DYNAMIC_SQL_ALLOWED` list with a reason (only 0019's role guard; a stale entry
                     fails too) (TD-SEC-36, T252, D-1677)
stand_ins/           Python fakes of Twilio, Stripe, Resend, Lob and the AI, served over HTTP by `stand_ins/server.py`
                     at the vendors' URL shapes, so the Rust adapters use the same client code everywhere (SEC-032)
tests/               the Rust test-layer crates integration, security and recovery (package `askhava-recovery-tests`, since the
                     recovery module already holds the name, D-488) (T168, and integration in
                     T175), files in tests/<layer>/tests/, each reaching the app crates only through their `api`;
                     compile-fail cases with their expected `.stderr` in tests/security/ui/, built by trybuild in a
                     crate with no feature on, so a proof type's privacy is tested as production sees it;
                     `tests/fixtures` (T010) is the fake-data crate; Playwright in TypeScript for e2e and a11y;
                     `tests/e2e/fixtures.ts` gives every spec the `control` fixture over the test-control router,
                     `tests/e2e/webserver.ts` (the config's `globalSetup`, D-958) builds and starts the stand-ins,
                     worker and web binary with `test-control` and stops them after the run;
                     `tests/a11y/` (T053, D-1238 to D-1241) reuses that `globalSetup`, reads its page list from
                     the GET routes in app/*/src (a route with no seeded state fails), takes each route's states
                     and seed function from the shared data file `tests/a11y/page_states.ts` (T267, D-1788,
                     D-2106; the checks stay in `fixtures.ts`), seeds each state's fake
                     data through psql on DATABASE_URL and runs axe and the structure, target, contrast, reflow,
                     spacing and motion checks at 320, 768 and 1280 px (CSP bypassed only for text spacing,
                     D-1241; JSON report in test-results/a11y-report); `Ci`'s `E2e` job runs tests/e2e and
                     `Ally` runs tests/a11y, each with Node 22, `npm run build`, cached Chromium, a test-control
                     build and a Postgres service (stripe-mock too in `E2e`), T173, D-1282; and
                     `package.json`, `tsconfig.json` and `eslint.config.js` at the root pin the TypeScript tooling (D-741);
                     pytest for docs and contract. Every database test uses `#[sqlx::test(migrator = ...)]`, one
                     fresh database per test (D-481); `tests/mutation/mutants.toml` holds the named mutants
                     (exact text replacements and the tests that must fail), applied in place by
                     `scripts/run_named_mutants.py`: `Ci`'s `Checks` runs those whose rule or test files a branch changed
                     (a stale `find` is reported there, failed only at night),
                     nightly-main's `named-mutants` job all of them, and its `cargo-mutants` job the listed rule
                     files, report only for now (T056, D-1285, D-1286)
twin/                the twin (Python) and its hard-failing gate, calling the Rust rules through rules_bridge
wargames/            the Phase 2 war games (Python, standard library): personas, scenario runner, chaos injectors and the
                     money reconciliation, driving the Railway test site's real entry points (T234, T235, D-1154)
```

- Sentinel scan: `tests/security/tests/sentinel_db_logs.rs` (T217) writes the fixture sentinels (`askhava-fixtures`) through the vault, sends a request carrying them through the `build_router` layers and runs the off-database stream, then searches every table but `privacy.contact` (bytea as hex too), the JSON log, the stream's stand-in objects and a capturing Sentry transport for them; T120 repeats it after J1 to J4 (TD-PRIV-02, PRIV-016, PRIV-019, D-795).
- AI calls: `app/ai` sends only `ScrubbedText` through its `Transport` (over `adapters`' `StandInAi` in the build) to `/v1/messages`, with the versioned instruction, the text inside one escaped `<homeowner_message>` tag, `schemas/intake_reading.json` as `output_config` and no tools; it retries per AI-023 within the `ASKHAVA_AI_TIMEOUT_SECONDS` budget, stops at `ASKHAVA_AI_DAILY_CAP`, writes one `ai.call_stat` row per call (model, instruction version, request ID, outcome, alert ID) and screens AI-written text with `catalog`'s CARD-010 list, a link ban and length caps, on a normalized copy (T024, D-810 to D-814).
- AI-008 gate: in live, `ai::api::read_intake` calls the AI only when a zero-retention signing date is recorded or the owner's "AI on scrubbed text" switch is on with the newest `ai.provider_terms` row complete (training-free, flagged-content retention, a Console spend limit at most 3 times the expected cost) and today's `ai.privacy_policy_version` holding the AI-018 sentence; otherwise it returns `AiOff::SwitchOff` without a call and intake runs keyword-only. Each reading is stored in `ai.reading` with no text column and its trade, job type and flags are queued for the off-database stream in the call record's transaction; `ai::api::weekly_counts` gives the weekly summary its counts (T025, D-841 to D-843).
- Text outbox: every text is queued through `messaging::api::enqueue` under an idempotency key derived (`sms.send`, `ID_SECRET`) from its template ID, recipient ID and subject IDs, unique in `messaging.outbox`; `send` claims the row in one statement, calls the `SmsAdapter` outside any transaction with the key re-derived from the stored IDs, then marks it sent and clears its body (D-850), so a killed worker's resend carries the same key and a sent row is never resent (TXT-011); a homeowner's number is read from the vault for the row's recipient at send time, a business phone is given by the caller, and a send must name the recipient kind the row was queued for (D-849); a past customer is texted only after she wrote since her request closed, from times the caller supplies, checked at enqueue and again at send (TXT-009). `messaging.sender_directory` keeps each business main or staff phone as `sender-dir` blind-index digests for `sender_add`, `sender_remove` and `sender_business` (T028, D-492, D-846, D-847, D-849, D-850).
- Text retries and email (T094, D-1378 to D-1381): a failed text is retried 10, 20 and 30 minutes after its first failure (`messaging::api::record_failure`); then `give_up` marks it `failed` and, in one transaction, lists an important text in `messaging.portal_notice` and queues it as an email when an address is on file, or records a call-back (`messaging.text_retry`, `ops.alert` `messaging.call_back_due`, due 24 hours urgent, 4 days otherwise) for a homeowner with none (TXT-014, TXT-015, INT-023). Every email goes once through `messaging.email_outbox` (`queue_email`, `send_email`, key `email.send` over template, recipient and subject IDs, no address stored), rendered in `app/messaging/templates/email_layout.html` with `lang` and the SEC-028 footer; `Bulk` emails wait for the midnight-UTC reset above the daily limit's share, a provider 429 waits and is never dropped (OPS-038). Verified Resend webhooks are stored once in `messaging.email_event` in event-time order, a complaint and 24 hours of silence raise alerts (SEC-050, SEC-051). The homeowner's update channel is `messaging.update_channel` (TXT-018). Not wired yet: the email-sending worker handler, the Resend webhook route and the `core.task` email request for modules above `messaging` (D-1380).
- Inbox and delivery statuses: `messaging::api::receive_inbound` and `receive_status` take only a `VerifiedWebhook` from Twilio and the instant from the caller's injected clock (stored as every row's `received_at`, no database default, D-863), parse MessageSid (`SM`/`MM` plus 32), MessageStatus and ErrorCode once, and record the event in `messaging.webhook_event` (source `twilio_inbound` keyed on the MessageSid, `twilio_status` on (MessageSid, MessageStatus), SEC-039) in the same transaction as its effect, so a repeat changes nothing (TXT-012): the first inbound delivery makes one `messaging.inbox` row, the first status callback appends a `messaging.delivery_event` (status folded to queued, sending, sent, delivered, undelivered, failed, with the error code). `messaging::api::delivery_status` reads a text's status from its outbox row and the events for its provider SID: never moved back by a late earlier status, so a callback that arrived before the send was marked still counts (TXT-013, T178, D-861).
- Twilio webhook routes (T241, D-1307 to D-1309): `server`'s `inbound_text::routes` (`POST /webhooks/twilio/sms` and `POST /webhooks/twilio/status`), merged by `build_router` only when `server::webhooks` built them (D-1310, D-1311) (`ASKHAVA_PUBLIC_BASE_URL` set; unset is no routes in dev and CI and a start-up error in test and live). Each delivery is checked by `adapters::api::verify_webhook` over the public base URL plus the route path and query (403 and one log line otherwise, SEC-016) and then runs in one transaction, answered 200 unless the database failed (500). An inbound text: `receive_inbound` (a repeat MessageSid stops), `privacy::api::read_inbound` (the raw body goes no further), `on_inbound_text` (an opt-out stops), `reply_to_help` (a HELP text, also from a listed number, stops after its reply; any other text from a listed number stops), then for a number not in the sender directory `intake::api::receive_reply`; a business phone's STOP also calls `businesses::api::phone_stopped`, and its other texts queue `business_auto_reply` when `businesses::api::auto_reply_due` holds (T256, D-2039). A racing first text from a new phone continues with the contact that won the unique phone index, or rolls back with a 500 (D-1751). A text a non-database module error stops after the repeat check (`Halt::Failed`) is still answered 200 and raises one High `messaging.inbound_text_failed` alert about its inbox row, the failed step in the alert's `ops` audit row, in the delivery's transaction, with no body, phone or error text (T325, D-2207, D-3107). A status callback: `receive_status`.
- Opt-outs and the one suppression list: `messaging::api::on_inbound_text` takes an inbound text's scrubbed words, its `OptOutType` and emergency flag; `rules::detect_opt_out` (sole word or listed phrase, CON-008, CON-010, CON-011) or the provider's STOP lists the number in `messaging.suppression` (digests under the `homeowner-phone` index key) and logs `messaging.optout_event` (channel, how detected, time; append-only) in one transaction; the app queues the CON-009 confirmation only for words the provider did not answer, on the first listing (the one whose insert added the suppression rows) and while texting is not paused; only the provider's `OptOutType=START` (START or UNSTOP) lifts it; anything else from a listed number sends nothing, and an emergency flag on any message from it raises a critical `ops` alert. `messaging::api::suppress` is the entry for every other revocation channel. `messaging::api::send_checked` is the send every text takes: it refuses a listed number except the HELP reply and the current listing's confirmation within 5 minutes, and a 21610 refusal lists the number and marks the outbox row `blocked`, never claimed again (T029, CON-013 to CON-017, CON-030, CON-035, D-866 to D-868, D-870).
- Message log and body cut (T093, D-1851 to D-1854; TXT-019, TXT-020, CON-036): `messaging.outbox` is the outbound message log, holding the recipient's vault reference (never the number), template ID, recipient kind, the body's `language` (set at enqueue by matching the body against the template, `retention::language_of`; NULL when it fits neither or both), the request among `subject_ids`, `sent_at`, `attempts` and Twilio's status via `provider_sid`; `messaging::api::message_log` reads one entry as `LogEntry`. A sent row keeps no body (SEC-037); the daily `messaging.cut_bodies` task (`run_cut`, scheduled by the worker at start and by each run) cuts the body of any row still unsent 30 days after it was queued and ends it `failed`. No log row is deleted. `messaging.inbox` stores no words. A texted opt-out's `messaging.optout_event` row keeps its detection and the `inbox_id` of the message that carried it (migration 0128). The day-31 delete of Twilio's own copy is `messaging`'s `purge.rs` (T276, D-2160): the daily `messaging.body_delete_scan` queues one `messaging.body_delete` task per inbound or outbound SID 31 or more days old, deduped by SID, which redacts the body through `SmsBodyDelete` (a 20404 counts as done, any other error retries). Not here: the 5-year end of the log (CON-022).
- HELP, INFO and AYUDA (T030, D-887 to D-890): `messaging::api::reply_to_help` queues the CON-018 reply for a text that is only one of those words, from any number including an opted-out one, under template ID `help_reply`; `HelpSettings` carries the reply times and the owner-away date; no voice number is ever given.
- First reply and consent text (T038, D-907 to D-909): `intake::api::on_text` takes an inbound text's IDs, flags and scrubbed words after `messaging` has handled opt-outs and HELP; a contact with no open `intake.conversation` gets one opened, in one transaction with its audit row, with the 911 text (on a 911-list match) and then the CON-031 consent text queued in that order (`Handled::texts`, the order the caller sends them), English unless the message reads as Spanish and Spanish conversations are on (CON-026); one open conversation per homeowner (partial unique index) and the outbox key make the consent text go once per request; every later reply is a conversation step (`rules::Then`), never a wait for a reply (CON-027, D-419). Migration `0053_intake_tables.sql` creates the seven `intake` tables of section 3 (D-493).
- Consent and terms records (T181, D-933, D-934): `intake::api::receive_text` is the entry point for every inbound homeowner text; it runs the first reply inside its own transaction and, when that text queued the consent text, writes the `intake.consent_record` row (channel `sms`, inbound message ID, time, consent-text version `<template>:<language>:<hash>`) before one commit (CON-005). `card_allowed` (CON-007) and `may_text` (CON-021) are the gates the card and send tasks call first. `record_terms_acceptance` stores one `intake.terms_acceptance` row per pick (document, version, SHA256 of the published text, language, channel, assent line, time, request, pick, and the inbound message ID for a text pick; migration `0054_intake_terms_acceptance.sql`, TERMS-016, TERMS-033); no reply ever writes either record.
- The one 911 text (T152, D-973): `intake::api::safety_text` renders the INT-004 `emergency_911` template exactly (the Spanish one stays on while Spanish conversations are off), `safety_outgoing` gives the send path its quiet-hours view (`Kind::Safety911`, which QH-004 never holds) and `sent_in_time` checks the 5-minute limit (`SAFETY_WITHIN`). No safety-contacts file exists or is loaded (D-436): `tests/docs/test_no_phone_numbers_in_templates.py` fails the build on any phone number other than 911, any other N11 code such as 211, a utility name or "cooling center" in a text template or in any file under an `app/*/templates` folder.
- The 911-list screen and the emergency flow (T039, D-978 to D-982): `intake::api::receive_text` runs `intake`'s stem matcher (`emergency::check`, after street-address patterns are removed; `receive_answer` with `Asked::NameAndAddress` skips the address answer) first on every message and alone decides the INT-004 text (INT-026); a match is an `intake.emergency_event` row and holds the cards until SAFE (`safe_hold`, INT-005); a smoke or CO alarm said only to beep gets the INT-037 question. `answer_how_soon` stores the INT-030 answer and, on answer 1 or after a 911 match answered SAFE, offers the INT-006 choice (never while a 911 text waits for SAFE: `Answered::choice_after_safe`, then `offer_emergency_choice` after SAFE; answer 4 always asks the day; one INT-004 text per hold, D-982), at night after the INT-033 count from `businesses::api::night_count` (`emergency_count`); `answer_choice` stores it, and `release_emergency` enqueues one `intake.emergency_released` `core.task` for `quotes` once consent, SAFE, the emergency choice and the ZIP and area check are done, before the trade questions (INT-029).
- What opens a job and the under-18 reply (T040, D-993 to D-995): `intake::api::begin_job` is the one job-opening path (also the ALSO reply's second job, INT-032): it refuses a message of one or two words without an emergency keyword and any cancel-like message (INT-007, INT-008), keeps one active job per homeowner, property and trade (INT-009) and otherwise calls `jobs::api::create`; the job links are `intake.intake_answer` rows (no new table). `joining_job` gives the job a follow-up within the INT-010 window joins (the after-window rule is T042's); `record_answer` stores an answer only for an open request and on an exact word match (INT-011, INT-012); `stop_if_under_18` sends the one INT-038 reply (`under_18`, Spanish stays on) and closes the request, deleting its data under 13; `ask_name_address` asks the INT-027 question after the area check and `take_name_address` stores the answer in the vault only.
- The keyword second opinion and the trade question (T042, D-998, D-999): `intake::matching` (pure) holds the keyword rules (`keyword_reading`: category, catalog job type, whole-word phrases, license-boundary flag; AI-011), `second_opinion` (both readings agree and the keywords name that trade or none, or a `TradeQuestion` offering every distinct trade, readings first, up to three, D-104), `TradeQuestion::read` (a lone number, "Not sure", or the whole words of exactly one option: its trade's keywords or its label's words, INT-012, D-103) and `step` (one re-ask with the same options, then the fallback: the keyword trade if exactly one matched, else the first reading, INT-015), `candidates` (the chosen trade's reading and keyword job types, `<trade>_other` with "Which fits best?" only when none, plus every license-boundary keyword job type, D-102, D-105, CARD-001), `two_trades` and `starts_second` (INT-032). T183 (D-1003): candidates across trades are INT-014's "different licenses" (no class in the license map covers two of the keyword trades), "water heater", "pool heater" and "spa heater" keep the catalog's licensed-only "water heater replacement" and "gas pool heater" as license-boundary candidates beside any other job type of their trade, "Which fits best?" always offers every licensed candidate, and `Candidates::fit_step` reads its reply (a number and words naming different options are unusable; a choice leaves that job type alone under its trade; "Not sure" or a second unusable reply keeps all). `rules::follow_up_joins` adds INT-010's after-window rule (`shares_words`, `JOIN_SHARE_PERCENT`). Wiring these into the conversation and the `two_jobs` text belong to the copy- and conversation-owning task (D-999).
- Web security (T051, D-943, D-944, D-945, D-946): `server::build_router`'s outermost layer is `portals::api::security_headers`, so every response, a 404 included, carries the SEC-012 CSP (own origin only, no inline or eval script, `object-src 'none'`, `frame-ancestors 'none'`), one-year HSTS with `includeSubDomains` and no `preload` (SEC-054), the Permissions-Policy, `nosniff` and `Referrer-Policy: no-referrer`; `no_store` goes on contact and photo pages (SEC-034). Portal and site routers (never webhooks) layer `refuse_cross_site`, which refuses a state-changing request whose `Sec-Fetch-Site` is not `same-origin`, whose `Origin` is not `https://` on the `Host`, or that has neither; POST handlers read their form through `CsrfForm`, whose `csrf` field must equal `csrf_token(&jar)`: the SHA256 of a label and the `__Host-askhava-session` cookie's token, or with no session the `__Host-askhava-pre` cookie's, so no key or table is needed. `start_session` deletes the browser's earlier `portals.session` row and drops the pre-session cookie (SEC-042). Each refusal is a bare 403 and a `record_security_event`: one log line and one `portals.security_event` row (kind, route template, request ID, and the session's row ID as actor reference when the browser is signed in); the event that brings its kind to 50 within an hour raises one High `ops` alert unless one was raised (flagged `raised_alert`) within that hour, so a steady attack raises at most one per kind per hour (SEC-048). Portal templates extend `app/site/templates/base.html` through `app/portals/askama.toml` (D-491).
- Request time limit and panics (T286, D-1870, D-2208, D-2356, D-2357): `build_router`'s layers, innermost first, are the routes, `request_timeout::catch_panic()` (a panicking handler is a 500 with `Cache-Control: no-store` and an empty body, the panic text never read), `request_timeout::limit_request` (the whole request, body reading included, under `ASKHAVA_REQUEST_TIMEOUT_SECS`, default 60, 10 to 300, from `core`'s typed settings; past it a 503 with `Retry-After: 5`, `no-store` and an empty body, the handler future dropped), the request-log middleware, `TraceLayer`, `PropagateRequestIdLayer`, `SetRequestIdLayer` and outermost `security_headers`, so both answers carry the SEC-012 headers and the request ID and are logged with their status. `main` passes `(hooks, RequestTimeout::from_settings(&settings))`; bare `Hooks` take the 60-second default. `ASKHAVA_PUBLIC_BASE_URL` with a path after its host stops start-up, since routes are served at the root and Twilio signs the base plus the route path (SEC-016).
- Text templates: `app/messaging/templates/en.json` and `es.json` are the registry of every fixed text (TXT-001, TXT-004), one key per template ID with both languages and a recorded `segments` count; each task adds only its own keys (D-759, D-491).

## 3. Modules, what each owns, and its tables

Each module owns its files and its tables, and no module reaches into another's tables (`BUILD_BRIEF.md:126`). Each module's tables live in a Postgres schema named after it (D-091). "Calls" lists the lower crates whose `api` it may use, which are exactly the path dependencies in its `Cargo.toml`; `core` is always allowed and is not repeated.

| Module | Owns (`BUILD_BRIEF.md:128`–`BUILD_BRIEF.md:146`) | Tables (schema-qualified) | Calls |
|---|---|---|---|
| `ops` | Alerts, pause switches, safe mode, status banner, web-and-email mode, audit log (decisions and records only: every page that shows `ops` figures is a `portals` page, every email or call `ops` wants sent is a `messaging` handler on a `core.task` event, D-491); which alerts push to Casey's phone, the 9 a.m. and 3 p.m. daytime batch push (OPS-032) and the weekly digest of everything else; safe mode only from pushed alerts; the safety-voice-call setting, off until a TCPA attorney's written approval is recorded (CALL-019); web-and-email mode able to run the whole service (TXT-034); the business-texting switch and the filed written answer it needs (TXT-035); alerts to Casey by email and, for critical safety alerts, a call (OPS-035); help requests: HELP auto-answers (CON-034), one HELP notification per request to the alert address with hourly and daily limits, the owner help page and its screened reply box, and the business "Get help" form (OPS-036, BIZ-047, D-348); the sending-limit alert (OPS-004, D-351). `ops` is layer 1, so it never sends or reads anything itself: it records alerts and help requests as rows holding IDs only and enqueues `core.task` events, and the handlers of `messaging` (alert emails, HELP notifications, Casey's critical call through the voice adapter) and `portals` (the owner help page, the scorecard page reading `payments::api` and `intake::api`) do the sending and reading; a homeowner's help-message text lives in `privacy.message_body` and `ops.help_message` holds its reference (PRIV-001, D-482) (D-218, D-219, D-275, D-277, D-278, D-297, D-303, D-314) | `ops.audit_log` (append-only), `ops.alert`, `ops.alert_ack`, `ops.pause_switch`, `ops.switch_history`, `ops.safe_mode`, `ops.status_banner` (built by T279, D-1843), `ops.help_request`, `ops.help_message` (IDs and a `privacy.message_body` reference, never text), `ops.not_a_fit_answer`, `ops.minutes_entry` (T158, D-493) | — |
| `adapters` | One interface per vendor; real, sandbox and stand-in implementations chosen by configuration | none | — |
| `catalog` | Categories, job types, license rules, fee rules, feature switches (fee mode, category on/off) and numeric settings | `catalog.category`, `catalog.job_type`, `catalog.license_rule`, `catalog.fee_rule`, `catalog.setting`, `catalog.setting_history`; markets as reviewed data and job locations by ZIP centroid (INT-048, T248, D-1518): `catalog.market`, `catalog.market_town`, `catalog.zcta_centroid` | `ops` |
| `privacy` | The plain-code scrubber; the contact vault (field-level encrypted); the inbound reader that is the only code to see raw homeowner text (section 8); retention and purge jobs; photos | `privacy.contact`, `privacy.contact_index` (blind index), `privacy.message_body` (homeowner message text only), `privacy.photo` (each photo's job, key version and storage key only; the encrypted photo bytes are in object storage behind the `storage` adapter, never in PostgreSQL, PRIV-023), `privacy.retention_hold`, `privacy.purge_run` | `adapters`, `ops` |
| `ai` | Request reading through the AI adapter, and quote summarizing only while the owner setting "AI quote summaries" is on (off by default and at launch, PICK-003, D-373); strict schemas; accepts only `ScrubbedText`, homeowner and business quote text alike (AI-002) | `ai.reading` (fields only, no personal details), `ai.call_stat` | `adapters`, `privacy` (the `ScrubbedText` type only), `catalog` (the open trades for AI-005 and the CARD-010 restricted-word list, D-492), `ops` |
| `messaging` | Outbox and inbox (never send twice); templates in English and Spanish; quiet hours; opt-out keywords and the one suppression list fed by every channel (text, help email, email replies, help form, portal switch, owner-recorded requests) and checked before every text and automated call (CON-035, D-372); the sender directory (which numbers are business phones); delivery tracking; email and letters; the business-notice fan-out by each business's chosen channels (email by default; text or both only while business texting is on), every channel of one notice sent or held together, and email bounce and complaint handling (BIZ-019, BIZ-040, QH-005, D-307, D-308); one fixed sender name and address for business notices (SEC-025); the open-cards email at up to two times a day, with a waiting pick first (CARD-022, D-380; its lines come from `quotes::api` and `picks::api` through the `core.task` event that `quotes` enqueues, since `messaging` is below them, D-482), and the monthly missed-card account email (CARD-022, BIZ-044, D-330, D-331); the email footer with the LLC postal address and "Manage email notices" (SEC-028); the email-limit deferral of non-urgent emails above 90% of the daily limit, nothing dropped (OPS-038, D-381); no homeowner details in any business text (TXT-036). Business notices arrive with their recipients resolved: the enqueuing module (`quotes`, `picks`, `businesses`) looks them up through `businesses::api::notice_addresses` and puts opaque address IDs in the event payload, and `messaging` sends to what it is given, never calling `businesses` (D-491) | `messaging.outbox`, `messaging.inbox` (scrubbed homeowner text and flags; business text as sent), `messaging.sender_directory` (blind-indexed business and staff phones), `messaging.delivery_event`, `messaging.opt_out`, `messaging.opt_out_event`, `messaging.email_outbox`, `messaging.email_event`, `messaging.letter`, `messaging.mode_state` (texting switch, number suspension and launch channel, append-only, T279, D-1843), `messaging.webhook_event` | `adapters`, `privacy`, `catalog`, `ops` |
| `payments` | Stripe customers, optional saved cards, card authorization, on-session Confirm-and-pay charges started only by the business's press on the Confirm page (FEE-014, FEE-036, D-365), the one off-session exception being the optional auto-confirm charge on a quote the business ticked, offered only while Stripe's written answer is filed (PICK-048, D-401), the money view's set-aside and manual expenses outside the ledger (FEE-046, D-410), refunds, disputes, the per-charge evidence record (FEE-042), free first wins, the ledger, nightly reconcile | `payments.customer`, `payments.payment_method`, `payments.card_authorization`, `payments.charge_attempt`, `payments.charge_evidence` (append-only), `payments.charge_three_ds` (each charged pick's 3-D Secure result as a boolean only, unknown read as false, T405, D-3118), `payments.ledger_entry` (append-only), `payments.refund`, `payments.dispute`, `payments.free_win`, `payments.webhook_event`, `payments.reconcile_run`, `payments.expense` (Casey's manual expenses list, FEE-046, never in the ledger; D-491), `payments.fee_terms` (the one row of fee terms in force, written by `switch_fee_mode`, T287, D-1871), `payments.pick_fee` (the fee fixed at each pick, written once by `picks` through `payments::api` in the pick's transaction, FEE-005, T287, D-1871), `payments.reconcile_open` (charges the nightly reconcile could not settle yet, retried until settled, T288, D-1872) | `adapters`, `privacy`, `catalog`, `ops` |
| `licensing` | Agency daily lists; live lookup at pick; other agencies; the city license list; handyman rules | `licensing.list_import`, `licensing.agency_record`, `licensing.city_license_entry`, `licensing.business_license`, `licensing.license_check` | `adapters`, `catalog`, `ops` |
| `businesses` | Sign-up, identity chain, job-type checklist, towns, tags, business consent records, pause rules (including PAUSE and BUSY, D-198), staff phones, team members and their roles (D-191), complaints and business replies (D-200), the channel preference and verified notice emails (BIZ-036, BIZ-040, SEC-024, D-307, D-312); the sign-up deliverability check with a sample card, backup notice addresses, the "Emergency emails at any hour" switch with its test emergency email, and the "reply OK, star, add to contacts" step; no automated-call consent is stored since no business is ever called (BIZ-037, BIZ-039, BIZ-042, BIZ-043, CON-019, D-328, D-329, D-344, D-346, D-352) | `businesses.business`, `businesses.town`, `businesses.service_area` (base ZIP and radius, BIZ-058, T248, D-1518), `businesses.job_type_choice`, `businesses.tag`, `businesses.staff_phone`, `businesses.team_member`, `businesses.consent_record`, `businesses.identity_attempt`, `businesses.signup_review`, `businesses.pause_event`, `businesses.complaint`, `businesses.channel_preference`, `businesses.email_verification` | `licensing`, `payments`, `messaging`, `catalog`, `adapters`, `ops` |
| `jobs` | The job lifecycle state machine (section 6); job numbers; status history; quote-page token hashes; private feedback and its share tick and removal reasons (JOB-017, T230, D-493) | `jobs.job`, `jobs.status_history`, `jobs.job_number`, `jobs.feedback`, `jobs.job_facts` (append-only narrowing rows of each job's category and candidate job type codes, T339, D-2330, D-3112) | `messaging`, `catalog`, `ops` |
| `calls` | Masked numbers from a pool, PIN routing, call sessions, call logs (never recorded), built on standard programmable voice, never Twilio Proxy (CALL-021, D-296) | `calls.number_pool`, `calls.call_session`, `calls.call_log`, `calls.call_start` (each call's start by parent CallSid, T262, D-1836), `calls.webhook_event` | `businesses`, `privacy`, `messaging`, `adapters`, `ops` |
| `quotes` | Who receives each job card, and the cards themselves; quote intake and parsing, including the required quote facts, pre-filled per job type from the business's last answers (D-233, D-283); eligibility rules; arrival order; original wording beside any summary | `quotes.job_card`, `quotes.quote`, `quotes.quote_bounce` | `jobs`, `businesses`, `licensing`, `catalog`, `ai`, `privacy`, `messaging`, `ops` |
| `picks` | Pick by code; contact-method choice; confirm window of 4 business hours by email alone, counted from when the pick email is sent, with a reminder every business hour, each carrying the Hold link, the last one marked "Last reminder" (PICK-019, PICK-037, D-345, D-380); the homeowner's end-time and lapse re-pick texts (PICK-045, PICK-046); EMERGENCY pick emails, at night only to people with "Emergency emails at any hour" on (PICK-037, D-346; the EMERGENCY card email itself is `quotes`' card content, CARD-021, sent through `messaging`'s fan-out, D-482); no automated call to any business phone (BIZ-039, D-344); payment hand-off; release of contact details (portal and email only, PICK-024); losers notified | `picks.pick`, `picks.code_attempt`, `picks.release`, `picks.confirm_reminder` | `quotes`, `jobs`, `payments`, `calls`, `businesses`, `licensing`, `privacy`, `messaging`, `adapters`, `ops` |
| `intake` | Inbound SMS and web chat routing (from flags and `ScrubbedText`, never raw homeowner text); conversation state; homeowner consent; the one 911 text and its keyword list for gas, a CO alarm going off, smoke or fire, sparking or burning electrical and a downed power line (INT-002, INT-004, D-436); the one intake order (INT-043); photos, inspiration pictures and concept images (INT-044 to INT-046), photo checks running on Ask Hava's own server, never an outside AI, until zero retention (D-432); category matching; homeowners and properties | `intake.homeowner`, `intake.property`, `intake.conversation`, `intake.consent_record`, `intake.intake_answer`, `intake.emergency_event`, `intake.recruiting_demand`, `intake.golden_result` (OPS-040, T156, D-493); `intake.conversation.source` records how the request started (INT-047, T103, D-493) | `picks`, `quotes`, `jobs`, `businesses`, `ai`, `privacy`, `catalog`, `messaging`, `ops` |
| `recovery` | Backup checks, the restore drill, replay with derived IDs | `recovery.backup_check`, `recovery.restore_drill`, `recovery.replay_run`, `recovery.replay_item` | the `api` of every module in layers 0–7 |
| `portals` | Homeowner page and portal, business portal, owner portal; their sign-in, sessions and second factors; the business action pages and signed single-purpose links in business emails that replace text keywords (BIZ-038, SEC-025, D-309), which act only from the page's button, never on opening (SEC-026), with Quote links that need no session, Confirm by link plus a Verify code, and the shared-inbox switch (SEC-004, SEC-027, D-333); the per-job notice log (BIZ-045, D-332); no service worker until the later app phase (BIZ-041) | `portals.session`, `portals.login_link`, `portals.code_try` (numbered code-check reservations, T272, D-1837), `portals.known_device`, `portals.owner_credential`, `portals.passkey`, `portals.totp_secret` (encrypted), `portals.recovery_code` (hashed), `portals.action_link` (hashed) | the `api` of every module in layers 0–7 |
| `site` | Public website in the plaster and brass look of `docs/design/DESIGN_SPEC.md` (D-451); the web-chat page | none (web-chat text goes to `intake`) | `intake`, `catalog`, `ops`, `payments`, `businesses` (D-491) |

`core` owns `core.task` (the task queue and timers) and `core.rate_limit` (D-090).

Twelve v9.3 record types map onto these tables (`sources/01_spec/Havasu_Home_Quotes_Technical_Blueprint_v9.3.md:79`–`sources/01_spec/Havasu_Home_Quotes_Technical_Blueprint_v9.3.md:96`): Contact → `privacy.contact`; Homeowner, Property → `intake`; Request → `jobs.job`; Photo → `privacy.photo`; Contractor → `businesses.business` plus `licensing.business_license` and `payments.customer`; Job card, Quote → `quotes`; Pick → `picks.pick`; Ledger entry → `payments.ledger_entry`; Message → `messaging.outbox`/`messaging.inbox`, with homeowner message text in `privacy.message_body`; Alert/audit → `ops`.

## 4. Dependency rules

1. **Layers.** A module may use only the `api` of a module in a lower layer, never its `models` or `service`; the pure decision functions a lower crate re-exports as `api::rules` are part of its `api` and may be called (D-091, D-481, D-484). In Rust this is a compile error, not a convention: a crate lists only its allowed lower crates in `Cargo.toml`, Cargo refuses a dependency cycle, and only `api` is `pub` (D-453):

   | Layer | Modules |
   |---|---|
   | 0 | `core` |
   | 1 | `ops`, `adapters` |
   | 2 | `catalog`, `privacy` |
   | 3 | `ai`, `messaging`, `payments`, `licensing` |
   | 4 | `businesses`, `jobs` |
   | 5 | `calls`, `quotes` |
   | 6 | `picks` |
   | 7 | `intake` |
   | 8 | `recovery`, `portals`, `site` |

   `adapters` uses only `core` and the HTTP and crypto crates. Same-layer imports are allowed only where section 3's "Calls" column lists them (none today). The layer table is kept in machine-readable form in `kit/layers.json` (protected), which the architecture test reads (D-479). There is no `testing` feature: unit and property tests live inside each crate, and the test-layer crates see only `api` (D-481).
2. **Events go up, calls go down.** When a lower module must tell a higher one something (Stripe says a charge succeeded; an inbound text arrives; a license lapses; an alert is raised), it enqueues a `core.task` with an event kind and IDs only, never personal details. `app/server/src/lib.rs` (`register_handlers()`) registers which module's handler runs for each kind (D-481). No module imports a higher one.
3. **Tables.** Every query and migration names its own module's schema. The architecture test (T175, SEC-029) reads every crate's `Cargo.toml` and every migration and fails on a dependency not allowed by the layer table, any cross-schema foreign key, and any query that names another module's schema (the exceptions: `core` may name `public`, to lock it down and hold `_sqlx_migrations`; and a `core` migration may name other modules' schemas only when every statement in it is `ALTER DEFAULT PRIVILEGES IN SCHEMA <schema>, … REVOKE <privileges> ON TABLES FROM askhava_app`, which can only take a default away, T250, D-1632), on a migration whose first line (or second, after `-- no-transaction`) does not name its module and task, whose number does not rise, whose owner is missing from `kit/tasks.json`, or whose number order disagrees with its tasks' dependency order; and on any SQL outside the query macros (the function forms, `AssertSqlSafe`, `QueryBuilder`, `Executor`, `query_unchecked!`), found by a lexer scan with comments and strings removed (D-491); and on dynamic SQL, which a scan of names cannot see through (in a migration a `DO` statement, an `EXECUTE` that runs a string or a `format` or `quote_*` call; in a query macro's SQL `DO` or `EXECUTE`), outside a short allow list in the test that gives each file's reason (T252, D-1677); SQLx checks each query against the schema when it compiles.
4. **Money** moves only through `payments` (FEE-032). **Vault plaintext** comes only from `privacy::api` and only to the readers in section 8. **Release needs proof:** the function that reads contact fields for a release takes a `ReleaseProof`. Because `privacy` (layer 2) cannot name a `payments` (layer 3) type, the design is a capability (D-481): `privacy::api` defines `ReleaseProof` and a non-`Clone` `ReleaseMint`, both with private fields; `privacy::api::release_mint()` can be called once per process and panics on a second call; `server/src/lib.rs` calls it once in `register_handlers()` and hands the mint to `payments` (which therefore calls `privacy`, D-488), and `payments` alone turns a signature-checked Stripe event for a succeeded charge, or a fee decision of no fee due, into a proof. What this proves and what it does not (the skeleton trial, D-488): the compile-fail cases in a crate with no feature on prove that no other crate can construct a proof or a mint (struct literal, `Default`, `Clone`); they cannot stop a crate that depends on `privacy` from calling `release_mint()` itself, so the once-guard and the architecture test's source scan for `ReleaseMint`, `release_mint`, `SessionMint` and `session_mint` outside `privacy`, `payments`, `server` (and `portals` for the session mint) are what stop a second minter (SEC-030). Because the mint is taken once per process, tests that build the composition share one fixture per test binary. The types are T022's; the minter and the server hookup are T189's. The homeowner and owner realm reads take the capability types of SEC-046 the same way.
5. **Statuses are enums.** Job, pick, quote, charge and consent statuses are Rust enums; every `match` on them names every status: the modules that act on statuses deny clippy `wildcard_enum_match_arm` (not the whole workspace, since `Option` and foreign non-exhaustive enums need a wildcard arm), and a source scan fails on `_ =>`, `matches!` or `if let` on a status enum outside its own helpers, so a new status does not build until every place handles it (SEC-031).
6. **Invariants** (`BUILD_BRIEF.md:148`–`BUILD_BRIEF.md:152`): every outside action carries an idempotency key derived from what it identifies (SEC-018); every state change writes an audit row through `ops.api.audit()` in the same transaction (JOB-005); no homeowner contact detail leaves the vault except on a confirmed pick that is paid or has a recorded $0 fee decision (PRIV-005, D-477); the ledger always reconciles to Stripe (FEE-029, FEE-030).

## 5. Shared kernel (`core`)

`core` holds what every module needs and owns no business data (D-090):

- **Settings:** one settings struct built by `Settings::from_pairs(impl IntoIterator<Item = (String, String)>)` (a hand-written parser over the `ASKHAVA_` prefix: unknown `ASKHAVA_*` keys are refused, other variables ignored, and no error message ever echoes a value; `from_iter` would trip clippy's `should_implement_trait`, D-491), which the binaries feed from `std::env::vars()` and tests from a map (edition 2024 makes `set_var` unsafe, and `unsafe` is forbidden, D-481); it refuses to start on a missing value, a live key in a non-live environment, a test key in live, or a vendor account identifier from the other environment's allow-list (SEC-002, SEC-036; `BUILD_BRIEF.md:521`).
- **Database:** the SQLx connection pool and a transaction helper; the `Guarded<T>` outcome type (`Applied` or `MovedOn`) for "only if still in the expected status" updates, each module writing its own guarded `UPDATE … WHERE id = $1 AND status = $2 RETURNING …` as a literal macro, since one shared statement cannot name another module's table (JOB-004, D-491; `sources/01_spec/Havasu_Home_Quotes_Technical_Blueprint_v9.3.md:689`). Two database roles: a migration role that owns the schemas, and an app role with no `UPDATE` or `DELETE` on append-only tables (D-092). Roles are created outside migrations, by the host or a bootstrap script with passwords from host variables; migrations hold only idempotent `GRANT`s and `ALTER DEFAULT PRIVILEGES` per schema; `server migrate` (`sqlx::migrate!`) is the release step; the append-only tests run `SET ROLE askhava_app` so the rule is exercised, not assumed (D-481). The pool's `max_connections` per process is set so that web replicas times pool plus the worker stays under the host's connection limit.
- **Clock:** UTC storage; Arizona time (no daylight saving) and the business clock of 8:00 a.m.–8:00 p.m. every day (JOB-007, D-004); the local time in a recipient's zone, which `messaging` uses for quiet hours (D-003, D-025); tests inject the clock.
- **Webhook dedupe:** one helper that records a vendor event ID in the calling module's own `webhook_event` table and reports whether it was new, so every module dedupes the same way (SEC-017).
- **Derived IDs:** HMAC-SHA-256 under a server secret over what an ID identifies, for job cards, quotes, picks, quote-page links and Stripe idempotency keys (CARD-017, PICK-028, OPS-016).
- **Task queue:** `core.task` (kind, IDs-only payload, `run_at`, attempts, lease, unique dedupe key). Workers claim with `FOR UPDATE SKIP LOCKED` (`attempts < max_attempts`, lease expired or null, ordered by `run_at`, the lease end computed in Rust from an injected clock), finish or fail with the lease owner in the `WHERE` (fencing: a stale owner's finish returns false), hold a lease that is renewed at half-life, and a crashed worker's lease expires so another picks the task up; `register_handlers()` returns the registry, which tests hold in a `OnceLock` (D-488); the trial of D-491 measured 100 tasks of 20 ms across two workers in 1.0 s and about 350 to 400 instant tasks per second, each exactly once. Every handler first re-checks the current status and does nothing if things moved on (JOB-006). The worker is one Tokio runtime running a claim loop of fixed concurrency (a `JoinSet` behind a semaphore), a poll interval with backoff, lease renewal for long handlers, a per-handler timeout, and nightly jobs inserted under date-derived dedupe keys so several replicas never double-run; on SIGTERM it stops claiming, drains within the host's grace period and releases its leases; `web` uses `axum::serve(...).with_graceful_shutdown` (D-481). Timestamps are `timestamptz` read and written through `jiff-sqlx` (D-481).
- **Rate limits:** `core.rate_limit` counters per phone, IP and business (SEC-014, SEC-015).
- **Brand:** the one brand file `app/core/data/brand.json`, compiled in: display, business, short, legal and spoken legal names, domain, email sender, card-statement prefix, the words never used alone and the Spanish forms; `brand()` serves it (installed and checked at start-up, a fake one installable in a test) and `Brand::fill` fills the `{brand}`, `{brand_business}`, `{brand_short}`, `{brand_legal}` and `{domain}` tokens of text templates; the build's Python checks read it through `scripts/brand.py`. No customer-visible line names the brand outside it, except the versioned legal records (T291 to T295, D-1932, D-1933).
- **Logging:** `tracing` with a request ID on every line; contact values are wrapped in a `Redacted` type whose `Debug` and `Display` print only a marker, so they cannot reach a log by accident; no phone numbers, names or tokens in logs (PRIV-016); Sentry with default PII off plus a scrubber (PRIV-017). Secrets are `secrecy::SecretString`, zeroed on drop.

## 6. Job lifecycle state machine

Statuses are JOB-001's twelve. A move not listed here is refused and logged as a bug (JOB-002). Every move takes the row lock, checks the expected status, writes `jobs.status_history` and an audit row, and enqueues the next timer (JOB-004 to JOB-006).

```
Intake ──> Open ──> Quotes ready ──> Awaiting confirm ──> Payment pending ──> Connected ──> Done
  │          │  \        ▲  ▲  │            │                    │                │
  │          │   └> No quotes yet           └────────────────────┴────────────────┘
  │          │          │     │               back to Quotes ready (no confirm, card fails,
  │          │          └─────┘ (a quote)     ineligible at Confirm, no call in 72 h, fraud warning before release)
  │          └─> Not covered
  ├─> Not covered (no open category or no active business, INT-017, D-482)
  └─> Closed early
Quotes ready, No quotes yet, Connected ──> Closed     Any non-final status ──> Cancelled
```

(`sources/01_spec/Havasu_Home_Quotes_Technical_Blueprint_v9.3.md:155`–`sources/01_spec/Havasu_Home_Quotes_Technical_Blueprint_v9.3.md:177`, as changed by D-011, D-030, D-043, D-045, D-046.)

**Every waiting status has a timeout** (JOB-003; `sources/01_spec/Changes_Since_v9.3_AUTHORITATIVE.md:111`). "Business hours" count only 8 a.m.–8 p.m. Arizona time (D-004).

| Status | Waiting for | Timeout | Then | Requirement |
|---|---|---|---|---|
| Intake | Answers, the "I'm safe" reply after an emergency (consent is recorded with the first text, so nothing waits for it, D-419) | Reminder at 24 h, close at 72 h; a returning web-chat visitor sees the request closed | Closed early, reason "no reply", with the INT-019 closing text (D-095, D-419) | INT-019, INT-005, CON-025, CON-027 |
| Intake → Not covered | Nothing: the move happens at once when no open category or no active business in the town covers the request (INT-017, INT-022, D-482) | — | Not covered, with the INT-017 "We don't cover" text and the OPS-041 owner email | INT-016, INT-017, OPS-041 |
| Open | The first quote | 6 p.m. Arizona time the next day | No quotes yet, with the "no quotes yet" text | JOB-009, JOB-010 |
| No quotes yet | A quote | 5 days | Closed | JOB-011 |
| Quotes ready | A pick | 3 days, restarted on every entry; at once if no choosable quote is left and every job card's 24-hour quote window has ended (D-140) | Closed | JOB-012, JOB-025 |
| Awaiting confirm | The business's Confirm (the portal review step, one tap from the pick email; CONFIRM by text only for a business that chose text, PICK-033) | 4 business hours from when the pick notice (email and any chosen text together) is sent (D-148, D-310, D-345), or from the end of the business's BIZ-034 away time, plus 2 business hours once on a HOLD (PICK-043), plus 1 business hour once when all its emails bounced or failed (D-332, D-345); 60 clock minutes on an emergency job (PICK-044, D-482); a second "<n> YES" replaces the pick (PICK-032) | Quotes ready | JOB-013, PICK-019, PICK-043 |
| Payment pending | A working card and Stripe's answer | 1 business hour after a decline or "needs card" (FEE-020, FEE-036), 60 clock minutes on an emergency job (JOB-014, PICK-044, D-482); Stripe asked directly at 30 min (FEE-019); pick released if Stripe is unreachable for 4 h (FEE-018); these timers stop while charges are paused (FEE-038); a Stripe early fraud warning on a charge whose details were not yet released refunds it at once, the pick ending as fraud-refunded (FEE-052, D-491) | Quotes ready | JOB-014, FEE-052 |
| Connected | First call; DONE, NO or PROBLEM at day 3 or day 14 | Call contact: no call in 72 h → Quotes ready; email contact: a day-3 NO → Quotes ready (D-143); unreachable refund decided at 96 h → Closed (FEE-022, JOB-027); no reply 7 days after the day-14 check-in → Closed | Quotes ready, Done or Closed | JOB-015, JOB-026, JOB-027 |
| Done, Closed, Closed early, Not covered, Cancelled | Nothing (final) | — | — | JOB-001; `sources/01_spec/Havasu_Home_Quotes_Technical_Blueprint_v9.3.md:160` |

Timers that are not job statuses but still bound every wait: each job card's 24-hour quote window (PICK-001); quote-count updates at most every 3 hours (TXT-017); pick code 10 minutes, 5 tries, 15-minute cooldown (PICK-013); sign-in links 15 minutes (SEC-004); masked line closes at 30 days or earlier (CALL-009); text retries 3 times over 30 minutes then email (TXT-014); quote-page tokens die 30 days after close (PICK-011); a failed refund retries hourly (FEE-026); the "we have your message" AI retry every 30 minutes (INT-021); safe mode 24 hours after an unacknowledged High alert, pausing charges and releases only (OPS-001, D-133), with self-cleared delivery alerts acknowledged automatically (OPS-023); a job paused by a sole-word CANCEL keeps its status and timers (CON-029).

**Pick status** (owned by `picks`, kept beside the job status from the pick onward, including while the job is Connected, where paid, free and no-show still apply): awaiting confirm, needs card, charging, auth needed, payment failed, paid, free, expired, cancelled, no-show, fraud-refunded (FEE-052, D-491) (`sources/01_spec/Havasu_Home_Quotes_Technical_Blueprint_v9.3.md:93`). Release happens only on paid or free (PICK-022).

## 7. Adapters and stand-ins

One trait per vendor in `adapters`, implemented by an HTTP client (reqwest with rustls) whose base URL points at the stand-in server, the vendor's sandbox or the live API; the stand-ins in `stand_ins/` are served over HTTP (SEC-032) (`BUILD_BRIEF.md:158`–`BUILD_BRIEF.md:165`). The setting `ADAPTER_MODE_<VENDOR>` picks `stand_in`, `sandbox` or `live`; `live` is accepted only when `APP_ENV=live`, and `stand_in` never in live (SEC-002). Every inbound webhook is signature-checked in the adapter before any module sees it, and recorded by event ID in the owning module's `webhook_event` table (SEC-016, SEC-017). `payments` reads refunds through its own `RefundStripe` trait (T258), whose answer is a typed refund (ID, state `pending`, `requires_action`, `succeeded`, `failed` or `canceled`, amount, currency, PaymentIntent), implemented for the Stripe HTTP client; the adapter's `create_refund` (ID only) stays until T132 retires it, and `payments` records a refund as done only on `succeeded` (D-1753).

| Adapter | Outbound calls | Inbound (owner module) | Phase 1 stand-in | Phase 2 |
|---|---|---|---|---|
| `sms` (Twilio Messaging) | Send text with template ID and language, from the homeowner campaign number, or from the separate business number only while business texting is on (TXT-035); redact a sent or received text's body (`SmsBodyDelete`: `POST Messages/{sid}.json` with an empty `Body`, Twilio's documented redaction; the stand-in then returns an empty body and answers a repeat the same, PRIV-027, TXT-020, D-2156) | Incoming text, delivery status (`messaging`) | Fake Twilio from the twin | Twilio test credentials and magic numbers (`BUILD_BRIEF.md:190`) |
| `voice` (standard programmable voice; Twilio Programmable Voice at launch) | Open/close call session (incoming-call webhook, then dial out with the pool number as caller ID), Verify code calls only to a homeowner who taps "Call me with the code instead" (PICK-013, SEC-008) and never to a business (BIZ-046, D-366), the homeowner safety call behind its attorney switch and the CALL-022 conditions, off at launch (CALL-019, CALL-022, D-384), and Casey's critical-alert call (OPS-035); never an automated call to a business phone (BIZ-039, D-344); never Twilio Proxy, which is a Public Beta with no SLA (CALL-021, D-296) | Call routing, call finished (`calls`) | Fake Twilio | Twilio sandbox |
| `verify` (Twilio Verify) | Send and check a code by SMS or email (voice only for a homeowner's tap, D-366) | — | Fake Twilio | Twilio sandbox |
| `payments` (Stripe) | Customer, optional SetupIntent, one PaymentIntent per pick (FEE-047), created on the first Confirm-page load and confirmed on-session from the Confirm page (D-365), the one off-session exception being the PICK-048 auto-confirm charge, whose PaymentIntent is created and confirmed at the pick (D-401, D-491), PaymentIntent fetch, refund (`send_refund` and `read_refund` return Stripe's Refund with its status, which `payments::api::RefundStripe` parses; only `succeeded` is refunded, D-1753, D-2066), event list | Payment, refund, dispute events (`payments`) | `stripe/stripe-mock` plus the twin's fake Stripe for webhooks and timing | Stripe test mode |
| `ai` (Claude API) | Read request, read quote, screen photo (scrubbed text only; photos only after metadata is stripped, PRIV-014); summarize a quote only while AI quote summaries are on (PICK-003, D-373) | — | Deterministic stand-in AI | Real AI from the Railway test project, fake data only (D-040) |
| `email` (Resend) | Send email from the askhava.com sending subdomain with SPF, DKIM and DMARC (SEC-025) | Bounce, complaint (`messaging`; a hard bounce or complaint marks a business address unverified, BIZ-040) | Fake Resend | Resend test |
| `letters` (Lob) | Send identity letter | Letter status (`messaging`) | Fake Lob | Lob test |
| `license_sources` | Fetch agency daily lists; one throttled live lookup | — | Fixture lists | Real public lists (verification only, `sources/01_spec/Changes_Since_v9.3_AUTHORITATIVE.md:250`) |
| `storage` (object storage) | Write off-database stream, nightly encrypted export and app-encrypted photos (PRIV-023; photos are not stored in PostgreSQL, superseding that part of D-094); delete and list (`StorageDelete`) for the photo purge and its orphan sweep, under the `photos/` prefix's own key only (PRIV-009, D-1841) | — | Local directory (each write attempt its own synced temporary file, published by a hard link that never replaces, D-2155) | Backblaze B2 bucket (D-492: R2 tokens cannot be write-only or prefix-scoped, PRIV-029; the audit-stream and export prefixes keep the write-only key, versioning and object lock) |

Each stand-in has contract tests written from the vendor's published API documentation, run again against the real sandbox in Phase 2 (`BUILD_BRIEF.md:165`, `BUILD_BRIEF.md:191`).

**What the architecture test needs from `adapters`** (`tests/integration/tests/architecture.rs`, T175). The payments adapter sits at `askhava_adapters::api::payments`; any path from `askhava_adapters` with a `payments` segment, a segment containing `stripe`, an alias (`as`) or a glob (`*`) counts as the payments adapter, and only `adapters`, `payments` and `server` may write one (FEE-032, D-595). Every `fn` in an adapter `trait` takes a parameter named or typed with `idempotency` unless its name starts with `fetch_`, `get_`, `list_`, `read_`, `check_` or `lookup_`, the read-only prefixes; `verify_` is not one, as the verify adapter sends codes (SEC-018, D-596).

**What T016 built (D-703).** `app/adapters`: `TwilioConfig` (mode, base URLs, auth token, Verify service, the fixed masked pool from `ASKHAVA_MASKED_NUMBERS`, sized by `ASKHAVA_MASKED_POOL_SIZE`, 10 by default), `IdempotencyKey` (HMAC-SHA256 under the caller's `ID_SECRET` over the action and record IDs), and the `SmsAdapter`, `VoiceAdapter` and `VerifyAdapter` traits with one HTTP client each (`TwilioSms`, `TwilioVoice`, `TwilioVerify`, built by `twilio_clients`). The voice adapter takes only a `PoolNumber` as caller ID and has no method that buys a number (CALL-017).

**What T176 built (D-724 to D-728).** `app/adapters` also holds `StripePayments` (`PaymentsAdapter`), `StandInAi` (`AiAdapter`; loopback IP literal stand-in URL only, no key, AI-009), `ResendEmail` (`EmailAdapter`, with `remove_suppression` for BIZ-056 as `DELETE /suppressions/{address}`), `LobLetters` (`LettersAdapter`), `LicenseLists` (`LicenseSource`; `file://` fixtures or public HTTP lists) and `LocalStorage` (`StorageAdapter`; write-once objects in a `file://` directory; a same-bytes retry succeeds, other bytes are `ObjectExists`), sharing `base::VendorHttp` (encoded path segments, checked vendor IDs, no redirects, no environment proxy, `Idempotency-Key` on every outside action). Every server-side fetch of a URL a person or vendor supplied goes through `fetch_untrusted` with a `FetchPolicy` (allowed hosts, dotted entries only; size cap; one timeout for the whole fetch; https on port 443 only, no userinfo, no environment proxy, lookup under the timeout; every hop's resolved addresses must be public and are the only ones connected to; at most 3 redirects, each re-checked; `FetchPolicy::twilio_media()` for MMS media) (SEC-041). The vendors' settings keys are added by the tasks that wire each client.

**What K8 built (D-121 to D-126).** `stand_ins/` holds Python stand-ins, standard library only, with the vendors' response shapes, error envelopes, idempotency-key behavior and webhook signatures: `twilio.py` (Messages with magic numbers, opt-out words and segment counting, plus Verify), `stripe.py` (a stateful fake with stripe-mock's resource shapes, declines by test payment method, refund limits, events and signed webhooks), `resend.py` (Svix signatures), `lob.py`, and `ai.py` (keyword rules, `ScrubbedText` only, no tools, output validated against the fixed intake fields). The contract tests are in `tests/contract/`; in Phase 2 the same cases run against each vendor's sandbox. `stand_ins/server.py` (T167) serves them all over HTTP on 127.0.0.1 (`python3 -m stand_ins.server --port 4010`), each under its own prefix (`/twilio`, `/stripe`, `/resend`, `/lob`, `/anthropic`) with the vendor's paths, form or JSON bodies, auth headers (only stand-in credentials pass: the stand-in Twilio account SID, Stripe `sk_test_`, Resend `re_stand_in_`, Lob `test_`, and no Claude `sk-ant-` key; D-561), error envelopes and Idempotency-Key behavior below it, so an adapter's base URL setting is the only difference between stand-in, sandbox and live (SEC-032, D-453, D-557); `tests/contract/test_stand_in_server.py` runs the contract cases over the socket. Paths under `/_stand_in/` do what the vendor would do on its own (an inbound text with its signature, the code on the phone, a status callback, a Stripe dispute, a Lob tracking step, and signed Stripe, Resend and Lob webhook deliveries; D-561). The provider's opt-out replies are kept apart from the app's sends (`Messages.json?Direction=outbound-reply`), and a media URL with `.json` gives the Media resource JSON. The fake Twilio also keeps each text's derived key (`X-Askhava-Message-Key`, queried by `MessageKey` on the message list) for OPS-017, a Calls stand-in for the masked voice session, inbound picture texts with a Media DELETE, and the provider's Advanced Opt-Out from `stand_ins/provider_optout.json` (CON-030; D-491, D-492, D-558, D-560). The stand-in AI answers `/v1/messages` and `/v1/messages/count_tokens`, validates its reading against `schemas/intake_reading.json` (AI-022, D-559) and takes the `x-stand-in-fault` and `x-stand-in-plant` headers for TD-AI-14 and TD-AI-10 (D-493, D-560). `stripe/stripe-mock` itself runs in CI.

**The runner's logic** is `runner/`: `state.py` (the real `kit/state.json` shape: list of tasks, one lease per lane in `leases` with lane "1" mirrored in the top-level `lease`, `lanes` from `kit/runner.json`, 1 unless a logged plan change sets more (5 since D-3043), D-515; a task in flight occupies its lane, by its live lease or, once MERGE_PENDING, by its own `lane` field, `in_flight`, D-620; claims, each reserving the task's block of decision numbers `decision_ids` above `main`'s highest entry, the state's `next_decision` and the entries on the head of every open plan or harness pull request (`GitHubEnv.open_plan_decision_numbers`: `claude/` branches that are not task branches, D-1144), D-530; eligibility, backoff; `sync_graph` adds new graph tasks and copies a `deps` list a plan change altered onto every entry not yet DONE or SKIPPED, so eligibility and the lane check read the same dependencies, D-1126), `verify.py` (path check, model check from session records, the DONE check), `outbox.py` (outbox files, the account checkpoint and other owner gates, Gate P), `cycle.py` (one cycle, with git, GitHub and the worker session behind the `Env` protocol; inbox files are acknowledged only after the state recording them is pushed; lease renewal and the final lease release go through `update_state`, which re-reads and retries a lost race, D-515; an empty hand-in, with no commits on top of `main`, never waits for a pull request GitHub will not open: a cleanup task's resolves to DONE or to BLOCKED for its stage proof once `main`'s checks on that head are known green, and fails if they are red or stay unknown for 4 hours, and a task left MERGE_PENDING on one heals on the next cycle, while any other task's, or a hand-in with no task branch, is a failed attempt, D-551) and `proof.py` (the stage proofs the runner records itself from a green nightly run that contains every merge of the stage and the row's tests with no skip path, stage 1 only, D-551). A conditional gate (the real-texts question) clears on a short 'yes' or 'no', records `answer`, and `cycle.py` hands it to dependent tasks as `gate_answers` in the task file (D-552, D-553). The scheduled session supplies the real `Env` (`github_env.py`); `tests/test_failure_drills.py` supplies a fake one. The start-routine smoke test builds on the job's cached `target` (`CARGO_TARGET_DIR`), gets `SMOKE_TIMEOUT` (540 s; since D-2560 the in-session smoke runs detached within `PRE_SMOKE_TIMEOUT` and each call waits up to `SMOKE_WAIT`, 450 s, see section 12), and a timeout is recorded (`smoke_runs[].timed_out`), claims nothing, never counts as a D-498 strike and never sets `main` red, with one outbox note after three on a head (D-1178); the job's Rust build cache is keyed per commit so each run that may save leaves a fresh snapshot (D-1178). When a required check fails on a task's pull request, the failure the retry worker gets (`last_failure`) names each failed check, then, for each check's latest Actions job, its failed steps and the last 15 lines of the failed step's log, read through the jobs REST API with the job token (`RUNNER_CHECKS_TOKEN`, as for check runs) and kept to 1,500 characters; the step's command and `env:` block are left out, and on any API error the failure is the plain list of failed checks (D-582). When the log cannot be downloaded or holds no error line, the reason (the `gh` exit code and the first 200 characters of its stderr, redacted) goes to the cycle log and the check run's failure annotations (`path:line: message`, read with the same job token) stand in for the log lines (D-1263). Before every attempt the runner makes the local task branch itself (`prepare_branch`): from `main` on a first attempt; on a retry whose earlier attempt pushed the branch, from origin's tip with `main` merged in by a normal merge commit (`git merge-tree --write-tree`), so the new commits go on top and the push fast-forwards; an open pull request on a continued branch that has auto-merge on is closed with a comment first (its number kept in the task's `runner_closed_prs`, so later attempts still continue the branch), so no push of unreviewed work can merge, and the runner opens a fresh one after its review; when that branch's pull request was closed or merged by anyone else, cannot be read, or `main` does not merge cleanly, from `main` under a new name `<branch>-a<n>` recorded as the task's `branch` (on a conflict the old pull request is closed through REST with a comment; the old branch stays). Whether origin has a branch is asked with `git ls-remote --exit-code`, and a pull request is closed only while the attempt holds the lease.
