The hand-off carries `branch_ready`, `base` and a `branch_note` telling the session to switch to that branch and never recreate it, `--after` refuses a hand-in that does not build on origin's branch, a push rejected as not a fast-forward is a failed attempt rather than a credential block (the attempt's history and usage kept either way), and a pull request head that is not the task's `reviewed_sha` is never trusted unless its tree is exactly the clean automatic merge of the reviewed commit with the `main` commit the head is based on, never older than the reviewed commit's own fork point from `main` (`reviewed_plus_main`, which accepts GitHub's "Update branch" by merge or by rebase and rejects a new commit, a merge of anything else and a merge that adds content of its own; the reviewed commit is fetched first). Unmerged and not trusted, it fails the attempt instead of waiting forever for an auto-merge the runner refuses, and when auto-merge is on the pull request is closed first (with the `DROP_MARKER` comment and recorded in `runner_closed_prs`) so GitHub cannot merge that head; if the close fails, or a second read shows GitHub merged it just before the close, the task stays MERGE_PENDING so any merge is judged. A trusted updated head merges only if auto-merge is already on; with it off, the attempt fails. Merged and not trusted, the task is BLOCKED and `main` is red with check "review", so FIX-MAIN reverts it and the task then returns to the queue; a merged head git cannot place waits at most 2 hours (`HEAD_CHECK_WAIT`), then the task is BLOCKED with an outbox item for a person. A branch's earlier pull request never judges an empty hand-in whose head is already on `main` (D-584). After a session dies, only an open (or merged) pull request whose head is the reviewed head of that very attempt (its `review_lease`) is adopted as MERGE_PENDING; a closed, runner-closed or unreviewed one leaves the task for its retry (D-584). `python3 -m runner --requeue T### --reason TEXT` moves a task BLOCKED by something outside its own work (a rejected push, no head, a permission block, a missing stage proof, an unchecked merge) back to QUEUED, with `attempts` unchanged (never past the 3-attempt cap) and the reason appended to its `last_failure` (D-584); it acts only while no lease is live, and records the re-queue in the task's `requeues` list (D-583); for FIX-MAIN it acts only while `main` is red and FIX-MAIN is BLOCKED, and restarts it with `attempts` 0 (D-1099), while `--plan-fix` still refuses FIX-MAIN. A FIX-MAIN BLOCKED for any reason while `main` is red is retried by the red-main step itself (`blocked_fix_main`, D-1099): the cycle that first finds it BLOCKED stamps `blocked_at`, at least 30 minutes later (`FIX_MAIN_COOL_OFF`) it is QUEUED again with `attempts` 0, `owns`, `revert_sha` and `offending_task` kept, the retry recorded in its and `main_red`'s `fix_main_rounds`, every earlier failure added to its goal and one `info` outbox item written per retry; after 3 retries (`FIX_MAIN_ROUNDS`) it stays BLOCKED with one `blocked` outbox item naming the files, the revert SHA and `--requeue FIX-MAIN`. A smoke run still red after FIX-MAIN's revert merged leaves FIX-MAIN DONE with one `blocked` outbox item (`revert_landed_red`), and each later cycle smoke-tests the head again; a retried or re-queued FIX-MAIN no longer counts as not started for the D-498 false-red withdrawal, and `main` stays red until a verified green run. The one way past the cap is a plan fix (D-677): `--requeue T### --plan-fix D-### --reason TEXT` gives a task that used all its attempts exactly one more, on Opus, only when, measured from the start of its last attempt (its `started_at`, else its `ended_at`, `cycle.plan_since`), D-### is in `main`'s `docs/decisions.md` and in the task's own `decisions` list (`verify.task_decisions`) now but in neither then, and the task's plan fingerprint (`verify.plan_fingerprint`: its `kit/tasks.json` entry and its prompt) differs between `main` then and now (`GitHubEnv.plan_evidence`); once per decision, never under a lease that matters, with `plan_fix`, `plan_since`, both `main` commits and both fingerprints kept in `requeues`. The same command brings back a task whose worker reported a plan gap (D-2859): a worker that finds the task cannot pass without a change the plan forbids it (a file or test owned by another task, two lines that contradict, a budget it cannot fit) opens its report with `PLAN GAP: <file:line> <owner or contradiction> <smallest plan change>` (`kit/prompts/TEMPLATE.md`), and the session hands in with `--outcome plan_gap` once its reviewer confirms the cited ownership or contradiction, passing `--log` through a quoted heredoc. `cycle.session_outcome`, run only by `--after` on the session's own `--log` (a RUNNER_WORKER_CMD result file reports its outcome itself), also turns a `tests_failed` into `plan_gap` when the log opens with the marker, or when its first sentence (up to the first ". " or line end) says "a/the plan change must" or "<T###, it, this task, the task, or ; : ,> needs a plan/harness change, task or fix" (or "needs plan/harness fix") with no hedge in that sentence (`PLAN_GAP_HEDGES`: not, no, never, n't, but, thought, think(s), if, whether, maybe, might, goal, says, said, a quotation mark or backtick). `cycle.record_plan_gap` counts the attempt and sets the task BLOCKED at once with a reason opening `plan gap reported`, which STATUS.md lists under "Needs attention", and writes the outbox item `T###-plan-gap` (as D-1869's owned gap does); `--plan-fix` lifts it after the plan fix with the attempts it has left, and a plain `--requeue T### --reason` lifts a mistaken block while attempts remain (the attempt stays counted; at the cap only D-677 applies), so the task is never claimed again on the prompt that could not pass. A pull request is closed before it is commented on, and the auto-merge closing comment carries a marker, so a later attempt still recognises a pull request the runner closed when recording it in `runner_closed_prs` was lost (D-584). **Lanes (D-620).** `runner/lanes.py` decides where a cycle may claim: at most `lanes` tasks in flight (1 while `lanes_fallback` is set), a backup-runner run claims only on the lane its gate picked (`RUNNER_LANE`; `run_lane` in `state.py`: for a merged pull request's run, that task's own lane whenever it holds no live lease, since the merge frees it, D-723; otherwise a lane with no task in flight, the event's task's own first, then a lane with no live lease, else lane 1; only lane 1 while `lanes_fallback` is set), and a task is claimed only with every dependency of its `kit/tasks.json` entry DONE or SKIPPED, checked with both lanes free too (a proof-waiting cleanup task of its own stage counts then, D-551; D-1127), and beside a task in flight only in the same stage, with no owned path overlapping (a task owning a locked file also owns `kit/plan.lock`), and never a cleanup task on either side; FIX-MAIN is exempt, and while `main` is red nothing else is eligible on any lane. Every MERGE_PENDING task is resolved each cycle; a waiting or failed result ends the cycle only on the run's own lane, so a run serving a free lane still claims. Pacing counts the recorded estimate of every other IN_PROGRESS task as used (`in_flight_tokens`); the plan usage the action reports about itself (its `rate_limit_event` messages, the last one kept from its execution file by the job step `python3 -m runner --usage-reading`, `runner/usage_reading.py`) is `usage.session_reading`, which pacing uses for the windows it holds while under six hours old, after a probe reading and before the ledger (D-1155); with `spread` in `kit/pacing.json` the week's spend is held to a straight line from an anchor reading to the effective budget a few hours before the reset, a claim above the line waiting until the line reaches it (`spend_line`, D-1128; `spread` is absent since D-1189, so the pacer claims whenever the task fits the five-hour and weekly budgets, and putting `spread` back restores the hold; a null `budget_tokens_five_hour`, as since D-3056, leaves the five-hour window out of that test, the standing and the projected finish, while a real reading at 100% for any window still holds the claim until its reset, `budgeted` and `at_limit`), and `kit/runner.json` is back to `lanes: 1` (D-1128). While `main` is red no pull request but FIX-MAIN's gets an update merge or auto-merge, and when no numbered lane can take FIX-MAIN (tasks that guard holds fill them, or a fall back or a lower `lanes` cut the capacity) it takes the reserved "fix" lane, which never counts against `lanes` (`lanes.fix_lane`); a cycle with no lane to claim on still runs the red-main step, so a finished FIX-MAIN always clears `main` (D-630). Two harness-type failures in a row on a lane above 1, each an attempt that ended (a rejected branch push, an attempt that lost its writes, three lost races in one run, a required check that failed on an update merge after the same check passed on the reviewed head it started from, judged on that head's final conclusions, read again when they were still running at the update (`checks_on`, D-630), an update merge that conflicted or whose third push was rejected), set `lanes_fallback`, write an outbox item and keep the runner on one lane until `python3 -m runner --restore-lanes`; a task on that lane reaching DONE or failing for its own work clears the strikes. With two or more lanes a lost race is tried again from a fresh read twice (hand-off mode only), and the run's report names the free lanes a task could be claimed on (`refill`; none while the pacer's last decision is a wait still ahead, D-723), from which `backup-runner.yml`'s chain step starts one run per lane after a result that moved a task, or a row whose `changed.tasks` shows a task moved, such as a run whose own lane still waits but which resolved another lane's merge (D-723), and a cycle that hands off starts those runs at once itself (`start_refill`, `GitHubEnv.dispatch_lane`, `gh workflow run` with the job token `RUNNER_CHECKS_TOKEN`, D-723), so a freed lane never waits for a worker's whole session (the chain step reads this job's own last row that did something; `--after` removes the hand-off once it finished the task, so the safety step adds no empty row); the chain step also starts the next cycle at once after this job's `smoke_timeout` row (an in-session smoke past `SMOKE_TIMEOUT` or this job's pre-session smoke past `PRE_SMOKE_TIMEOUT`, D-2028) when it is the first timeout on that head (the row's `changed.keys.smoke_runs` holds one `timed_out` run for the last run's SHA), since "Smoke build cache, save" has already kept the partial build; a second timeout on the same head waits for the keeper, so it chains at most once per commit of `main` (D-1970); finish's last write lays on only its own `usage` delta (a pacing window that rolled over keeps only the newer window's tokens, D-630) and its lane's strikes; `--requeue` with two lanes waits only for a lease on the task itself or on a task that could not run beside it; the hourly tick dispatches one run per lane without a live lease; the backup job's concurrency group is per lane (lane 1 keeps `backup-runner`). STATUS.md lists every lane's lease. **Plan-change numbers and plan gaps (D-1058).** `python3 -m runner --reserve-decision --reason TEXT` (`reserve_decision` in `runner/__main__.py`, `state.reserve_plan_number`) gives a change made outside the runner one D-number from the live counter through `update_state`: the first at or above `next_decision`, above `main`'s highest entry and above every reserved number, kept in the state's `plan_reservations` ({number, at, reason, session}, which `_reserved` and `scripts/check_state.py` count as reserved) with `next_decision` moved past it; refused with an empty reason or an unreadable `main` file. A submitted attempt whose only problems are files its plan did not list (`not owned by T###`, `cycle.plan_gap_failure`) goes back to QUEUED with `attempts` unchanged and one entry in the task's `plan_gap_strikes` ({at, paths, attempt}), its history entry marked `plan_gap`; a second such failure, one beside any other problem or a failed test run, or one on a task at the attempt cap (its plan-fix attempt) counts as an attempt. **FIX-MAIN keeps the log (D-1088).** Its goal (`_fix_main_fields`) tells the worker to revert the squash commit and then restore `docs/decisions.md` from `main` before appending its own entry, because a plain revert removes the reverted task's entries and fails the append-only rule. **Decision entries in order (D-1029).** When a retry's earlier pushed branch (`prepare_branch`) or a submitted hand-in (`collect_result`) has `docs/decisions.md` entries inserted before its merge base's last entry, as a worker holding a lower reserved block does when a plan change took a higher number, `UpdateMerge.in_order` (`runner/update_merge.py`) commits on top of it the base's file followed by the new entries in the branch's order, so `scripts/check_pr_change.py`'s append-only rule passes; a changed or removed entry is left alone and still fails. **Keeping pull requests current (D-620).** When a MERGE_PENDING task's pull request head is its reviewed head and `main` is not in it (behind, or conflicted), `GitHubEnv.update_branch` merges `main` in itself: a normal merge commit (reviewed head first, `main` second, dated at `main`'s commit so two runners make the same commit), pushed as a fast-forward only while origin's branch is still the reviewed head, never rebased or forced. Its tree is `UpdateMerge.tree` (`runner/update_merge.py`): `git merge-tree --write-tree`, with `docs/decisions.md` as `main`'s text then the lines the branch appended (whatever the union driver did; a branch that edited old entries or reused a number `main` has is a conflict) and `kit/plan.lock` recomputed by `scripts/check_plan_lock.py` when both sides rewrote it, each side's lock matched its own tree and neither side changed that script; a conflicting `docs/for-casey-review.md`, `docs/architecture.md` or `Cargo.toml` is resolved only when every conflict in it is a pure insertion on both sides at the same place (`git merge-file --diff3` shows no base line between them), keeping main's block then the branch's, a block after a new section opening its own, no side holding a line shaped like a 40-character conflict marker, a resolved `Cargo.toml` that still parses with no inserted dotted key and no locked section of `docs/architecture.md` that is neither side's (D-891); any other conflicting path is a real conflict. `prepare_branch` merges `main` into a retry's branch with the same tree, and a new review drops the attempt's `update_merges`. `enable_auto_merge` waits up to 30 seconds for the pull request to show a just-pushed head, and update pushes use `RUNNER_PUSH_TOKEN` like any task-branch push. The merge commit is checked against that tree before it is pushed, then becomes the task's `reviewed_sha` (recorded in `update_merges`), so `_merged`, the unreviewed-head path and auto-merge treat it as reviewed; `reviewed_plus_main` uses the same tree. A head that is an update merge of the reviewed head (parents exactly the reviewed commit and a commit on `main`, that tree) is adopted as reviewed when the record was lost, and a PR still showing an earlier reviewed head just after an update waits. A real conflict is resynced on the same attempt (D-891): a PR with auto-merge on is closed first (as for an unreviewed head; a failed close keeps the task MERGE_PENDING), the task goes back to QUEUED with `attempts` unchanged and a `resync` record (attempt, reviewed head, main, paths; kept in `resyncs`), the next claim on any lane takes it first, `prepare_branch` makes the branch at the reviewed head without `main` merged in (only while origin's tip is still that head and its PR was not ended by someone else), and the worker, told only to merge `main` in and resolve (`resync_log`, the hand-off's `resync` and `branch_note`), hands in through the normal path check, fresh-eyes review, review record, push and auto-merge; its history entry carries `resync: true` and `plan_since` skips it. A crashed resync re-runs like any crashed attempt, and one whose reviewed merge origin already holds drops its `resync` and goes on normally. A resync that cannot start on the reviewed head, fails, or a conflict after 2 resyncs in the attempt (`RESYNC_LIMIT`) fails the attempt, and the retry starts from the branch through `prepare_branch` (`main` merged in, or fresh on `-a<n>` when that still conflicts, D-583); rejected update pushes fail the attempt after 3, and an update git cannot make waits at most 2 hours (`UPDATE_WAIT`). This works with one lane too, for a `main` moved by a person. **The hand-in merge (D-2798).** When a hand-in's reviewed head is already behind `main` at the push, `finish` (`cycle._hand_in_merge`) asks `GitHubEnv.hand_in_merge` for the very update merge stage C would build later (`_built_update`, shared with `update_branch`: `UpdateMerge.commit` checked by `is_update_merge` against the reviewed head and `main`'s head; same tree, parents, message and dates), and the review record adopts it in the same write (`_adopt_update`, `how: "hand-in"`, `from` the reviewed head), so the state names the pushed head before the one push carries both commits and only the merged head's checks run; auto-merge, the PR-head check and crash adoption see one reviewed head. Not behind, `main` red (for any task but FIX-MAIN, checked before the build and again in the review record's write, as stage C refuses an update merge then), a conflict, a failed tree check, a git error, a dry run or an `Env` without the method push the reviewed head unmerged as before, and stage C (and its resync) handles it; `main` moving again during the checks is stage C's as always. The pre-hand-in gate still runs on the reviewed head. A red check on such a merge neither strikes its lane nor clears the lane's strikes (`_unjudged_hand_in`: there is no earlier round on the reviewed head to compare, so `_broken_by_main` cannot judge it). **Claim order (D-2540).** A cycle tries the claimable ids (eligible, by day, compatible with the lanes) in the order `cycle.claim_order` gives: a resync first (D-891), then the ids `kit/runner.json` lists in `claim_first` (read by `runner/__main__.py` `claim_first`, `()` when missing or malformed), in the list's order, then the rest in state order; only the order changes, so a listed id that is not claimable is ignored and plan health and pacing still judge each candidate in turn. With `"order": "critical_path"` in `kit/runner.json` (D-2591; read by `runner/__main__.py` `claim_order_setting`, `"state"` when missing, malformed or any other value) the rest are tried longest chain first: `cycle.chain_lengths` counts, for each plan task, the tasks on its longest chain of dependents not DONE or SKIPPED, itself and gate tasks included (memoised, iterative, a malformed cycle cut where it closes), and state order breaks ties; removing the key restores the state order. With `"prefer_disjoint_crates": true` in `kit/runner.json` (D-3082; read by `runner/__main__.py` `prefer_disjoint_crates`, false when missing, malformed or anything but `true`) one more key comes after the chain and before state order: an id that owns a path in an `app/` crate that a task in flight (a live lease or MERGE_PENDING, `state.in_flight`) also owns (`lanes.crowded`, crates from `lanes.crates`) is tried after the ids of equal resync, `claim_first` and chain rank that share none, since both would update that crate's `CLAUDE.md` and the later one would need a resync (D-891). It never drops an id, so when every candidate shares a crate the claim is the same as before and every lane still fills; setting it false restores the order without it.

## 8. Contact vault data flow

**What is in the vault** (`privacy.contact` and friends): homeowner names, phone numbers, emails, street addresses, on-site contacts, original photos, and the raw text of homeowner messages (texts and web-chat lines, both ways) (PRIV-001; D-093). Every other table refers to them by ID. Business text is not homeowner data: a business's quote stays in `quotes.quote` as its original wording (PICK-003), checked there for contact details (PICK-007).

**Protection:** each field is encrypted with AES-256-GCM under `VAULT_KEY`, a variable separate from the database credentials (PRIV-002); the associated data binds each ciphertext to its table, column and row, so a ciphertext copied to another row fails to decrypt; each ciphertext names its key version so the key can be rotated. Phone lookup uses a blind index, HMAC-SHA-256 of the normalized number under `VAULT_INDEX_KEY`, so the database can find a number without holding it in clear. Every plaintext read writes an audit row naming the reader, the purpose and the record (PRIV-003). Photos are encrypted the same way, under `VAULT_KEY` with their job and photo ID as associated data, before the `storage` adapter writes them, and are served only by the app's photo route (PRIV-023, PRIV-014, D-454). An outside key service (envelope encryption under AWS or Google Cloud KMS) is an option for the account checkpoint, not part of the build; the key-version field lets it be added later (D-454).

**As built (T022, D-749, D-750):** each `privacy.contact` column holds a 2-byte key version, a random 96-bit nonce and the ciphertext, with associated data `privacy.contact|<column>|<row id>|v<version>`; `VAULT_KEY` and `VAULT_INDEX_KEY` each list `<version>:<base64>` keys, the highest active and all readable, and `rotate_batch` moves rows and phone digests to the active versions. Each reader and purpose is a grant in `privacy`'s `models::GRANTS` that also limits the fields it opens (the release never opens the phone); `read_contact` serves `messaging` and `calls`, and the release and realm reads take their proof types. Each read writes one audit row per field (`vault_read.<reader>.<purpose>`) and an hourly count in `privacy.read_count`; `check_read_rates` raises a High alert above three times the 14-day hourly baseline, and a read outside every grant is refused and alerted at once (SEC-047). A read refuses a connection already inside a transaction, the release read consumes its `ReleaseProof` and logs one more audit row naming the pick, and the refusal's alert names the reader and purpose (D-752). A `ReleaseProof` names both the pick and its homeowner's contact, and the release read opens only that contact; the in-transaction check covers refusals too and asks the server as well as sqlx, so a raw `BEGIN` is seen (D-753). **Built by T246 (D-1516, D-1602):** a tenth grant, `(Reader::Picks, Purpose::PickCode, [Phone], "vault_read.picks.pick_code")`, read only through `privacy::api::read_for_pick_code`, which only `picks` may name (the reader scan lists it): `picks` reads the homeowner's phone to send and check the page pick's Verify code, and the number never reaches `portals`, a response, a log or an error. `picks::api::send_page_code` and `check_page_code` take the job and her vault contact ID (from `intake::api::job_homeowner`), read the phone themselves on a connection outside any transaction (a malformed code reads nothing) and call `send_code` and `check_code`; a failed read is `PicksError::Database` with no value.

**Built by T253 (D-1739, D-1891 to D-1895):** the business portal's own read of a released pick, through `picks` and never `privacy`, with no stored copy (PRIV-002). `migrations/0129_picks_release_binding.sql` binds each `picks.release` row to the contact the `ReleaseProof` named (`contact_id`, an ID only) and her PICK-018 choice (`mail_chosen`), both `NOT NULL`, written in `release`'s claim on the regular and the emergency path. `privacy` adds `BusinessSessionProof` (private person and business; no `Clone` or `Default`), made only by `SessionMint::business`, which `portals`' `BusinessPrincipal::session_proof` calls for a verified business session (`tests/security/ui/forge_session_proof_business.rs`); `server::session_minter()` takes the session mint once per process in a `OnceLock`. An eleventh grant, `(Reader::Picks, Purpose::ReleasedView, [Name, Address, Email], "vault_read.picks.released_view")`, has no phone; `privacy::api::read_released(db, vault, &proof, (pick, contact), fields)` reads under it, one row per field with the person as actor (`Actor::Business(person)`) and one with the pick as subject, refuses a connection inside a transaction, and returns `None` (opening and auditing nothing) for a contact the purge or "Delete my data" cleared. `picks::api::released_view(pool, vault, &proof, pick)` reads the release binding, then checks the pure `may_view`: a release row, status `paid` or `free` (`status::shows_release`), the proof's business is the pick's, and the person is now its owner or a "Confirm and billing" member (`businesses::api::members` at each view); every refusal is `ViewAnswer::NotFound` and reads nothing; it opens the bound contact's name and address, the email only when `email_chosen`, and answers `Shown(ReleaseView)` (first name only) or `Removed`. Viewing writes no release row, notice, charge, fee or masked line. Only `picks` may name `read_released` (the reader scan).

**As built (T023, D-800 to D-804):** `privacy::api::scrub` is the only maker of `ScrubbedText` (private field, no `Default`, `From` or public constructor; `tests/security/ui/forge_scrubbed_text.rs`). It matches on an NFKC copy with Cf characters dropped and Arabic-Indic and Devanagari digits read as ASCII, and replaces each removed run with `[removed]`: stated names (the AI-002 phrases plus "i am", "my name's" and "mi nombre es", and sign-offs at the end), digit runs of 7 or more in digits and English or Spanish number words, emails, "at"/"dot" spelled emails, @handles, links, bare domains, street numbers with their names, gate codes, the vault name and every name held in the caller's opaque `NameMemory` (AI-002, AI-003, AI-020). The common-words list is a `CommonWords` the caller passes, `DEFAULT_COMMON_WORDS` until the owner setting exists (D-800). `read_inbound(raw, words, known, memory)` returns `Flags` (emergency stem at a word start; sole-word opt-out, HELP, START) and `ScrubbedText`. `read_inbound_email` checks the Svix signature (`whsec_` key, `v1,` entries, 5-minute window), reads an `email.received` body with `data.from`, `data.text` and `data.auth.{spf,dkim,dmarc}`, gives `Standing::OptOutOnly` unless all three pass (a missing auth block fails; HELP and START are cleared for it; an opt-out word alone on the first line opts out; callers dedupe on `svix-id`, D-804), and a `SenderIndex` under the `email` index key with a `business|` or `homeowner|` input prefix (PRIV-026, D-802). Writing the raw body to `privacy.message_body` and the contact details a person states to the contact record wait for the migration that creates `privacy.message_body`, which no task owns yet (D-801).

**As built through stage 2 (T026 to T030, T178, T240, T241; D-1362):** the inbound text runs in `server`'s route (`app/server/src/inbound_text.rs`), one transaction per Twilio delivery, in this order: `messaging::api::receive_inbound` (dedupe by MessageSid, TXT-012), `privacy::api::read_inbound` (flags on the raw text, then `ScrubbedText`; the raw body goes no further), `messaging::api::on_inbound_text` (opt-out, the CON-016 listed-number stop and the emergency alert for a listed number), `messaging::api::reply_to_help`, then `intake::api::receive_reply` for a number that is not a linked business phone. Step 1's storage of the raw body and the stated contact details still waits for `privacy.message_body` (D-801). Outbound, a homeowner's phone is read from the vault in one function, `messaging`'s `service::recipient_phone` (`read_contact`, `Reader::Messaging`, `Purpose::Send`, PRIV-003, PRIV-004), used both by `service::send` and by `optout::send_checked`'s suppression check, so the number checked is the number texted; `messaging::api::send_window` reads it under the same grant for callers that may not see it (D-1301). The worker that sends queued `messaging.outbox` rows through `send_checked` is not built yet (T241's entry, section 10). `ai`'s screen drops Unicode format characters with `privacy::api::is_format`, the scrubber's own list, so the two cannot drift (AI-020, AI-021, D-814).

**As built (T257, D-1752, D-2086 to D-2090):** the SMS route resolves the sender by blind index first; a homeowner's text then goes to `privacy::api::read_inbound_for(conn, vault, contact, raw, words)`, which opens her vault name under the `(Messaging, Inbound)` grant (one `vault_read.messaging.inbound` audit row and one SEC-047 count per text) and her stated names from `privacy.stated_name` (`migrations/0130_privacy_stated_names.sql`: sealed with associated data `privacy.stated_name|name|<contact>|v<version>`, a `stated-name|vN` digest unique per contact, `ON DELETE CASCADE` from the contact, app role SELECT, INSERT, DELETE), scrubs with both, seals any name the text newly states, and returns only flags and `ScrubbedText`. It runs inside the delivery's transaction, so its audit rows and new names commit or roll back with the delivery and a resent delivery is read again; every function that returns plaintext still refuses a transaction (D-752). A business phone's text is scrubbed with an empty context. A vault database or audit failure in the read answers 500 (the delivery rolls back); any other vault error (a value that will not open, no contact) falls back to the empty-context read, so an opt-out or HELP still runs, and the text then stops before `intake` (D-2088). The PRIV-007 purge and "Delete my data" delete her stated names; `rotate_batch` re-seals them, its re-insert skipping a name the contact already holds under the active digest (D-2089). No name, history or digest of a name leaves `privacy`.

**Who may read plaintext** (D-093, from PRIV-004), each read through `privacy.api` and audit-logged:

- `messaging`: to send a text, email or letter, and to hand a raw inbound homeowner message to the inbound reader;
- `picks`: the release, and the only path by which a business sees released details (the business portal asks `picks.api`, never `privacy`);
- `calls`: to bridge a masked call; the number goes straight to the voice adapter; and, for the CALL-005 read-back to a phone linked to the paid business after 2 bad PINs on an A-attested call, the homeowner's first name and street only, read under the same audited-read rule (PRIV-003, D-491);
- `portals`, homeowner realm: a signed-in homeowner's own record (PRIV-021);
- `portals`, owner realm: the contact on a call-back alert (INT-023, CON-016, TXT-015, TXT-023), and a help request's own conversation on that request's page (OPS-036, D-348, D-478). Alerts in `ops.alert` carry the contact ID, never the number (PRIV-006); Casey's view reads the number at the moment he opens the alert;
- `privacy` itself: the inbound reader, scrubbing, purges; `read_inbound_for` opens a homeowner's vault name (under the `(Messaging, Inbound)` grant) and her sealed stated names (`privacy.stated_name`) only to scrub her text and returns no plaintext, so unlike the plaintext reads it may run inside the caller's transaction (D-1752, T257).

- `quotes`: card photos only (CARD-013, CARD-014), the screened copies, for the card email and the business job page; never a contact field (PRIV-004, D-477). Nobody else.

**Flow of one request:**

1. **Inbound.** Twilio's webhook (signature checked) reaches `messaging`, which dedupes by message ID (TXT-012) and looks the sender up in its sender directory.
   - **From a business phone:** the text is business text. It is stored in `messaging.inbox`. For a business that chose text it is passed on (as an event with its ID) to `intake`, which routes it to `quotes` or `picks`; for a business on email it is never routed as a quote, command or homeowner request and is answered only as TXT-037 says (D-303).
   - **From anyone else (a homeowner):** `messaging` hands the raw text, with the keyword lists it reads from `catalog`, to `privacy.api.read_inbound()`. This is the only code that sees raw homeowner text. It stores the text in `privacy.message_body`; stores contact details the person gave ("my name is …", "my email is …") in the contact record (INT-012); matches the emergency keyword stems (INT-002) and the sole-word opt-out, HELP and START words (CON-008, CON-018) on the raw text; and returns the flags plus `ScrubbedText` (names after "this is / my name is", street-number patterns, 7+ digit runs, emails, and the homeowner's vault name removed; AI-002, AI-003). Short commands ("2 YES", "CLOSE 1042", "1", "ok") survive the scrub.
   - **Order** (`messaging`, then `intake`): dedupe; emergency check; then opt-out, HELP and START. An emergency match from an opted-out number still raises the critical alert to Casey and sends no text (CON-017, CON-013). Then `intake` gets an event with the message ID, the flags and the `ScrubbedText` (stored in `messaging.inbox`) and runs the safety flow, consent and routing. Web chat follows the same path from `site`.
2. **Intake** never sees raw homeowner text; it works from the flags and `ScrubbedText`. `businesses` keeps the sender directory current through `messaging.api` whenever a main or staff phone is added or removed.
3. **AI.** `ai` accepts only `ScrubbedText`, a type only `privacy` can build (AI-004); it returns schema-checked fields or nothing (AI-005). No AI call happens in live until zero retention is signed or the owner's "AI on scrubbed text" switch is on after the provider's terms are recorded (AI-008, D-412); either way photos never go to the AI before zero retention (CARD-013).
4. **Card.** `quotes` builds the card from the scrubbed description, fixed fields and the screened card photos it reads through `privacy.api` (CARD-013, CARD-014, D-477), and runs the final plain-code gate on every card (CARD-007, CARD-008). Cards carry town and kind of place only (CARD-006).
5. **Pick and release.** `picks` asks `payments` whether the pick is paid (or free); only then does it read first name, address, full-size photos (the screened card photos went out with the card) and, when the homeowner chose email contact, the email they chose to share (PICK-018), and hand them, through `picks.api`, to the signed-in business portal only (PICK-022, PRIV-005); `messaging` sends a release email that carries no homeowner detail, only the job number and a sign-in link (PICK-024, SEC-034, D-454), and never a text (TXT-036, D-306). The homeowner's phone number is never released; `calls` reads it only to connect a masked call (PICK-023, CALL-002). The owner portal's release power only re-sends such a release (D-074).
6. **Purge.** A daily `privacy` job deletes name, address, email and photos 180 days after close unless a dispute or complaint hold exists; keeps the phone, encrypted, only with its consent and opt-out records for 5 years after the last message; deletes photos 30 days after close; and after 30 days cuts every message body (TXT-020, D-079): an outbound text keeps only its template ID; an inbound text, which has no template ID, keeps only its kind (for example "inbound homeowner text" or "inbound business text"), its time, its sender reference and its job ID, and its raw text in `privacy.message_body` and its `ScrubbedText` copy in `messaging.inbox` are both deleted. The job's scrubbed description in `jobs.job` is kept 3 years (PRIV-008), and a business's quote wording stays in `quotes.quote` (PICK-003) (PRIV-007, PRIV-009).

   **Retention lock order (T274, D-1839, D-2150 to D-2154).** Everything that decides retention takes, in this order and never another: (1) the transaction-scoped retention advisory key (`hashtextextended('privacy.retention', 274)`), exclusive for a contact purge's decide-and-clear transaction (`privacy::api::lock_retention_for_purge`) and shared inside every `place_hold` and `release_hold`; (2) the homeowner row (`intake`'s `lock_homeowner`, which every request-opening writer also takes, the request upsert locking it); (3) the vault contact row (`purge_contact`'s `UPDATE`). `intake`'s contact stage and jobless-request stage (`run_purge`, `run_request_purge`) take (1) and (2), then read eligibility (her other live requests and all her jobs in one statement, and `privacy::api::any_held` over those jobs), and clear the contact in the same transaction (`purge_contact` as a savepoint). Before taking any lock they read `core::api::unfinished` for hold-producing tasks (any unfinished `businesses.complaint_hold` event, and another job's contact-stage `jobs.purge_due` or `privacy.purge_recheck`, where `payments` places dispute holds); a task's `done_at` is written only after its hold committed, so this is safe outside the lock and keeps the `core.task` scan off the exclusive key. With one unfinished the purge clears nothing and queues itself again an hour later (dedupe key naming the task that queued it) until it has finished (D-2153). Known gap: an answer to an open jobless request takes no homeowner lock (D-2154). A request or hold that commits first is seen; one that comes later waits and then finds the contact cleared, as it would today. No outside call is made inside it (`tests/integration/tests/retention_purge_races.rs`, TD-PRIV-18).

## 9. Environments and deployment shape

| Environment | Where | Data | Adapters | Keys |
|---|---|---|---|---|
| Build | Cloud session VM, local PostgreSQL 16 | Fake only | `stand_in` | None in Phase 1; sandbox network secrets in Phase 2 (`BUILD_BRIEF.md:296`) |
| CI | GitHub Actions, Postgres service container | Fake only | `stand_in` | None (`BUILD_BRIEF.md:517`) |
| Test | Railway **test** project | Fake only | `sandbox` | Test keys in test-project variables |
| Live | Railway **live** project | Real | `live` | Live keys entered by Casey at Gate C |

(`BUILD_BRIEF.md:512`–`BUILD_BRIEF.md:521`.) "Railway" means the host chosen per the changes file (OPS-012, D-024). In CI the check workflows `ci.yml` (`Ci`), `twin.yml` (`Twin`) and `a11y.yml` (`Ally`) start only on a push to `claude/**` or `dependabot/**` (never on `pull_request`), so each head commit gets exactly one run of each and one `governance.yml` run after its `ci` run; `governance.yml` publishes nothing for a head whose pull request has already merged (D-1087).

Each Railway project has four services: `web`, `worker`, `migrate` and PostgreSQL 16, plus its own phone numbers, webhook endpoints, `VAULT_KEY`, `VAULT_INDEX_KEY`, `ID_SECRET`, storage bucket and vendor keys; nothing is shared between test and live (SEC-003). The test project auto-deploys from `main`; the live project has auto-deploy off and deploys only when Casey presses Deploy (OPS-022; `sources/01_spec/Changes_Since_v9.3_AUTHORITATIVE.md:305`). Live deploys from a protected `release` branch Casey moves to the signed SHA (OPS-043). Claude never holds a hosting key. Migrations run on a separate `migrate` service (`server migrate`, restart Never), the only holder of `ASKHAVA_MIGRATION_DATABASE_URL` and the bootstrap token; it deploys before `web` and `worker`, and each migration keeps the previous release working (SEC-052, D-491). Settings live in the `docs/deploy.md` table, never a `railway.toml` (OPS-050); `web` and `worker` restart On Failure and exit after 3 minutes of failed liveness (OPS-049); never roll back past a key rotation (OPS-054); the roles themselves are created once per cluster by `scripts/bootstrap_roles.sql` (T012; its timeouts and grants, D-699), never by a migration, and `scripts/smoke.sh` and `Ci` create `askhava_app` and `askhava_migrator` before any migration. Migration numbers are frozen from the first deploy to the test project: sqlx refuses a renumbered applied migration, so a later migration may have a lower number but an applied one never changes (D-491). The live project serves askhava.com through Cloudflare DNS, every hostname DNS-only at launch, webhooks on `hooks.askhava.com` (SEC-053, SEC-054); the test project uses only its own host-provided domain.

## 10. Off-database records and recovery

Written as they happen to the `storage` adapter (OPS-010, OPS-011; `sources/01_spec/Havasu_Home_Quotes_Technical_Blueprint_v9.3.md:690`): the audit stream, the web request log (request IDs, routes with tokens redacted, status codes) and the AI structured readings. With Twilio's and Stripe's own records, they are the replay inputs after a restore (OPS-015 to OPS-019). The nightly encrypted export (`pg_dump -Fc`) goes to the same bucket under a separate key, kept 30 days (OPS-013); a restore runs the role bootstrap first (OPS-051). Point-in-time recovery comes from the host (OPS-012).

**What T250 built (D-1520, D-1617).** `migrations/0110_schema_default_privileges.sql` narrows the default privileges of every schema but `payments` (0098, T245): a table created from here on in `core`, `ops`, `catalog`, `jobs`, `privacy`, `ai`, `messaging`, `licensing`, `intake`, `businesses`, `portals`, `quotes` or `picks` gives `askhava_app` SELECT and INSERT only, so an append-only record (the audit log, OPS-010; consent and opt-out records, CON-022) no longer depends on a remembered REVOKE (D-092). The migration is 13 plain `ALTER DEFAULT PRIVILEGES IN SCHEMA <schema> REVOKE UPDATE, DELETE ON TABLES FROM askhava_app` statements and nothing else, the one shape in which a `core` migration may name other modules' schemas (section 4 rule 3); `tests/integration/tests/architecture_migrations.rs` (TD-SEC-35) allows exactly that shape and voids it for a file holding any other statement, `FOR ROLE`, `GRANT OPTION FOR`, another object kind or another grantee. No existing table's grants changed. A table needing UPDATE or DELETE now gets an explicit `GRANT ... TO askhava_app` in its own migration, and a later schema (`calls`, T076; `recovery`, T109) writes its default as SELECT, INSERT. `tests/integration/tests/schema_grants.rs` (TD-SEC-34) pins all 66 non-payments tables' app-role privileges with the migration and need behind each UPDATE or DELETE, checks every schema's default in `pg_default_acl`, forbids TRUNCATE, REFERENCES and TRIGGER, keeps `ops.audit_log`, `intake.consent_record`, `messaging.optout_event` and `quotes.job_card` at SELECT, INSERT, proves a new table in each schema refuses UPDATE, DELETE and TRUNCATE as the app role, and holds a table outside the list to SELECT, INSERT plus the explicit GRANTs it parses from `migrations/`, so later tasks need no edit there.

**What T015 built (D-729).** `app/ops` and `migrations/0021_ops_audit.sql`: `ops.audit_log` (append-only: the app role keeps `SELECT, INSERT` and loses `UPDATE, DELETE, TRUNCATE`), `ops.alert`, `ops.pause_switch` and the append-only `ops.switch_history`. `ops::api::audit(&mut *tx, &entry)` writes an audit row in the caller's transaction (JOB-005); the row holds actor and subject UUIDs and code names only (action, subject kind, statuses, field name), each a `&'static str` checked by `is_code_name` and by the table's `CHECK`s, so no contact value fits (PRIV-028). `ops::api::export_audit(after, limit)` is a paged export in `id` order after a cursor and `AuditRow::to_json` gives each row's JSON line (OPS-010). Identity IDs can commit out of order, so T017's off-database stream (OPS-011) must not read that cursor: it uses a per-row `core.task` or outbox row inserted in the audit row's transaction, or reads only below a safe horizon. `set_switch` changes an owner pause switch only when it differs and, in one transaction, logs actor and state in `ops.switch_history` and an `ops.audit_log` row (`switch_on`/`switch_off`, subject the `ops.pause_switch` UUID; SEC-006); a switch never set is off (OPS-003). `ops.alert` and `ops.pause_switch` have UUID IDs. In this crate the queries sit in `src/service.rs`, since every function is one statement with no orchestration.

**What T017 built (D-734, D-735).** The off-database stream (OPS-010, OPS-011, PRIV-029). `ops::api::stream_event` enqueues an `ops.offdb_stream` `core.task` in the writing transaction; `ops::api::audit` and `ops::api::set_switch` call it for every audit row, so the copy commits or rolls back with the row and no cursor is read (D-729). The `log_request` middleware in `build_router(pool)` hands one line per request (request ID, route template or `unmatched`, status, time; never the path) to `RequestLog`, a bounded channel whose background writer queues it, so no response waits on the database (overflow is dropped and counted, D-737), and an AI reading is queued with its enumerated fields only, each a code name. `app/server/src/offdb_stream.rs` registers the writer, which puts `audit/<id>.json` (the row's line, the previous row's ID and the sha256 of its line), `request/<date>/<task id>.json` and `ai_reading/<id>.json` through the storage adapter with a derived idempotency key. The writer finds the previous row with one `max(id) < n` lookup (`ops::api::previous_audit_id`). An audit row whose lower neighbour is missing waits up to 2 minutes for it (identity IDs commit out of order) before linking past the gap. The nightly `ops.offdb_verify` task (03:00 Arizona, one per date; the worker at start and each run before its work queue today's and tomorrow's, D-737) walks the audit rows older than an hour against their objects, on the local stand-in removes request days older than 30, and last raises a High `ops.audit_chain_broken` alert on the first mismatch (known limit: it walks the whole chain each night; a verified watermark is a follow-up, D-735); in Phase 2 that purge is the bucket's lifecycle rule and the check reads with its own read key, never the app's write-only, prefix-scoped key (T134, T109). Settings: `ASKHAVA_STORAGE_BASE_URL` (a `file://` directory in Phase 1) and `ASKHAVA_ID_SECRET`; the worker refuses to start without them.

**What T018 built (D-820 to D-822).** `adapters::api::verify_webhook(vendor, secret, Delivery { url, body, headers }, now)` checks every inbound webhook on its raw body before any module sees it (SEC-016, SEC-039, SEC-051): Twilio base64 HMAC-SHA1 under the auth token over the full URL and the sorted form fields; Stripe hex HMAC-SHA256 over `<t>.<body>` against any `v1` of `Stripe-Signature`; Resend's Svix base64 HMAC-SHA256 under the decoded `whsec_` key over `<id>.<timestamp>.<body>`; Lob hex HMAC-SHA256 over `<timestamp>.<body>`. Every MAC is compared in constant time and every timestamp must be within 300 seconds of now, either way. Only it builds a `VerifiedWebhook` (private fields, `Debug` without the body), whose `event_key()` (Stripe and Lob body `id`, `svix-id`; none for Twilio, whose module keys on its pair) the module records through `core::api::Dedupe` (SEC-017). A refusal goes through `refuse(vendor, error)`, which writes one `webhook refused` warn line with the vendor and reason codes and returns `REJECTED_STATUS` (403) for the route to answer. `check_webhook_source(mode, client, stand_in_host)`, the Stripe route's check, takes the client address as SEC-040 reads it and allows, in sandbox and live, only addresses in `app/adapters/data/stripe_webhook_ips.json` (Stripe's `ips_webhooks.json` verbatim, compiled in with `include_str!`, never fetched), and in stand-in mode only the stand-in's host (SEC-049). Tests: `tests/security/tests/webhook_forgery.rs`, `replay.rs`, `idempotency_keys.rs`.

**What T014 built (D-815 to D-817, D-819).** Derived IDs, link tokens and webhook dedupe for replay (CARD-017, PICK-028, SEC-017, SEC-037, SEC-039). `core::api::IdSecret` is the versioned `ID_SECRET` (32 bytes or more, version 1 or above; `Debug` shows the version only); `card_id`, `quote_id` and `pick_id` (all `derive_id` over an `IdKind`) are the first 128 bits of HMAC-SHA-256 over the version, the kind and the record IDs, each field length-prefixed. `link_token` is the full HMAC over a `LinkKind`, the record ID and a `LinkSalt` (16 bytes from the OS source), 64 hex digits; `LinkToken` has no `Display`, a redacted `Debug` and equality by SHA-256 (no character-by-character compare), `LinkSalt` a redacted `Debug`, and only the token's SHA-256 is stored. Derived IDs are pinned to the secret version in force when they were made: a rotation never re-derives a stored ID, and replay derives each record's ID with the version in force at its original time (D-819). `core::api::Dedupe` (`First`, `Repeat`, read from `fetch_optional` by `from_returning`) and `EventKey` (one vendor ID, or a Twilio pair joined by `:`) are the dedupe helper; each module writes its own `INSERT INTO <module>.webhook_event (source, event_key, received_at) … ON CONFLICT (source, event_key) DO NOTHING RETURNING event_key` in the transaction that applies the event (D-491). `migrations/0032_jobs_quote_tokens.sql`: `jobs.quote_token` (salt, key version, token hash unique, issued and revoked times, one live row per job); `jobs::api::quote_token::{issue, revoke, resolve}` re-issue by revoking the live row and inserting a new salt, each audited; a revoke is stamped no earlier than the row's issue time (clock skew), and an issue that loses the one-live-token race answers `TokenError::Busy`. Known gap: the salt reaches the off-database stream only once `ops` has a stream event that can carry it (D-817).

**What T179 built (D-851, D-852).** Job numbers and the status-enum scan (JOB-024, SEC-031). `migrations/0034_jobs_numbers.sql`: `jobs.job_number` (number from 1001, primary key; `job_id` unique; append-only for the app role) and the one-row counter `jobs.job_number_next`. `jobs::api::create` takes the next number in the job's own transaction (`service::issue_number`; the counter's row lock orders concurrent creates, and an already issued number fails the create rather than being reused); `jobs::api::job_number(db, id)` reads it. A restore rolls the counter back, so the replay (T110) calls `jobs::api::raise_number_floor(db, highest_texted)` with the highest job number in the vendors' sent-message records before the app takes new jobs; the counter only moves up. `tests/integration/tests/job_numbers.rs` proves it, a simulated restore included (TD-JOB-06). `tests/security/tests/status_enums.rs` runs `scripts/check_rust_rules.py` over the repository and over planted `_ =>`, `matches!` and `if let` shapes, and checks that every `status.rs` denies `clippy::wildcard_enum_match_arm` and that no first-party struct keeps a status as text (TD-SEC-12).

**What T034 built (D-856 to D-860).** The `licensing` crate (`app/licensing`, layer 3) with its first rules, all pure (LIC-011 to LIC-016, LIC-028 to LIC-031, LIC-047). `licensing::api::eligibility(&Job, &Holder)` says whether a business may receive a job's card on its licenses alone: a no-license job type only to a business matched on the city list (`HandymanCard` in the handyman category), an ROC job type to a business whose classes cover it (`Holder::roc_covers`, filled from the license map by T153) or, for a listed job type of a handyman-allowed category, to a handyman on the city list as a `HandymanCard` (under $1,000, no permit; the price and permit checks are T035's), a refused job type (gas pool heater, a new outlet, gas appliance work, a vent reroute outside the handyman category) never to a handyman or no-license business, an agency job type only to that agency's licensees, alarms needing the Board certificate and ROC cover, and pest only through the sign-up attestation (`PestAttestation`), with termites and pre-emergent weed control needing the attested record category and a homeowner's report pausing it. `licensing::api::city_list_standing` matches a business on one city-list row by business, trade or owner name, else `Pending` (D-177); `licensing::api::inspection_fee` makes a sale-question yes or a fee-free job type of a `home_inspection` job `FeeFree` and every other category `Normal` (FEE-045); a handyman-category job reaches only a business signed up as a handyman (`NotHandyman` otherwise). `migrations/0048_licensing_agency_rules.sql` creates the `licensing` schema and `licensing.business_license` (one row per other-agency license, pest rows `self_attested` with a letters-digits-dash number of at most 20, agency-record numbers as the agency shows them, D-859). Towns and ticked job types (CARD-001) stay `quotes`' to combine. `app/licensing/src/tests/prop_rules.rs` holds the property tests of these rules (D-860).

**What T035 built (D-897, D-898).** The handyman limits in `app/licensing/src/handyman.rs`, all pure (AI-012, LIC-019 to LIC-025, LIC-036). `licensing::api::eligibility_for_request(&Job, &Holder, &JobRequest)` is `eligibility` except that a `HandymanCard` becomes `NoCard(RefusedJobType)` when the scrubbed request text or any candidate job type holds a word of the gas list `docs/catalog/gas_keywords.yaml` (compiled in; a list that does not parse blocks every handyman card), or a candidate is a smoke-alarm, fire-sprinkler or permit-type job type (water heater replacement, panel upgrade, new circuit, re-roof, wall removal); the AI's handyman reading is never an input. `check_quote(&QuoteSubmission)` refuses a handyman quote without the certification box (`NotCertified`) and a construction-type quote from a handyman or in a no-license construction category whose top is $1,000.00 or more (`OverHandymanLimit`; a trip-fee quote has no top, D-035). `split_job(new, others, window_days)` blocks a quote when another handyman job of the same business at the same address (`quotes` selects them) lies less than the window away (30 days, `SPLIT_JOB_WINDOW_DAYS`, the caller passing the setting, D-027) and shares a job type or the two tops reach $1,000 (D-154). `HANDYMAN_CARD_LINE` is LIC-024's line. Amounts are cents; the words a business reads on a refusal are the portal's (T148).

**What T036 built (D-902 to D-906).** The daily list imports and the daily license check (CARD-015, LIC-006, LIC-007, LIC-010, LIC-027). `app/licensing/src/imports.rs` reads the ROC and city lists (fixed headers; a wrong header rejects the list, a malformed row is skipped and counted); `app/licensing/src/service.rs` stores them, each row with its source and list date, in one transaction per list and day (a second run records nothing). `licensing::api::check_license` records a business's daily check against the latest ROC list (`active`, `inactive`, `not_listed`), `roc_check_allows_card` lets an ROC-licensed card go only when the latest check of the covering license was `active` and within the caller's `Freshness` (pest is attested, LIC-048; other categories never call it); a header-only or mostly malformed list, or one under half the previous list's rows (D-905), is refused (`TooFewRows`), the check is per license number (D-906), license numbers are normalised and conflicting repeats keep the stricter status, `read_back` gives the classes and issue year from the state record for the sign-up read-back (the ROC's only, so LIC-027 is partial until T180), and `city_standing` runs the LIC-011 match on the latest city list. `migrations/0050_licensing_imports.sql` adds `licensing.list_import`, `licensing.agency_record`, `licensing.city_license_entry` and `licensing.license_check`, append-only for `askhava_app` and with no contact column (LIC-010). The download of the files and the worker schedule wait for a download adapter; `businesses` drives the per-business check.

**What T180 built (D-938 to D-942).** The live lookup and the failure clocks (LIC-007, LIC-008, LIC-009, LIC-039) in `app/licensing/src/handlers.rs`. `licensing::api::lookup_at_pick` makes exactly one `LicenseSource::lookup_license` call, never retried, and reads the answer with the ROC list reader (D-282): a failed call or a page that does not read as rows for that number alone is `LookupOutcome::Unknown`, records nothing and can't Confirm, so a misread never changes a verified status; a read answer is stored as a `licensing.license_check` row dated the Arizona day (D-938). `unknown_standing` keeps, warns (24 hours) and pauses (72 hours) a business from its first failed lookup; `end_date_pauses` pauses from 00:00 Arizona time on a license-end or bond-cancellation date; `list_failure_alerts` and `alert_list_failure` raise `licensing.list_failing` at 12 hours and `licensing.list_still_failing` (the OPS-032 push item) at 24, both `normal` so a failing list never starts OPS-001 safe mode, from the caller's failure start and previous run (D-939, D-942); `lookup_while_list_fails` runs one lookup per active business while a list fails, at least `lookup_spacing` apart (the throttle). The limits are `FailureLimits` (defaults 24, 72, 12, 24 hours and 2 seconds) filled by the caller (D-940). No table is added: `businesses` holds the pause, the failure start and the daily count of cleared pauses, and the worker wiring waits for the download schedule (D-941).

**What T037 built (D-948).** The quote-card license lines in `app/licensing/src/lines.rs`, pure, with the words in `app/licensing/templates/license_lines.json` (English and Spanish, plus each agency's link) (LIC-026, LIC-032, LIC-034, LIC-037, LIC-038, WORD-006, WORD-016). `licensing::api::licensed_lines(&LicenseFacts, CardLanguage)` gives a licensed card's "showed Active ... when we checked on <date>. Check it yourself." line with the agency's link, then "<Agency> license since <year>" only when the state record has an original issue year; a pest-management license gets no lines (its LIC-047 line is not a check). `no_license_lines(&NoLicenseFacts, CardLanguage)` gives the LIC-024 line first for a handyman, then the city-license line (with the year only when the city list has a date), then the business-reported insurance line when the business recorded an answer. No function takes or returns a rating, review count, hire count or ranking. The "About us" line (BIZ-022) and the card order are T081's and T052's.

**What T153 built (D-983 to D-985).** The license class map in `app/licensing/src/class_map.rs`, pure (LIC-035, LIC-043 to LIC-046). `licensing::api::ClassMap::shipped()` loads the compiled-in `docs/catalog/license_map.yaml` and `docs/catalog/categories.yaml` once (a map that fails to load covers nothing) and resolves each class: `all` gives the category's listed job types, `also` adds refused ones, `only` names them; a subclassification row keeps only job types whose `scope_tags` meet its `scope`; `includes` adds the included class's job types; a row under `unclear` is removed from its class and so never inherited; a `no_structural` class (R-62) loses `STRUCTURAL_JOB_TYPES` (D-983). Class names compare upper case with single spaces. `ClassMap::covers(classes, category, job_type)` is the value `quotes` puts in `Holder::roc_covers`; `sign_up(classes)` returns `CommercialOnly` when every class is commercial-only (`C-` is a prefix), `NothingMapped` when they reach no home job type (D-984) and otherwise `Activate` with the job types for the checklist; `check_project_limit` refuses a quote whose top is above the R-61 or R-62 limit when every qualifying class has one (D-985); `opening(category)` gives `OpenNow`, `ComingLater` or `Off` from `enabled` and `launch_open`, and `request_intake(opening, active, min_active)` turns a launch-open category with too few active businesses into INT-017's case. The load also refuses a repeated class, a mapped commercial-only class, an unknown category, job type or include, an include cycle and a launch-open category that is not enabled. The LIC-044 words and the limit explanation are the portal's; the settings log of a flag change is `ops`'.

**What T044 built (D-1013 to D-1015).** The `quotes` crate (`app/quotes`, layer 5) with its matching, CARD-001 to CARD-003 and LIC-035. `quotes::api::rules::card_for(&ClassMap, &CardJob, &CardBusiness)` gives `Card`, `HandymanCard` or `Skip(reason)`: the business must be active (`businesses::api::AccountStatus::receives_cards`), verified, serve one of the job's Arizona offer towns (the job's own town or a neighbouring one, CARD-002, INT-022), have ticked at least one candidate job type, and have every candidate be a card under `licensing::api::eligibility_for_request`, with `roc_covers` filled per job type from `ClassMap::covers` (LIC-043, LIC-035). `rules::recipients` keeps every qualifying business with no cap (CARD-003). `quotes::api::send_cards` records one `quotes.job_card` row per recipient (`migrations/0067_quotes_matching.sql`; unique per job and business, safe to run twice) and `card_holders` reads them. The caller reads towns, ticks and licenses through the lower crates' `api`; the BIZ-049 skips are T155's (D-492).

**What T184 built (D-1018 to D-1022).** Overflow, pauses, late cards and the coverage line. `quotes::api::rules::match_job(&ClassMap, &CardJob, &Pool)` runs the CARD-001 match under the owner pauses (`OwnerPauses`: the "all" switch, paused category and town codes the caller fills through `ops::api::is_paused`; the job's own town paused, or every offer town a business serves, skips it: `Skip::OwnerPaused`, CARD-016) and, when it reaches nobody, sends to every `Pooled` business with "jobs nobody else covers" on that passes every check but the ticks (CARD-004); a rerun uses `match_job_held`, where a card holder whose checklist covers the job keeps overflow off. `send_job_cards` records the new recipients; `send_late_cards` does the same only while `jobs::api::current` is Open or No quotes yet (`JobStatus::takes_late_cards`), each late card sent now with its own 24 hours (CARD-018, D-042); the event handler that calls it when a business becomes eligible is the worker wiring's. `quotes::api::coverage` gives `Coverage::Send(n)`, `Split` or `NotCovered` from the same match; `intake::api::tell_coverage` queues the INT-017 count line (`coverage_count_many`, `coverage_count_one`), the INT-032 `split_offer`, or moves the told job to Not covered with `Why::NobodyCovers` and a demand row (the whole request only when no other job of it is still live); `split_request` opens the second trade's job on SPLIT or DIVIDIR.

**What T045 built (D-1024 to D-1026).** The anonymous job card and the final plain-code gate, both pure (CARD-005 to CARD-009, PRIV-006). `quotes::api::build_card(&CardParts, min_words)` returns a `JobCard` whose private fields are the town, the kind of place (`Place`: house, marina, storage lot, ramp), the screened card-photo IDs and the card text; no field can hold a name, phone, email, street address, on-site contact or original photo. The text is the approved description, then one line per card-field answer, "How soon: <answer>" (`Timing`, "Needed by <day>" for answer 4), "911 text sent: <gas / CO alarm / smoke or fire / electrical / power line>" (`Danger`, never her words) and any concept-image label, every line through `quotes::api::final_gate` and the whole text through it again last, so the stored text is what businesses get (TD-CARD-11). The gate cuts each sentence at its first contact-shaped word: a name after "ask for", "talk to" or "I'm", a title before a capitalised word, a contact phrase, an email or link, a spelled email, a street number before a street word, three number words in a row or 7 or more digits however spaced. A gated description with fewer than `min_words` words (the caller's setting, `DEFAULT_MIN_WORDS` 5) becomes "<Category> job (details on the job page)". The CARD-010 restricted-word removal and the card notice itself are later tasks'.

**What T185 built (D-1031 to D-1033).** The card's restricted-word removal and its sent lines (CARD-010, CARD-012, CARD-019, FEE-045). Each pass of `quotes::api::final_gate`, after the contact cut (so a domain holding a listed word, "joes.beer.biz", is cut whole before the list could split it), removes, line by line, every phrase on `catalog::api::RESTRICTED_WORDS` as whole words in any case (longest phrase first, so "neo-Nazi" is one removal), drops the punctuation and any hyphen-ended fragment ("e-") a removal leaves alone and logs `entry=<list entry>` at info for each removal, never the text around it (D-485, D-492); a line with no listed phrase comes back unchanged. `JobCard::text_for(&CardLines, speaks_spanish)` is the text one business gets: the stored card text, then "Homeowner prefers Spanish." only when `CardLines::prefers_spanish` and the business ticked "We speak Spanish", "No introduction fee for this job." when `CardLines::fee_free` (the caller's `catalog::api::JobType::fee_free`) and "Sent to <N> businesses." ("Sent to 1 business." for one) with N the provider-accepted count the card-notice sender passes (T147). Language is no matching input (`CardJob` and `CardBusiness` have no language field), so no business can skip a job by language.

**What T041 built (D-953 to D-955).** The `businesses` crate (`app/businesses`, layer 4) and its first records (BIZ-004 to BIZ-009). `migrations/0055_businesses_records.sql` adds the `businesses` schema with `businesses.business` (status, pause reason exactly when paused, the overflow switch, the Spanish tick, the business-reported insurance answer, NULL until given and with no verified, expiry or pause column, and the after-hours tick; no name, phone, email or address; no `DELETE` for `askhava_app`), `businesses.town` and `businesses.job_type_choice` (category code and job type name). `src/status.rs` holds `AccountStatus` (pending, active, paused, waitlist, needs review, removed) and `PauseReason`, and `next` is the only place a status moves: a sign-up check with the code and the match and no flag activates, a flag sends it to needs review, where only Casey's approval with the code and the match activates (BIZ-003, BIZ-016), waitlist opens to pending, only an active business pauses and only a paused one resumes, removed is final. `businesses::api::apply` runs that move fenced on the status it read (JOB-004) with its audit row in one transaction; `set_ticks`, `set_towns` and `set_job_types` audit by field name only; `set_job_types` accepts only job types in the caller's `allowed` set, built from the license map and the no-license job types (BIZ-004, BIZ-049, LIC-043). `night_count(category, town)` counts active businesses with the after-hours tick serving that town with a ticked job type in that category, for INT-033; T150 narrows it with the per-person switch and test-email Yes (D-491). Pause rules, quiet times and the remaining tables of section 3 come with T182, T220 and the sign-up tasks.

**What T046 built (D-1048 to D-1052).** Quote intake (PICK-001, PICK-002, PICK-007, FEE-043, FEE-044) and the every-window-ended trigger (TXT-042, JOB-025). `quotes::api::parse_quote(QuoteForm, &QuoteRules)` returns a checked `Quote` or a `Bounce`: the notes, original wording and tier names bounce when `final_gate` would cut a word (one contact rule for cards and quotes) or hold a carrier-restricted phrase; the price must use the job's format, an hourly quote with hours being a range and without hours a site visit only in a `diagnostic_visit` job; the handyman limit is `licensing::api::check_quote` on the top, never a setup charge; no fee is computed here (T148 through `payments::api`). `submit_quote` stores it in `quotes.quote` (`migrations/0070_quotes_quote.sql`, one row per business per job, a resubmission an edit, no expiry column) only while `window_open(sent_at, now)` (24 hours from the business's own `quotes.job_card.sent_at`) and `JobStatus::takes_quotes` (Open to Awaiting confirm); an edit is refused once the job has had a pick (`JobStatus::takes_quote_edits`, or any quote marked unavailable), a resubmission after a withdrawal clears it; an AI-read quote waits for `confirm_quote` (T148's read-back, refused once the job takes no quotes), and `shown_quotes` lists only confirmed, unwithdrawn ones. `mark_unavailable` is `picks`' mark on a failed pick's quote (`quotes.quote.unavailable_at`), which leaves it out of `choosable`. Each accepted quote enqueues a `quotes.windows_end` task for the last card's window end; `run_windows_end` (registered in `register_handlers()`) re-schedules for a later card's end or passes the quoted, sent and choosable counts to `jobs::api::all_windows_ended`. The first-quote move to Quotes ready (JOB-009), the PICK-040 fact columns and the withdraw action are later tasks'.

**What T186 built (D-1111 to D-1113).** The quote card (PICK-003 to PICK-005, PICK-040, PICK-041, PICK-044) in `app/quotes/src/quote_card.rs`. `QuoteCard::lines(language)` is pure and takes no viewer, so the business's preview and the homeowner's card are the same lines (PICK-005): the public name, the license lines `licensing::api::licensed_lines` gives (passed in), "Ask Hava doesn't rank businesses; check it yourself.", "Independent business, not affiliated with Ask Hava." (WORD-014), "Gets emergency requests at night" for a business with the BIZ-007 tick, the six PICK-040 facts in fixed order, the format's definition (and "Free estimate" for no trip fee), then the business's own words, in English or Spanish. `quotes::api::quote_card(db, (job, business), CardSource)` reads a shown quote's format, original wording and earliest visit (`models::stored_card`) and the after-hours tick (`businesses::api::business`); the name, license lines and the other facts come from the caller until T148 stores the facts and T165 the links. No AI summary is stored with a quote; `check_summary` gives one only when `SummarySetting::On` (which only `switch_on` returns, given a `D-<n>` decision and a published policy holding the AI-018 sentence) and every word and number of it is in the original wording (PICK-004). The card's place on the page and the page lines are T052's.

**What T047 built (D-1190 to D-1194).** The homeowner's quote texts in `app/quotes/src/notify.rs`, which build words and decide when a text is due but send nothing (TXT-016, TXT-017, TXT-031 to TXT-033, WORD-011). `quotes::api::notice(quoted, previous, now)` gives `First`, `Update`, `Held { until }` or `Nothing` with the 3-hour gap (`UPDATE_GAP`) counted from the previous quote text's delivery, which `intake` reads from `messaging` and passes as `Sent`; `first_quote_text`, `update_text`, `list_texts` (pickable quotes in arrival order, 10 to a text, with the "2 YES" ending on each, amounts and periods exactly as stored from `models::list_rows`), `which_job_text` and `cards_out_text` render the `messaging` templates (`first_quote`, `quote_update`, `quote_list`, `quote_list_end`, `quote_list_which`, `request_out` and `request_out_held`, `request_out_today`, `request_out_slow`) in English and Spanish, and `arizona_time` writes every time as Arizona time with the recipient's zone clock beside it when it differs. T048 routes LIST to `list_texts` (`intake::api::answer_list`); the `jobs.homeowner_text` handler D-1030 gave T048 is not built and needs an owning task (D-1198, D-1199).

**What T048 built (D-1195 to D-1198).** The no-reply wait, the LIST reply and the call-back clock in `app/intake/src/handlers.rs` (INT-019, INT-021, INT-023, TXT-031). `begin_job` queues two `intake.no_reply` tasks for every new job, counted from `jobs`' `timer_from`: the reminder at 24 hours (moved to 8 a.m. Arizona by `texting_window` when it falls in quiet hours) and the close at 72 hours; `hear` restarts each Intake job's wait at the reply (`jobs::api::restart_timer`, D-807) and queues a fresh pair, so a step whose `from` no longer equals `timer_from` does nothing. The close moves the job Intake to Closed early at the 72-hour mark, or finds that `jobs`' own Intake timer did (status Closed early entered exactly then), closes the request once every job of it is final, and queues the `intake_closed_no_reply` text, or a `tell` step at 8 a.m. when the close fell at night. Texts go only with a consent record and once per job (outbox key over template, contact and job). `server` registers the handler with the `ID_SECRET` the off-database stream already reads. `answer_list` answers LIST or LISTA (optionally with a request number) with `quotes::api::list_texts` for the request's one quoted job, or `which_job_text` when several have quotes; the businesses' public names come from the caller. Every step re-checks at run time: a step run before its due time does nothing, and a text step run outside 8 a.m. to 8 p.m. Arizona queues itself again for the next 8 a.m. (D-1199). `queue_call_back` raises INT-023's call-back alert to Casey (`ops` alert `intake.call_back_urgent`, an OPS-032 push item, due within 24 hours by `counts_urgent`, or `intake.call_back`, due in the setting's days by `call_back_due`), once per request; its callers, the failed-text path (T094) and web chat (T103), are not built yet. Intake has no `askhava-ai` dependency, so INT-021's AI-off path is every step today. Not built here: the `jobs.homeowner_text` sender (D-1198, D-1199), now T240's (D-1215: `app/intake/src/job_texts.rs`, with both-zone quiet hours from a new `messaging::api::send_window` in `app/messaging/src/send_window.rs`, registered in `app/server/src/lib.rs`); the area-code half of quiet hours for the reminder and the close (D-1196, still unowned); and the conversation dispatcher that calls `answer_list` and `hear` on an open request (D-999), now T241's together with the Twilio inbound-text and status routes nothing had wired (D-861, D-1215: `app/server/src/inbound_text.rs` and `intake::api::receive_reply` in `app/intake/src/replies.rs`); until T241 merges, no inbound text reaches them and a reply does not restart the wait.

**What T241 built (D-1307 to D-1311).** `intake::api::receive_reply` (`app/intake/src/replies.rs`) is the dispatcher of a homeowner text inside an open request (D-999, D-1199 (4)): in one transaction, LIST or LISTA (alone or with a request number) answered from a quoted job gives `answer_list`'s texts; a LIST with no quoted job or whose list cannot be built (`IntakeError::Quotes`, D-1310), or any other text that is not an opt-out or HELP, is heard (`hear` restarts each Intake job's 72-hour wait at the reply, INT-019) and then goes to `receive_text`, so consent, the 911 screen, CLOSE, cancel and `<n> YES` keep their paths. With no open request it is `receive_text` alone. `server`'s inbound-text route (`app/server/src/inbound_text.rs`) calls it last; the route passes no business names yet (`businesses` holds none), so a LIST with a quoted job gets no list and is handled as any other reply until a names source exists (D-1308, D-1310); a module error that stops a text is logged at warn with its variant name only (D-1311). Still open: INT-032's "That's 2 jobs" wiring (D-999), `intake::api::on_opt_out` on an opt-out (D-1229), and sending the queued rows (the outbox sender).

**What T240 built (D-1301 to D-1303, D-1305, D-1306).** The `jobs.homeowner_text` sender in `app/intake/src/job_texts.rs` and the two-zone send window in `app/messaging/src/send_window.rs` (JOB-010 to JOB-012, JOB-025, TXT-040, TXT-042, QH-001, QH-006, QH-007, SEC-037, CON-021, CON-028). `intake::api::job_text_step(db, keys, text, now)` skips a job whose version moved on (`jobs::api::text_due`; a close text carries a final status's version, so it still goes, QH-007), finds the newest request that opened the job (the `job.<trade>.<property>` answer or the request's first job, open or closed, D-993) and its homeowner, skips one with no consent record (`may_text`, CON-021), then asks `messaging::api::send_window(db, vault, contact, now)`, which reads her phone under `messaging`'s send grant, answers `OptedOut` for a listed number (CON-028), `NotTextable` outside the area-code table (QH-003) and otherwise `rules::decide`'s answer for a `Kind::Update` (`Now` or `Hold { until }`). A held event is queued again at `until` (dedupe key `jobs.homeowner_text.<job>.<template>.<until in microseconds>`). Inside the window it renders the template in the request's language with `{job}` and, for `all_quotes_in`, `{quoted}`, `{count}` and `{link}` (`https://askhava.com/q/<token>`, `jobs::api::quote_token::issue` at send time) and queues one outbox row (subject: the job) in one transaction with the token; a run that finds the row already queued rolls its new token back. `server::job_text_keys` builds `intake::api::JobTextKeys` (the `ID_SECRET` as link key version 1 and outbox key, and a `Vault` from `VAULT_KEY` and `VAULT_INDEX_KEY`); the worker passes it to `register_handlers(stream, texts)`, which registers the handler only when the keys are set (required in the test and live projects, D-1302; one key without the other stops start-up in every environment, D-1306). The phone read in `send_window` is audited as `Purpose::Send` even when the answer is a hold or a skip (D-1305). The INT-019 reminder and close keep T048's Arizona-only `texting_window` (D-1215).

**What T049 built (D-1200 to D-1203).** Closing at the homeowner's word in `app/intake/src/closing.rs` (JOB-018 to JOB-023). `rules::close_reading` reads a text as a close ("CLOSE 1042", "CERRAR 1042", "CLOSE ALL", or, while a "Close which?" waits, a lone open request number or "all"), cancel intent (a whole clause, filler trimmed, from a fixed list or a need phrase with nothing or an allowed object after it; "ya no" only alone; D-1203), a mere mention of cancelling, or nothing. `answer_close` works on the homeowner's open request in one transaction under the homeowner lock: a close moves each named job to Cancelled from any status before Done through `jobs::api::move_to` as the homeowner, closes the request once every job is final and texts `job_closed` or `jobs_closed`; intent closes the only open job, or with several sends `close_which` (numbers plus CLOSE ALL) and stores `close_which = asked` in `intake.intake_answer`, closing nothing until answered; a mention gets `close_mention`, JOB-023's exact line, naming the open number written in it, else the newest. Opt-outs and HELP change no job (D-005). `close_from_page` is the quote page's button: the same move, no text. Not wired yet: the inbound dispatcher (D-999) and the page route must call them.

**What T256 built (D-2039 to D-2043).** Recovery of a racing first text in `app/server/src/inbound_text.rs` (TXT-012, PRIV-001, D-1751): when two texts with different MessageSids from a phone the vault does not know both find no contact, the loser's `privacy::api::store_contact` answers `PhoneTaken` after its savepoint rolled back its contact row; the route looks the phone up again in the same delivery transaction and continues with the winner's contact, so both texts reach `messaging` and `intake`; if none is found it answers 500 and the whole delivery, inbox row included, rolls back, so Twilio's resend is handled rather than stopped as a repeat. D-1715's inbound wiring: an opt-out from a `sender_business` phone calls `businesses::api::phone_stopped` (one owner email task per inbox row) in the same transaction, and a business phone's other text queues `business_auto_reply` through the outbox when `auto_reply_due` holds (subject: the inbox row). Tests: `tests/integration/tests/inbound_first_contact.rs` (TD-TXT-32, TD-BIZ-39). Open (D-2041): a repeat STOP with a new MessageSid queues a second owner email; the route does not call `admit_new_request` (D-1951) or `intake::api::on_opt_out` (D-1229). Open (D-2042): the auto reply's outbox recipient is the business ID, not the line ID `may_text` takes, until `messaging` returns it; auto replies have no limit (spec gap).
