**What T317 built (D-3084 to D-3087).** SEC-014's new-request limit on the served inbound text route and TXT-031's LIST names (D-2206, D-3083), closing D-2041 (2) and D-1951. `core::api::rate_limit_hit_on` runs the counter upsert on the caller's connection; `intake::api::admit_new_request` takes any `Acquire` and counts per phone, per IP, then everyone last, with the alert marker and the one Normal alert, in one transaction on one connection (a savepoint inside the caller's). The route reads `intake::api::open_request_jobs`; with no open request (and not a START that just took her number off the opt-out list, nor a 911-list text, which is never counted or refused so INT-002's safety text always runs, D-3087) it counts in the delivery's transaction as the last step before `receive_reply`, keyed by her vault contact, so a rolled-back delivery counts nothing and a refused one (200, inbox row kept) is a repeat when resent; with an open request a LIST text's names come from `quotes::api::quoted_prices` and `businesses::api::public_names` at the time of the text. `Webhooks::names` is gone; `Webhooks::environment` waits for T299's AI gate. Tests: `tests/integration/tests/inbound_text_guards.rs` (TD-SEC-52).

**What T258 built (D-2066 to D-2070).** A refund recorded as Stripe reports it (FEE-026, FEE-028, FEE-029, FEE-030; audit A03, D-1753). `app/adapters/src/payments.rs`: `StripeRefund` and two provided `PaymentsAdapter` methods, `send_refund` (POST `/v1/refunds`) and `read_refund` (GET `/v1/refunds/{re_…}`), which `StripePayments` answers from Stripe; their provided bodies serve only the test fakes written before (`create_refund`'s `re_…` as `succeeded`; no read), and go with `create_refund` in T132. `app/payments/src/refund_stripe.rs`: `RefundState` (`Pending`, `RequiresAction`, `Succeeded`, `Failed`, `Canceled`), `Refund` (`checked`: an unknown status is `Decode`, never a success; the refund must name the PaymentIntent asked for) and `RefundStripe` (`refund`, `fetch_refund`), provided for every `PaymentsAdapter` so `picks`' T191 callers compile unchanged (D-2066). `refunds.rs`: `succeeded` records the refund and its ledger entry; `pending`/`requires_action` store the `re_…` as `pending` with no entry and queue `REFUND_CHECK_KIND` (`run_refund_check`, every `REFUND_CHECK_EVERY`, 30 minutes); `failed`/`canceled` take the FEE-026 failure path; `refund_succeeded`, `refund_failed`, `refund_canceled` (T259's webhook) move a `pending` row on; a failure while the pick's ledger holds an unreversed `refund` entry (after `succeeded`, or the reconcile's while `pending`) appends one `refund-reversal` (`LedgerEntry::reversal`, key `refund-reversal.<pick>.<re>`) and the retry's later success a `refund` keyed `refund.<pick>.<re>`; every refund-row writer holds the pick's `pick_intent` lock. A pending refund answers `RefundOutcome::Failed` (not refunded yet), so no "refunded" notice goes out before `succeeded` (D-2067). `reconcile.rs`: the own refund appends only on `succeeded`; reversals count against refunds in `ledger_facts`, the FEE-030 match, the money view and statements (as negative `Refund` lines). `migrations/0124_payments_refunds_and_events.sql`: refund status `pending`, `canceled`; ledger kind `refund-reversal`; `payments.webhook_event` (event ID, type, received time; SELECT and INSERT only) for T259. Not yet (D-2068, D-2069): `EntryKind::RefundReversal` (blocked by `portals`' exhaustive statement match), `RefundOutcome::Pending` (blocked by `picks`' match), `server` registration of `REFUND_CHECK_KIND` (T132). Tests: `tests/integration/tests/refund_lifecycle.rs` (TD-FEE-42).

**What T259 built (D-2071, D-2072).** The Stripe webhook dispatch (SEC-017, FEE-026, FEE-027, FEE-052; audit A08, D-1754). `app/payments/src/routes.rs` parses a checked event once (mode, account, `evt_…` ID, type, object) and acknowledges any it does not act on before a database read; an event ID already in `payments.webhook_event` answers 200. `payment_intent.succeeded` goes through `paid_release` to the `ReleaseSink` as T224 built it; `refund.updated` and `charge.refund.updated` go by the refund's status (a `succeeded` one re-read through `RefundStripe::fetch_refund` first) and `refund.failed` always to `refund_succeeded`, `refund_failed` or `refund_canceled`; `charge.dispute.created` to `record_dispute`; the event row is written right after those handlers, each idempotent on its own. `charge.dispute.closed` (one `payments.dispute_closed` audit row and High alert per dispute) and `radar.early_fraud_warning.created` (one `picks.fraud_warning` task per pick, `FRAUD_WARNING_KIND`, run by T281) write the event row in their own transaction. A database error or an unreachable Stripe answers 500; an unknown object 200 (an unknown refund still writes its event row), but a closed dispute not yet recorded 500 so Stripe resends it; a handled event whose object lacks a needed field raises one High `payments.stripe_event_unreadable` alert per event ID (D-2073). T331 (D-2289, D-2944 to D-2946): a `payment_intent.succeeded` whose `metadata.pick_id` is missing or not a UUID is such an unreadable event (event row and alert, 200, nothing released; the nightly reconcile still matches the charge by its PaymentIntent); an early fraud warning whose PaymentIntent has a `payments.pick_intent` row but no `payments.charge_evidence` row answers 500 with no event row and raises one High `payments.fraud_warning` alert for the pick in its own transaction unless one is open, and one matching no `pick_intent` commits its event row with one High `payments.fraud_warning` alert and queues nothing. Tests: `tests/integration/tests/stripe_webhook_dispatch.rs` (TD-FEE-43, TD-FEE-53, TD-FEE-54).

**What T260 built (D-2076).** The Confirm page's Stripe calls outside tests (FEE-014, FEE-016, FEE-036, FEE-047; audit A08, D-1754). `stand_ins/stripe.py` and `stand_ins/server.py`: a PaymentIntent created with `confirm=false` is `requires_payment_method` with a `client_secret` and no charge; `POST /stripe/v1/payment_intents/{id}/confirm` (an Idempotency-Key required) succeeds, declines with 402 or answers `requires_action` for the 3-D Secure test card, which the stand-in-only `.../stand_in_authenticate` (`result=pass|fail`) settles with a signed event; `--port 0` reports the bound port. `app/adapters/src/payments.rs`: `StripePayments::create_unconfirmed_intent` and `confirm_payment_intent`, answering `UnconfirmedIntent` and `ConfirmedIntent` with the client secret as a `SecretString`. `app/payments/src/intent_http.rs`: `impl IntentStripe for StripePayments`. The Confirm route that uses it is T261's. Tests: `tests/contract/test_stripe_confirm.py`, `intent_http.rs` `tests` (TD-FEE-44).

**What T050 built (D-1228, D-1229).** An opt-out's live jobs in `app/intake/src/service.rs` (CON-012, CON-028, CON-029, JOB-020, D-005, D-129). `intake::api::on_opt_out` runs after `messaging::api::on_inbound_text` has listed the number and queued the bare CON-009 confirmation: for the homeowner's open request with any job before a final status it changes no status and queues no text, and in one transaction under the homeowner lock marks every live job paused by the homeowner on a sole-word CANCEL or CANCELAR (`sole_cancel`; `migrations/0076_intake_optout_jobs.sql`, `intake.homeowner_pause`, one open pause per job, no DELETE or TRUNCATE for `askhava_app`, plus one `intake.paused_by_homeowner` audit row only when a pause was inserted) and enqueues one `intake.opted_out_live_job` task per inbox row (payload: request, live jobs, paused, inbox ID). That task's handlers are later tasks': the still-open line by email (T094) and on the quote page (T052), withdrawing a waiting pick (T054); `paused_by_homeowner(job)` is the check the pick, Confirm, charge and release paths make (T054, T224). Not wired yet: the inbound dispatcher (D-999) must call `on_opt_out` on `Outcome::OptedOut`, and nothing yet ends a pause (the pick or close on the page or by email, D-1229).

**What T187 built (D-1264 to D-1266).** `app/portals/src/pick_flow.rs` and `templates/pick_code.html` (PICK-013, PICK-014, PICK-018, PICK-042, A11Y-010 to A11Y-013, A11Y-015, A11Y-016, A11Y-032): `POST /q/{token}/code` (`CODE_ROUTE`, in `pick_flow_routes`, behind the cross-site refusal, CSRF and `no-store`) renders the code-and-contact step: a call through the masked number or an email she shares (no call window), one `autocomplete="one-time-code"` `inputmode="numeric"` code field that takes paste, labels and hints before each input, and on a failed "Finish my pick" an "Error: " title, a focused `role="alert"` summary linking each field and every value kept; "Send a new code" is a second button in the same form, clearing only the code. The code check reuses `picks::api::rules::parse_code` (portals now depends on `askhava-picks`, layer 6 below layer 8). Verify, the vault contact read and the stored page pick are not wired yet, so the step says nothing was picked and no code was sent, and the final Pick still shows "Nothing was picked" (D-1264). Guarded by `tests/a11y/pick_forms.spec.ts` and `pick_flow::code_tests`.

**What T055 built (D-1248 to D-1252).** `app/portals/src/pick_flow.rs` and `templates/pick_review.html` (PICK-016, PICK-017, PICK-045, PICK-054, A11Y-014, A11Y-031): `pick_flow_routes` answers the quote card's Pick form (`POST /q/{token}`) with the review step (what happens next, "or reply <n> YES", "Go back" to the card, the final Pick with only "By picking, you agree to the Terms" beside it, no Confirm end time) and takes the final Pick at `POST /q/{token}/pick` (`PICK_ROUTE`), which re-checks the link and quote and shows the review again under "Nothing was picked" until T187 adds the code step and the stored pick (D-1248, D-1251); a pick is taken from the first quote through Awaiting confirm, where it replaces the waiting one (`takes_pick`, PICK-012, PICK-032); `result_page` is the page after a stored pick, its heading focused on load, with the acceptance end time when the caller passes it (PICK-045), the contact card and the after-Done line. Both routes are POST only, behind the cross-site refusal, `no-store` and `noindex`. Open: the emergency switch, the stored acceptance and a Spanish page (D-1249), and `is_cross_site` refusing a real browser's `Origin: null` (D-1250).

**What T213 built (D-1290 to D-1294).** The business job page's data in `app/quotes/src/job_page.rs` and the TXT-040 check in `app/quotes/src/update_texts.rs` (CARD-023, TXT-040). `quotes::api::job_page(db, (job, business), &PageParts)` returns a `JobPage` (the approved `JobCard`, every intake answer and her later details through `final_gate`, the sent and quote counts from `models::page_counts`) or `None` for a business with no card for the job; the caller brings the card and the answer wording, and the portals route renders it (T148, T212). `quotes::api::scan` holds the homeowner quote-update templates in `UPDATE_TEXTS` to 15 words a sentence where no requirement fixes it, 20 always, her page link (`https://askhava.com/q/<token>` once rendered) and no quote details outside the LIST reply; the other homeowner texts after intake are `Shape::Capped` (20 words only), and `NOT_UPDATE_TEXTS` names every homeowner template left out, so a test fails on one in neither list. The page's quote count leaves out withdrawn quotes; the TXT-042 count does not (D-1294).

**What T054 built (D-1243 to D-1247).** The `picks` crate (`app/picks`) and `migrations/0079_picks_code.sql`: `picks.pick` (one awaiting-confirm pick per job; channel `text` with its inbound text, or `page`; status from the eleven pick codes; `end_reason` `replaced` or `paused`), `picks.code_attempt` (one row per Verify code: job, vault contact ID, channel, tries, last try, verified; never the number), `picks.verify_cap_day` and the append-only `picks.terms_line` (job, outbox row, template version, time). `picks::api::send_code` and `check_code` are the page pick's 6-digit Verify code (PICK-013, PICK-014, PICK-055): refused at exactly 10 minutes, 5 tries counted before Verify is asked, a 15-minute cooldown in which no try reaches Verify and no new code goes, a voice code only on the homeowner's tap and never 9 p.m. to 8 a.m. Arizona from another session, 5 codes and 3 calls per number and 10 per job per Arizona day, and at the caller's global threshold nothing more with one `picks.verify_daily_cap` High alert a day (D-1244); Verify is called after the short transaction commits, keyed on the send's row. The destination number comes from the caller (the homeowner's own session, `portals`, T055), since `picks` may read contact details only for a release. `intake::api::answer_text_pick` hands a "<number> YES" text (or "<request> <number> YES") to `picks::api::text_pick` under the homeowner lock with each live job's CON-029 pause: one job in Quotes ready or Awaiting confirm picks, more ask which, none gets the status reply; no pick before a text ending with the Terms line was recorded for the job (the first YES gets that line and "Reply <n> YES again to pick."); a second YES for another quote cancels the waiting pick and enqueues `picks.pick_withdrawn` for its business; each pick enqueues `picks.pick_made` and intake stores one terms acceptance with channel `text_pick` (PICK-015, PICK-016, PICK-031, PICK-032, PICK-036, D-1246). On a CANCEL pause `intake::on_opt_out` calls `picks::api::withdraw_paused` in its transaction: the waiting pick is cancelled and its business's notice enqueued, the job's status and timers untouched (CON-029, D-1245). Not wired yet: the inbound dispatcher (D-999), the page route (T055), handlers for the two `picks` events, and the LIST text's Terms-line record (D-1247).

**What T208 built (D-1274 to D-1279).** The homeowner's card approval step (INT-041, CARD-007) in `app/intake/src/summary_step.rs`. `intake::api::show_card(db, secret, &Preview)` takes the gated `quotes::api::JobCard` the businesses will get, her page link and the wait (default `AUTO_SEND_AFTER`, 30 minutes, passed in like CARD-009's word count), and in one transaction under the homeowner lock texts `card_shown` (the card in its `[card]` slot) when the whole text fits 2 segments by `messaging::api::count_segments`, else `card_link`, and queues one `intake.card_timer` task that watches the text's `messaging::api::delivery_status` each `RECHECK_AFTER` (1 minute) until it is sent and then queues the end of the wait from there, so the wait never starts before she has the card (a failed text stops the timer, D-1278); every call is a new show, its own text and timer, even of the same card; a job whose `emergency_choice` is `emergency` gets `Shown::Emergency` (no step) and a request with no consent record `Shown::NotAllowed`. `answer_card` reads SEND, OK, YES or ENVIAR (the cards go now) and EDIT or CAMBIAR (the `card_edit` question; the caller builds the new card and calls `show_card` again; the old card's timer then does nothing). State is `intake.intake_answer` rows `card.<job>` with `shown.<digest>.<show>`, `edit` and `sent.<digest>` (32 hex digits of the card text's SHA256, `<show>` the row's time in microseconds; rows written in time order; no new table, no homeowner text stored; D-1279). Approval, by SEND (audited `intake.card_approved` as the homeowner) or the timer (`intake.card_auto_sent` as the system) (`run_card_timer`, registered in `server`), enqueues one `intake.cards_approved` event per job with the digest; the card sender has no handler yet (the card-notice sender, T147) and must send only a card for which `approved_card` holds, so the card the businesses get equals the card she was shown (TD-INT-23, TD-AI-17). The page that shows the card behind the link and the email-updates choice are T095's.

**What T156 built (D-1269 to D-1273).** The question set, routing confidence and the golden run, pure except the run's table. `intake::api::BankRun` walks one `catalog::api::QuestionSet` in its order: each `ask_if` on an earlier answer applies, an emergency job asks only the `emergency` questions and only once the cards are out (`starts_now`), every numbered list ends "Not sure" ("No sé"), at most `MAX_ASKED` (8) prompts, and `card_answers` gives `quotes::api::CardParts::answers` (CARD-007). `BankSettings` holds the AI-008 switch and the INT-039 skip threshold, `None` until a recorded AI-027 run sets it: with AI off the template is asked word for word and a question is skipped only when exactly one option of two or more words is in her request (`keyword_answer`); with AI on a rewording goes through `ai::api::accept_question` (fallback the template; options stay the bank's), one follow-up is taken only when its brief equals the set's `kb_trade`, and a question is skipped only at a reading at or above a set threshold. A numbered answer naming a smell of no stated kind (`asks_smell_kind`; "Fuel smell" names one) asks the INT-039 smell question, whose answers 1 and 2 give `Heard::Safety911` for the INT-004 text and hold. `intake::api::decide` goes on only when the keyword reading (`keyword_scored`: 1.0 for one trade with one job type, 0.5 trade only) and, with AI on, the AI reading agree at or above `HIGH_CONFIDENCE` (0.8); otherwise it gives the INT-013 `TradeQuestion`; after INT-014, `after_fit` marks a job keeping more than one candidate `NOT_CERTAIN`, `quotes::api::rules::match_job` reaches only businesses covering every candidate, and `unsure_reach` reports `NoneCovers` (the homeowner text and the OPS-041 email are T215's). `intake::api::run_golden` routes every `docs/catalog/intake_golden.yaml` row on the AI-off path and stores the share in `intake.golden_result` (migration `0083_intake_golden_result.sql`, unique per `catalog_version`, a fingerprint of the compiled-in catalog, bank and golden files); the worker runs it at start-up, and `golden_results` is what T210's report reads. Wiring the run into the conversation is the conversation-owning task's (D-999).

**What T052 built (D-1233 to D-1237).** The homeowner's quote page in `app/portals/src/quote_page.rs` and `templates/quote_page.html` (PICK-008 to PICK-012, PICK-015, WORD-005, SEC-037, A11Y-033, CON-028). `portals::api::quote_page_routes(pool, clock)`, merged in `server::build_router`, serves `GET /q/{token}` behind `refuse_cross_site` (a cross-site POST is 403), `no_store` and `X-Robots-Tag: noindex, nofollow`. The token resolves through `jobs::api::quote_token::resolve`; malformed, unknown, re-issued, revoked, or 30 days after the job's final status (`token_expired`) is a bare 404. `quotes::api::page_quotes` reads the confirmed, unwithdrawn quotes in arrival order with their `unavailable` flag; `number_quotes` numbers them from 1, an unavailable quote keeping its number and showing "no longer available" with no Pick button; available ones carry a Pick form (posting to the page, CSRF bound to the pre-session cookie; the handler is T055's) with "or reply <n> YES" while `takes_quote_edits`. Each card is headed "Quote #<n>" ("Quote #<n> (no longer available)" when unavailable), and every available card carries the PICK-041 "Ask Hava doesn't rank businesses; check it yourself." line. The page has the CON-003 fee line, the PICK-041 top line, both WORD-005 lines, the LIST line and the not-a-contractor line, no rating, count or summary element, and the CON-028 line while a live job is paused by the homeowner. `app/portals/static/quote_page.ts` polls the page every 30 seconds, replaces a card whose `data-available` or `data-pickable` changed (so a job that closes loses its Pick buttons on the last fetch before polling stops), and writes one status line per batch covering both new quotes and quotes no longer available. Not here yet: the STOP case of the still-open line, the close button, the card facts and names, Spanish, and the `/static/` route (D-1235, D-1236).

**What T220 built (D-963, D-964).** Team members, roles and staff phones in `app/businesses/src/team.rs` (BIZ-023, CALL-012). `migrations/0056_businesses_team_members.sql` adds `businesses.team_member` (one row per person, the owner included and marked `is_owner`; `mailbox_id` the opaque ID of the sign-in email, never the email; the two role ticks, at least one, both for the owner by a check; `removed_at`, no `DELETE` for `askhava_app`) and `businesses.staff_phone` (one row per linked main or staff phone, its `id` the line ID of `messaging`'s sender directory, `linked_by` the member, at most one main phone; no number). `add_owner`, `add_member`, `set_roles` and `remove_member` each write one audit row; the owner can't drop a role or be removed. Every change but `add_owner` takes the acting member (`Acting { business, member }`): only the owner adds, changes or removes members, only the owner or a "Confirm and billing" member links, changes or unlinks a phone, only the owner the main one, and every lookup is by business and ID together; `staff_phone (linked_by, business_id)` is a composite foreign key to `team_member (id, business_id)`. Audit rows name the business as actor, since `ops`' `Actor` has no member variant yet (D-963). A phone added or changed reaches the directory through the `SenderLink` trait, which the composition implements with `messaging::api::sender_add` and the vault, since `businesses` may not name `privacy` (D-963); an unlink calls `messaging::api::sender_remove`; both run in the phone change's transaction, so the directory follows at once. Removing a member also unlinks every phone it linked and enqueues one `businesses.member_removed` task (dedupe key per member) for `portals` to end its sessions. `notice_addresses(business, purpose)` returns the owner's and fitting members' opaque email IDs, owner first: cards to "Cards and quotes", picks and release and money to "Confirm and billing", account notices to the owner alone (D-964).

**What T182 built (D-968, D-969).** The self-pause and a business's own quiet times (BIZ-010, D-085). `businesses::api::pause_self` pauses an active business with the reason `self`; `resume_self` locks the row and resumes only a business paused for that reason, so a pause for complaints, the license, no covered job type or by Casey is never ended by the business (D-969). `migrations/0057_businesses_own_hours.sql` adds `own_open_minute` and `own_close_minute` to `businesses.business` (minutes after midnight, Arizona time; both NULL or both set; a check keeps them inside 480 to 1200 and non-empty). `rules::OwnHours` is the checked window (only `new` or `from_minutes`, refusing anything outside 8 a.m. to 8 p.m.), `set_own_hours` and `own_hours` store and read it with a field audit row, and `api::rules::may_notify(own, arizona_time)` is true only inside both the app-wide window and the own one, so own quiet times only narrow it (D-968). The notice senders (`quotes`, `picks`, `messaging`'s fan-out) do not call `may_notify` yet; the task that holds business notices for quiet hours wires it in. Section 3's `businesses.pause_event` table is not built: the pause reason stays on the row and each move has its status audit row. A rule's pause goes through `pause_for_rule`, which on a business paused only by itself replaces the `self` reason (one field audit row), so the business can no longer resume itself; `apply` still refuses a pause on a paused business (D-970). `set_own_hours` never changes a removed business (D-971).

**What T081 built (D-1699 to D-1703).** Business sign-up and the identity chain in `app/businesses/src/signup.rs`, `identity.rs` and `about_us.rs`. `migrations/0119_businesses_signup_identity.sql` adds `businesses.signup` (one row per business: the sign-up email's opaque `mailbox_id`, the optional source answer and `g` code, the identity step reached, the code and match ticks, the flag, `review_since` and `review_told_at`, the About us line; no contact value, no `DELETE`) and the append-only `businesses.terms_acceptance` (TERMS-033, channel `business_signup`). Section 3's `businesses.identity_attempt` and `businesses.signup_review` are folded into `businesses.signup` (D-1699). `sign_up` checks the form against `licensing`'s `ClassMap::opening` ("Open now", "Coming later"), writes the business, the sign-up row and an audit row in one transaction, then creates the Stripe customer with no card through `payments::api::ensure_customer` outside it (R11). The chain sends a Twilio Verify text to the record phone, then a Verify email to the record email, then records the letter step, whose code `letter_code` derives from the business ID under the ID secret; the record contacts come from the caller, never the business. `settle` applies `Event::Checked` through `service::apply`, so activation is `status::next`'s; `approve` applies `Event::OwnerApproved`. `hand_entity_keys` passes the FEE-011 values to `payments::api::record_entity_keys` as keyed digests. `review_due` lists needs-review sign-ups to tell (BIZ-020); the email itself, the Lob mailing and the trade-opened notice are not wired yet (D-1701).

**What T193 built (D-1709 to D-1713).** The business sign-up screens: `app/portals/src/business_signup.rs` renders `templates/business_signup.html` for each `Screen` (the form with the category list, the LIC-047 pest attestation and the BIZ-030 fee box above the Terms link; the masked record contact and its kind before any code; the "That number is old" saved screen; the letter step with town and ZIP and "Stop, I'll fix my record first"; the done screen), each with the WEB-024 notice line for the business-texting switch and BIZ-024's line. `done_facts` reads the trade count through `businesses::api::trade_count` and the open jobs through the new `quotes::api::open_card_jobs` (D-491); `fee_numbers` takes the tiers from `payments::api::Tier::cents` and the windows from `picks`' settings. `app/businesses/src/signup.rs` adds `MaskedContact`, `fix_record_lines`, `RecordSeen`, `number_is_old` and `resume_on_new_record` (keyed digests of the record phone, email and mailing address, never a value; the chain restarts at the first changed contact), `SignupSettings` (`ASKHAVA_BIZ_REVIEW_TARGET_DAYS`, default 4; `ASKHAVA_BIZ_LIST_DELAY_DAYS`, default 3), `review_target` and its line, `fee_summary`, `trade_count` and `claim_launch_notice`. `migrations/0120_businesses_signup_resume.sql` adds `waiting_on_record_since`, `old_text_key`, `old_mail_key`, `old_letter_key`, `trades` and `launch_notice_sent_at` to `businesses.signup`. The routes that serve and post these screens, the daily-list hook that calls `resume_on_new_record` and sends `signup_record_updated`, and the launch email are wired later (D-1709).

**What T269 built (D-3025 to D-3028).** The release notice's signed link opens business sign-in (PICK-024, SEC-004, D-1761): `signin_page` is the composition's `GET /b/sign-in`; with `SignIn::with_notice_links(secret)` (the `IdSecret` `messaging`'s notice sender signs with) it recomputes `core`'s `link_token(secret, LinkKind::Action, p, salt(j ‖ e))` from the URL's `p` (the person's mailbox ID), `j` (job number) and `e` (expiry), compares it with `t` in constant time (`subtle`) and checks `e` against the clock. A valid link shows the existing "Sign in" button, whose `POST /b/sign-in/notice?…` checks again and emails the usual 15-minute one-time link to that mailbox's memberships (`businesses::api::businesses_of_mailbox`, under `LINKS_PER_LIFE`), with `?job=<j>` riding on it through the link and code steps (unchanged) to a 303 to `/b/jobs/<j>` once signed in. The routes read the query leniently (`RawQuery`, `NoticeQuery::from_raw`): an unknown or repeated field is ignored and `job` counts only as positive digits, so a bad value is plain sign-in, never a 400 or another redirect (D-3028). A tampered, expired or other signed-in person's link shows plain sign-in; the same person already signed in goes to the job page. The link alone starts no session and `portals` reads no job (TD-PICK-51, `tests/integration/tests/notice_signin_link.rs`).

**What T194 built (D-1773 to D-1775).** The new-device code on business sign-in (SEC-004, TXT-038, BIZ-023): `SignIn::with_device_codes(DeviceCodes::new(verify, code_address, key))` makes the emailed link's button, on a browser that is not a known device of that member, send one Twilio Verify code (by text to the member's own phone that gets texts, else by email to their own sign-in address, from the composition's `CodeAddress`; `CodeTo::channel` is only `Sms` or `Email`, never a call) and show a one-time-code field; only a correct code uses the link, starts the session and stores a known device (a `portals.login_link` row born used with a 180-day life, its token only in the `__Host-askhava-device` cookie) in one transaction. A known device of the same member skips the code; another member's device or a used link's token never does. Codes never go through the outbox. A link sends one code, recorded server-side first (a born-used marker row, since Twilio ignores idempotency keys, D-1776); each wrong code is an `auth_failed` event naming the link, and after `CODE_TRIES` (5) the link is used up (D-1777). Without `with_device_codes` the link alone signs in on any device (T084's behaviour, fails open), so the composition must never serve the routes without it (D-1777).

**What T272 built (D-3038 to D-3041).** Business sign-in's caps are atomic admissions (SEC-004, SEC-014, SEC-020, SEC-048; D-1837, D-2349, D-2635). `models::admit_link` counts a member's links of the last 15 minutes (leaving out remembered-device rows) and inserts the new one in one short transaction under that member's `pg_advisory_xact_lock`, committed before the email. Migration `0167_portals_code_tries.sql` adds `portals.code_send` (a link's one Verify send: `pending`, `sent` or `failed`; replaces the `code-sent:<link>` marker row) and `portals.code_try` (try 1 to 5 per link: `pending`, `right`, `wrong` or `abandoned`). A press reserves the send in one committed statement (new, `failed`, or `pending` older than 75 seconds), calls Verify with nothing held and records the outcome; a check needs a `sent` code, reserves the next try under the link's advisory lock, calls Verify with nothing held and records the outcome; a try left `pending` still counts, and with none left the link is used up. `device_step`, `send_once`, `check_once` and `start` take the pool and hold a connection only for each short step, so a hanging Verify holds no transaction and no connection (PT-04, AVAIL-01). Tests: `tests/security/tests/signin_admission.rs` (TD-SEC-38).

**What T084 built (D-1724 to D-1728).** Business sign-in: `app/portals/src/business_auth.rs` serves `/b/sign-in` (an email form; the reply is the same whether or not the address is on file) and `/b/sign-in/{token}` (opening shows only a "Sign in" button, SEC-026; the button's POST uses the link). A link is 256 OS-random bits, stored only as its SHA256 in `portals.login_link` with the member, the business, a 15-minute expiry and `used_at`, which one guarded `UPDATE` sets, so a link works once (SEC-004); at most 5 links go to one member in 15 minutes. The sign-in, the `Realm::Business` session of 30 days (`start_session`) and its `portals.business_session` row (the business the member signed in to) commit in one transaction; a used, expired or unknown link is an `auth_failed` security event. Which members an address is on file for, and the email carrying the link, are the composition's `SignInMail` (no `messaging` sign-in email exists yet), so no route is served yet (D-1724): `business_auth` exports the handlers and `SignIn::into_router` (state, `no-store`, cross-site refusal), and the composition writes the two `.route` lines when it serves them, which is when the Ally route table lists the pages and their `PAGE_STATES` rows are due (D-1728). `business_principal` is how every business route learns who is signed in: a business session whose member is still current on `businesses::api::members`, never an owner or homeowner session (SEC-006); `BusinessPrincipal::may(business, need)` scopes a route to that business and role, and `confirm_pay::may_confirm` lets only the owner or a "Confirm and billing" member of the picked business open or press Confirm (PICK-033, D-492). `run_member_removed`, registered in `server` for `businesses.member_removed`, deletes the removed member's business sessions and uses up their unused links (SEC-024). `migrations/0123_portals_business_signin.sql` adds the two tables. The new-device code is T194's; the generated route-by-principal matrix is T088's.

**What T083 built (D-1719 to D-1723).** `app/businesses/src/complaints.rs`: `record_complaint` stores one `businesses.complaint` row (business, job ID, kind code, safety mark, time; never the homeowner's words or contact), raises an `ops.alert` of kind `businesses.complaint_safety` at once when the words hold an OPS-005 safety word (`has_safety_word`, English and Spanish), queues `businesses.complaint_box_closed` for 5 days later and, at the third complaint within 90 days (inclusive), pauses the business through `pause_for_rule` with the `complaints` reason (BIZ-012, D-016). The homeowner's BIZ-011 reply is `ReplyFacts::text`, English and Spanish (apology, the not-a-contractor line, the business's phone, the job number and trade, askhava.com/help/roc-complaint) filled from `ReplyFacts`. The business reads `complaints` (kind and dates only) and writes one `reply` before the box closes (BIZ-029); `run_box_closed` (registered in `server`) calls `list_closed`, which marks the row listed and raises one `businesses.complaint` digest alert; `for_casey` shows a complaint with its reply. `api::link_phone` now wraps `team::link_phone` and, in the same transaction, `phone_link_notice` queues one `businesses.phone_linked` owner email task when the linking member is not the owner (BIZ-033). `migrations/0122_businesses_complaints.sql` adds the table. The portal page (T195), the digest and push (T204), the email handler, the Approve gate and the owner text (T148) come later.

**What T082 built (D-1714 to D-1718).** `app/businesses/src/consent.rs`: the CON-019 E-SIGN box (`consent_box`, always unticked; `consent_wording` names email, texts only when chosen, and says "Ask Hava never places recorded or automated calls to you."), `record_consent` (one append-only `businesses.esign_consent` row per channel with `CONSENT_VERSION` and the time, the text row naming the linked `businesses.staff_phone` it was ticked for; a repeated box adds no row; no call channel exists, by a check), `may_text` (a phone only after a current member ticked the text box for that phone, D-191); the CON-020 welcome request (`request_welcome` queues one `businesses.welcome` task for an active business, `welcome_sent`, `may_get_cards`, `welcome_lines`); BIZ-017's `phone_stopped` (the account keeps its status; one `businesses.phone_stopped` owner email task per opt-out text) and `stopped_email_line`; BIZ-032's `auto_reply_due` with the `business_auto_reply` template; BIZ-048's `recruiting_email` (the advertisement line first, an honest subject, the stop link and the postal mailbox) and the `businesses.recruit_stop` list (`stop_recruiting`, `may_recruit`). `migrations/0121_businesses_consent.sql` adds the three tables. The form's box, the email handlers for the two task kinds, the card sender's `may_get_cards` check and the inbound route's calls are wired later (D-1715).

**What T163 built (D-988 to D-990).** The AI-019 output checks and writer fallbacks in `app/ai` (AI-019, AI-021, D-492, D-493). `ai::api::accept_question(ai_text, template, sources)` and `accept_document(Document::JobCard | ProjectFile, ai_text, sources)` take the text of an AI reply the caller got (the caller makes the call, so AI-021's verified-code and daily-cap rules apply there) and `WriteSources` (her `ScrubbedText`, her answers, the photo notes and the business names, which the caller reads from `businesses`, D-493). `output_checks::check_output` runs the AI-021 screen first (cap, CARD-010, links), then on a folded copy (NFKD with accents dropped, lower-cased, curly apostrophes straightened, spaces collapsed) dollar signs and price words, the English and Spanish cause words, the WORD-022 phrases, the WORD-003 claims, the business names, a number or mid-sentence capitalized word absent from her words, answers, photo notes and (for a question) the bank wording (D-989), and last the CARD-008 shapes (7 or more digits with separators, 3 number words in a row, `@`, contact phrases). Any hit returns `Written` with the bank template (question) or her scrubbed words (card, project file) and the `CheckHit`, never the text. The WORD-022 phrases live in `app/ai/data/word_022_phrases.json`: each `NOT_A_CONTRACTOR` regex of `scripts/trace_spec.py` beside its phrases as slots (no regex crate is allowed, D-988); `tests/docs/test_word_022_phrases.py` expands both and fails on any difference. INT-041's approval or showing of the card is the caller's step (T208).

**What T225 built (D-1383, D-1384).** The `payments` crate (`app/payments`, layer 3) and `migrations/0090_payments_ledger.sql`: the `payments` schema and the append-only `payments.ledger_entry` (FEE-028, D-092): `kind` is one of the eleven FEE-028 codes (`charge`, `refund`, `credit`, `free-first-win`, `repeat-customer`, `fee-free-job-type`, `dispute`, `countering-fee`, `countering-fee-return`, `processing-fee`, `payout`), `amount_cents` is never negative (the kind gives the direction), exactly 0 with no Stripe ID for the three $0 kinds and above 0 for the rest; a payout names no business and no job, every other entry names its business, and every kind but payout and credit names its job; `idempotency_key` is unique. The app role may insert and select, never update, delete or truncate. `payments::api::ledger_append(conn, &LedgerEntry)` is the only writer: it takes a `LedgerEntry` checked by `LedgerEntry::new` against the same rules, runs on the caller's connection so the entry commits with the caller's state change, and on a repeated key returns `Appended::Already` for the identical entry or `LedgerError::KeyReused` for a different one (JOB-006). Charges, refunds, disputes, free wins and the nightly reconcile (T067) add their rows through it (FEE-032).

**What T058 built (D-1399, D-1400).** `app/payments/src/rules.rs` (pure): `payments::api::fee_for(FeeQuote, FeeJob) -> Result<Fee, FeeError>`, the introduction fee by job size of a chosen one-time quote. `Tier::for_basis` gives $25 under $500.00, $75 from $500.00 up to and including $1,500.00 and $199 above (FEE-001, D-009); `Fee::cents` is exactly the tier, never a percentage and never with tax added (FEE-002, FEE-008). A range, and an hourly quote with hours (rate times the low to rate times the high hours), is priced at its midpoint rounded down to the cent (FEE-003, FEE-043, D-033). A diagnostic-visit quote, and an hourly quote with no hours, pays the category's `VisitTier` ($25 or $75, built from the catalog's `trip_fee_tier`; $199 is refused, FEE-004, D-418) only in a `diagnostic_visit` job; otherwise `FeeError::NotDiagnosticVisit` or `FeeError::NeedsHours`. A fee-free job type (FEE-045) gives `Fee::FeeFree`, $0. The recurring 4-month rule, its $75 cap and the setup charge (REC-002, FEE-039, FEE-044) are T188's; the fee fixed at the pick and the fee-mode switch are T060's.

**What T188 built (D-1404 to D-1407).** `app/payments/src/rules.rs` gains `payments::api::period_fee_for(FeeQuote, Option<Period>, PeriodJob)`: a quote with a period in a recurring category is priced at 4 months of its monthly amount (`Period`: weekly x 52 / 12, every two weeks x 26 / 12, quarterly / 3, yearly / 12, only these five, other visit schedules converted to monthly by the caller, D-1407; a range at its exact midpoint; one round-down to the cent at the end), and a recurring job type's tier is capped at the `RecurringCap` setting, $75 by default (REC-002 to REC-006, FEE-039); a recurring job type with no period is `NeedsPeriod`, a period outside a recurring category is `PeriodNotRecurring`, a one-time job type is never capped (REC-010, D-265). There is no early-joiner price lock: T188's lock functions were deleted by T244 when Casey withdrew FEE-040 (D-1494, D-1532), so no fee function takes a join, trade-opening or activation date and every business pays the published fee by job size in effect at the pick (FEE-001, FEE-005, TD-FEE-41). Still pure; the caller maps the catalog's `recurring` and `one_time_job_types` to `Service`.

**What T059 built (D-1409, D-1410).** `migrations/0093_payments_customers_cards.sql`: `payments.customer` (one Stripe customer per business, keyed by `business_id`, no homeowner column, FEE-009), `payments.card_authorization` (append-only for the app role: business, version, the exact FEE-015 text, `accepted_at`) and `payments.payment_method` (a saved card: `pm_…` ID, brand, last four, `method_type` checked to `card`, D-073, `usage` checked to `off_session`, FEE-048, the authorization it rests on, `saved_at`, `removed_at` for BIZ-051's detach). `app/payments/src/models.rs`: `BusinessId`, `CustomerId`, `SavedCard` (refuses every method type but `card`, `CardError::CardsOnly`), `AUTHORIZATION_TEXT` and `AUTHORIZATION_VERSION`, and `SetupIntentRequest` (the $0 Confirm page's form: customer, `usage=off_session`, `payment_method_types[]=card`). `app/payments/src/service.rs`: `ensure_customer` (at sign-up, no card, idempotency key from the business ID only, no transaction across the Stripe call), `customer_of` and `save_card` (authorization and card in one transaction, idempotent on the PaymentMethod ID; charges nothing). The adapter has no SetupIntent create yet: T132 adds it and posts `SetupIntentRequest::form` (D-1410).

**What T060 built (D-1414, D-1415).** `app/payments/src/rules.rs` gains `fee_at_pick(FeeQuote, Option<Period>, PeriodJob, FeeMode, Option<Tier>) -> Result<PickedFee, FeeError>`: the fee fixed at the pick from the chosen quote alone, no AI flag and no other quote as input (FEE-005, D-009); in `FeeMode::Flat` a charged fee is the job type's `flat_tier` (the owner's edit, else the category's set tier), a recurring job type's never above its `RecurringCap` (FEE-039, D-1417), a fee-free job type stays $0 (FEE-007, D-072). `FeeTerms` is the fee-mode setting's value, the mode and the authorization version a business must have accepted; `switched` raises the version on every mode change. `app/payments/src/service.rs` gains `switch_fee_mode` (an `ops` audit row with the owner as actor, in the caller's transaction; the caller stores the returned terms as the `catalog` setting), `accept_authorization` (appends `payments.card_authorization` at the terms' version), `may_confirm` (`ConfirmError::NewAuthorizationNeeded` until then; it gates the charge itself and returns the `Authorized` proof, D-1417) and `record_fee_at_confirm` (takes that proof, reads no authorization again, and after the charge writes exactly the picked fee to the ledger, a charged fee only with a `ch_…` ID (`ConfirmError::ChargeId`), keyed by job and business; nothing takes a final bill or a reply, FEE-006, JOB-016). `payments` now calls `ops` (allowed by the layer table).

**What T061 built (D-1419, D-1420).** `migrations/0095_payments_free_wins.sql`: `payments.entity_key` (a business's free-win entity keys as keyed digests only), `payments.free_win` (one used win per business with its $0 ledger entry and `waived_cents`, FEE-012) and `payments.free_win_claim` (every key of a used win, unique on kind and digest, FEE-011), all append-only for the app role. `app/payments/src/free_wins.rs`: `record_entity_keys` (called by T081's sign-up and by the card-save step for the card fingerprint, D-491) and `waive_at_confirm`, which T224's Confirm page calls before any Stripe charge with the pick's business, its `JoinDates` (sign-up submitted, launch, each category's switch-on; read by the caller from `businesses` and `catalog`) and the customer's other jobs still on record (from `jobs`; none after the 180-day purge). It answers `Repeat` ($0 `repeat-customer` entry, FEE-013), `FreeFirstWin` ($0 entry, inside the 90-day window of `in_free_win_window`, no key claimed, FEE-010, FEE-011) or `NotWaived` (charge). `payments` still names no homeowner.

**What T062 built (D-1424 to D-1428).** `migrations/0096_payments_charge.sql`: `payments.pick_intent` (the pick's one PaymentIntent, its fee, emergency flag and `ended_at`, FEE-047), `payments.charge_attempt` (one row per press, `try_no`, `press_id`, status, FEE-016), `payments.pay_timer` (the card-fix window and the 4-hour Stripe release, `due_at` while running, `left_seconds` while charges are paused, FEE-038) and `payments.fee_limit` (FEE-041); no DELETE on the first three for the app role. `app/payments/src/charge.rs`: `IntentStripe` (create unconfirmed, confirm with an attempt key; the HTTP methods are T132's, D-1424), `open_intent` (key from `pick-<id>-intent`), `confirm_and_pay` (the only charge: a repeated press answers from its attempt; then the caller's `PickNow` and the pick's own record (FEE-017), the charging pause, an undecided earlier attempt settled by asking Stripe (FEE-037), the monthly limit, and only then the PaymentIntent and try `n` keyed from `pick-<id>-try<n>`, inserted under the pick's row lock), `set_fee_limit`. `app/payments/src/handlers.rs`: `record` (answer, audit row and effects in one transaction), the events `payments.card_needs_fix` and `payments.pick_ended`, the tasks `payments.charge_check` (30 minutes, FEE-019) and `payments.pay_timer` (`run_charge_check`, `run_pay_timer`), `charges_paused` (`ops` switches `charging` or `all`), `sync_pause` (FEE-038), the `payments.pause_watch` task (`run_pause_watch`, every minute while a timer is open, registered in `register_handlers()`) and `card_fix_window`. `app/payments/src/status.rs`: `AttemptStatus`. `app/picks/src/money_notices.rs`: `run_card_fix_notice`, registered on `payments.card_needs_fix`, resolves `businesses::api::notice_addresses(Money)` and queues `messaging.business_notice` with template `card_fix_notice` (D-492, D-1428); it skips a pick that no longer needs a fix and uses `card_approval_notice` for a bank approval. Its `messaging` sender, the `payments` check and timer handlers' registration (they need a Stripe client) and the `picks` handler on `payments.pick_ended` are later tasks'; until they exist those kinds fail with `NO_HANDLER` and are dead-lettered (`app/server/src/lib.rs`). Section 3 lists `payments.charge_attempt` only; `pick_intent`, `pay_timer` and `fee_limit` join it at the next plan change (section 3 is locked).

**What T263 changed (D-1758, D-2081 to D-2083).** The monthly fee limit (FEE-041) is a hard cap. `app/payments/src/charge.rs` `begin_attempt` locks the pick's `payments.pick_intent` row, then the business's `payments.fee_limit` row (`FOR UPDATE`; no row, no limit, no lock), sums this Arizona month's succeeded attempts and the undecided (`sent`, `processing`) attempts of the business's other picks (and a bank-approval `needs_action` latest try of an open pick while its card-fix window runs, D-2083) by the month each press started, and inserts the new `sent` attempt (the reservation) only when the fee fits; the transaction commits before the confirm is sent. The earlier unlocked check before the PaymentIntent stays, so a press already over calls no Stripe. `set_fee_limit` is one upsert or one `DELETE`. Tests: `tests/integration/tests/fee_limit_race.rs` (TD-FEE-46).

**What T189 built (D-1474 to D-1478).** `migrations/0097_payments_charge_evidence.sql`: `payments.charge_evidence`, the FEE-042 evidence record of each successful charge (one per pick: business, job, press, attempt, the idempotency label `pick-<id>-try<n>`, Stripe customer and PaymentIntent, the latest FEE-015 authorization version accepted before the press (D-1478), amount, press and success times; IDs and timestamps only, insert and select only for the app role), written by `charge.rs`'s `write_evidence` in the transaction that stores a succeeded attempt. `app/payments/src/release_proof.rs`: `VerifiedStripeEvent::from_webhook(&VerifiedWebhook)` (Stripe vendor only), `paid_release` (type `payment_intent.succeeded`, livemode equal to the configured `StripeAccount`, its account when the event names one, the pick's own PaymentIntent; then the intent is re-fetched and must say succeeded, in the same mode, for exactly the stored fee in `usd`, with metadata naming the pick; the success is stored on the latest attempt; an ended pick is `PickEnded`, D-1478), `no_fee_release` (never for a pick with a PaymentIntent; a $0 `free-first-win`, `repeat-customer` or `fee-free-job-type` ledger entry for the business and job), both returning a `Releasable` with private fields that only `Minter::mint(releasable, contact)` turns into a `privacy` `ReleaseProof` (PICK-022, SEC-030, SEC-039, SEC-049). `server::release_minter()` takes `privacy::api::release_mint()` once per process behind a `OnceLock`; `register_handlers()` calls it first; the webhook route (T224) uses it. `models.rs`: `IntentText::for_job(n)` (description "Ask Hava introduction service, job <n>", suffix "JOB <n>", full descriptor `ASKHAVA* JOB <n>` of at most 22 characters, FEE-049). Not here: the adapter form that sends the description and descriptor (T132), the Confirm page, Stripe Elements and the webhook route (T224), the press facts (IP, signed-in person, page as shown), the charge and receipt IDs and the release, refund and dispute rows of FEE-042 (D-1474).

**What T224 built (D-1479 to D-1481).** `app/payments/src/routes.rs`: `payments::api::stripe_webhook_routes(Arc<StripeWebhook>)`, `POST /webhooks/stripe` (SEC-039, SEC-049): the source rule (`ClientIpPolicy::client_ip`, `check_webhook_source`), the signature (`verify_webhook`), then `paid_release` and a `ReleaseSink` that mints and releases once; 403 for a refused source or signature, 500 only when Stripe or the database failed. `app/portals/src/confirm_pay.rs` with `templates/confirm_pay.html` and `static/confirm_pay.ts`: `portals::api::confirm_page(&ConfirmFacts)` renders the PICK-021 review step (fee, tier, basis, refund line, terms version, one "Confirm and pay $<fee>" or "Confirm", FEE-045's line, the unticked save box with the FEE-015 text, the CON-029 refusal), `press_reply` turns a `ChargeOutcome` into the page's answer; the script mounts Stripe Elements and runs 3-D Secure in the page. Neither route is merged in `server` yet, and the Confirm route's handler waits on a stored fee and job type, T132's `IntentStripe` and T084's business session (D-1480).

**What T063 built (D-1500 to D-1502).** `app/picks/src/confirm.rs`: `ConfirmSettings { window_minutes }` (240 by default, a setting) and `confirm_by(start, settings)`, the end of a non-emergency pick's Confirm window counted in business minutes from the pick notice's real send (a 7:41 p.m. send ends at 11:41 a.m., PICK-019, D-482); `check_at_pick` (the one live lookup through `licensing::api::lookup_at_pick`; a failed lookup falls back to a stored Active check under 24 hours old, else the pick waits) and `check_at_confirm` (stored results only: window, account Active, LIC-009 end and bond dates, latest check Active and fresh; `Refusal::Expired`, `Account`, `License`) (PICK-020, D-056); `fee_line` (PICK-034); `read_business_word` and `business_reply` (PICK-033, PICK-035: YES and CONFIRM never confirm or charge). `app/picks/src/handlers.rs`: `run_pick_notice` on `picks.pick_made` queues one `messaging.business_notice` to `businesses::api::notice_addresses(PicksAndRelease)` with `pick_notice` or `pick_notice_emergency` (PICK-037); `notice_sent` starts the window from the send, restarting `jobs`' Awaiting confirm backstop to end one business minute after it and queuing `picks.confirm_ends`; `run_confirm_ends` expires the pick (`PickStatus::Expired`) and returns the job to Quotes ready, fenced by `jobs`' `timer_from` so a stale timer does nothing. Templates `pick_notice`, `pick_notice_emergency`, `pick_fee_reply`, `pick_expired` (en, es). `picks` now calls `licensing` (allowed by the layer table). Not here: the license-number read for the pick lookup, the Confirm route's call of `check_at_confirm`, the sender's call of `notice_sent`, the email body and its Hold and "Can't take it" links, reminders and the bounce grace (D-1502).

**T063's review fixes (D-1503).** The window length is the setting `ASKHAVA_PICK_CONFIRM_WINDOW_MINUTES` (1 to 3,600 business minutes, 240 when absent), read once by the worker through `picks::api::ConfirmSettings::from_pairs` and handed to `server::register_handlers`, which passes it to `run_pick_notice`; `messaging`'s sender must pass the same value to `notice_sent` and fill "Accept by" from the deadline it returns. `jobs`' backstop length is read from `JobStatus::AwaitingConfirm.wait()` (`picks::status::confirm_backstop_minutes`), not copied. `notice_sent` stores a waiting pick as expired and returns `MovedOn` when `jobs` already returned its job (backstop or emergency 60 minutes), and a repeat with the same send restarts nothing. `fee_line(fee, basis, Language)` gives the English or Spanish line and none for a zero fee; `read_business_word` accepts trailing periods.

**What T065 built (D-1527 to D-1529, D-1531).** `app/payments/src/refund_rules.rs`, pure: `payments::api::decide_unreachable(&UnreachableFacts, now)` is the FEE-022 refund (`NotYet` before 96 hours after the release; then something charged, not already refunded, 2 business attempts not answered by a person, at least 3 hours apart, within 72 hours and 8 a.m. to 8 p.m. Arizona or any hour on an emergency, no call answered by a person either way, no DONE/PROBLEM, no email-contact YES, the missed-call notice sent or attempted); `decide_cancel(&CancelFacts)` is FEE-023 to FEE-025 (a charge after the cancel is refunded, else a refund only if no call was answered by a person before the cancel); `no_show(...)` is FEE-035 (call contact: no business call in 72 hours; email contact: a day-3 NO; never when a call either way was answered by a person), never a refund. Each `RefundDecision::NoRefund` names the first failed `Unmet` condition for the decision email (D-336). `picks` passes the call log as `CallRecord`s and the replies; `payments` reads no job, pick or call table (D-491). Once per pick: `refunded_before` gives `Unmet::AlreadyRefunded` and every refund uses `refund_label(pick)` = `pick-<id>-refund` (FEE-026). The 96-hour task, the texts, the decision email and the Stripe refund call are T191's and T067's; FEE-026's hourly retry and its block on a disputed charge are T066's.

**What T245 built (D-1537).** `migrations/0098_payments_default_privileges.sql` narrows the `payments` schema's default privileges: a table created from here on gives `askhava_app` SELECT and INSERT only (D-092, D-1495); 0090's default of SELECT, INSERT, UPDATE, DELETE is gone, and no existing table's grants changed. Any wider privilege on a new payments table is an explicit `GRANT ... TO askhava_app` in the migration that creates it. `tests/integration/tests/payments_grants.rs` (TD-SEC-33) pins every payments table's app-role privileges against an allow list (`payment_method` UPDATE of `removed_at` only; `pick_intent`, `charge_attempt`, `pay_timer` UPDATE; `fee_limit` UPDATE, DELETE; the rest SELECT, INSERT), checks `pg_default_acl`, forbids TRUNCATE, REFERENCES and TRIGGER, and holds a table outside the list to SELECT, INSERT plus the explicit GRANTs it parses from `migrations/`, so a later payments task (T066, T067, T210) needs no edit there. The other thirteen schemas keep the four-privilege default until T250 (D-1520).

**What T066 built (D-1542 to D-1544).** `migrations/0102_payments_refunds_disputes.sql`: `payments.refund` (one per pick, keyed to its `charge_evidence` row: trigger, status `sending`/`failed`/`succeeded`/`blocked`, try count, `re_…`; column UPDATE of status, tries, `stripe_refund_id`, `decided_at` only) and `payments.dispute` (unique `dp_…`, pick, amount, due date, `auto_submit`, `submitted_by`/`submitted_at`; column UPDATE of those three only). `app/payments/src/refunds.rs`: `request_refund` claims under the pick's `pick_intent` row lock, asks the pure `RefundStatus::next` (`status.rs`), stores the step with an hourly `payments.refund_retry` task, then calls Stripe outside the transaction keyed by `refund_label` and the try; success appends the pick's one `refund` ledger entry (key `refund.<pick>`, naming the PaymentIntent); the first failure raises `payments.refund_failed`; a disputed charge is blocked with one `payments.refund_blocked` alert; `refund_failed` handles Stripe's later `refund.failed`; `early_fraud_warning` alerts and refunds only an unreleased charge (FEE-026, FEE-052). `app/payments/src/disputes.rs`: `record_dispute` records a dispute once with one `dispute` ledger entry of the amount plus $15, an audit row, a High alert and a `payments.dispute_submit` task at due minus 2 days; `mark_auto_submit` (T191, FEE-050's condition) and `record_owner_submission` (D-229); `run_dispute_submit` sends the `EvidencePack` (from `charge_evidence` and the refund, IDs and timestamps only, PRIV-027) through the `DisputeStripe` trait and appends the `countering-fee` entry once; `list_disputes` feeds the weekly digest and the owner portal. Not here: the webhook route's dispatch of dispute, refund and fraud-warning events and the handler registration in `server` (T224/T067 wiring, D-1480), the Stripe adapter's evidence call (T132), the FEE-050 condition and the pick and job moves (T191), the receipt (T068).

**What T064 built (D-1547).** `app/payments/src/late.rs`: `payments::api::late_success(&mut conn, pick, PickNow, now)` runs in `picks`' transaction when a payment succeeds after its job moved on (FEE-021): `NothingCharged` with no `charge_evidence` row, `Current` for a `Payable` pick `payments` has not ended, else one `payments.late_success_refund` task (`LATE_KIND`, dedupe `<kind>.<pick>`) and, only when that task is new, one High `payments.late_success` alert (`Queued`; a repeat is `AlreadyQueued`). A pick that already has a refund or a dispute is `AlreadyQueued` with nothing written. `run_late_refund` handles the task through `request_refund` with `LATE_TRIGGER` (stored as `charged-after-cancel` until a migration and `RefundTrigger::LateSuccess` add a code of its own, which must land before T132 registers the handler); a pick with nothing charged makes the task done, not dead-lettered. `payments` reads only its own tables (D-491). Not wired yet: the `picks` caller (T191) and the handler's registration once `server` has a Stripe client (T132). `late_success` finds only a recorded success: when `picks` says moved on but `payments` has not set `ended_at`, `picks` calls `late_success` instead of minting a release; when `payments` ended the pick, `paid_release` stores nothing (`PickEnded`) and the `CHECK_KIND` round records the success later, so T191 calls `late_success` again after that round (D-1547). Test: `tests/integration/tests/late_success.rs` (TD-FEE-12).

**What T332 changed (D-2290, D-2955).** Migration `0177_payments_late_success_trigger.sql` replaces `payments.refund.trigger`'s check with one that also allows `late-success` (stored rows keep their code); `RefundTrigger::LateSuccess` (code `late-success`, read back by `RefundTrigger::from_code`, `RefundTrigger::ALL`) is `LATE_TRIGGER`, so a late success is no longer stored as `charged-after-cancel`. `handlers::record`'s success effects now call `late_success(tx, pick, PickNow::Payable, at)` right after `write_evidence`, in the transaction that stores the success: a success on a pick `payments` ended (the FEE-018 four-hour end or the FEE-020 card-fix window) queues its one `LATE_KIND` task and one alert whether the webhook (`paid_release`), the `CHECK_KIND` round or the pay timer stores it. `paid_release` no longer stops before the fetch on an ended pick: it verifies and stores the success with its evidence, then, finding the pick ended, calls `late_success` once more in its own transaction (a no-op when `record` queued it; it covers a pick ended after `record` read it) and answers `PickEnded`, so nothing is released. `record` takes the pick's `pick_intent` share lock before its timers and `late_success` reads `ended_at` `FOR SHARE`, and `end_pick` calls `late_success` for a success already stored, so a success and an end racing queue the refund once whichever commits first, with one lock order (`pick_intent` before `pay_timer`) and no deadlock (D-2958). T191 no longer needs to call `late_success` after the check round for a pick `payments` ended. The nightly reconcile refunds a charge on a pick `payments` ended through `request_refund` with `LATE_TRIGGER` too (`reconcile.rs`), so that refund is now stored `late-success` as well. Tests: `tests/integration/tests/late_success_ended.rs` (TD-FEE-56).

**What T067 built (D-1552 to D-1555).** `migrations/0104_payments_reconcile.sql`: `payments.reconcile_charge` (one row per unknown `ch_…` the nightly reconcile met: intent, pick or null, refund reason `no-pick`/`closed-job`/`second-charge`, `seen_at`; append-only, SELECT and INSERT only for the app role, UPDATE, DELETE and TRUNCATE revoked by name). `app/payments/src/reconcile.rs`: `payments::api::reconcile(&pool, &impl ReconcileStripe, id_secret, now) -> ReconcileReport` (FEE-029, FEE-030, FEE-051) reads Stripe's succeeded charges of the last 7 days (`LOOKBACK`, inside Stripe's 30-day event list) through the `payments`-local trait `ReconcileStripe::list_charges` (T132's Stripe client implements it), and for each well-formed `StripeCharge` applies the pure `plan_charge` (`models.rs`) in one short transaction: a missing `charge` entry for a charge on a known pick (by its PaymentIntent, else its metadata pick; the pick's own intent uses `record_fee_at_confirm`'s key so neither doubles it), the balance transaction's `processing-fee` (key `reconcile.fee.<txn>`), and a refund Stripe already made; an unknown charge is written once to `reconcile_charge` with one High `payments.unknown_charge` alert. After the commit (R11) it refunds a charge on a pick `payments` ended (job closed), a second kept charge for a pick (D-153) and a charge naming no pick, never a disputed or already refunded one; the pick's own intent through T066's `request_refund` when T066 has its evidence record (one `payments.refund` row under the pick's lock, shared with T064's late-success refund), else with the `refund.<pick>` ledger key; the reconcile's own refunds key by the `ch_…`, reason and night, so a rerun the same night refunds once and a refused refund, alerted `payments.refund_failed`, is a new try the next night (D-1555). A charge whose writes fail is alerted `payments.reconcile_charge_failed` and skipped; the run goes on. Last it compares gross charges, refunds and disputes (dispute entries less the $15 fee) in the ledger with Stripe's over the charges that name a pick, to the cent, and raises `payments.ledger_mismatch` when they differ or a listed charge is malformed (one-directional by intent, D-1553). `run_reconcile` is the `payments.reconcile` handler (`RECONCILE_KIND`): it queues the next night's task with a per-day dedupe key first, then runs, so a failed run never ends the chain. `money_view(&pool, (from, to)) -> MoneyView` and `business_statement(&pool, business, (from, to)) -> Statement` read only `payments.ledger_entry`; `MoneyView` shows charges, refunds, credits, disputes, dispute fees (with countering fees, less returns), processing fees and payouts as their own lines (FEE-030, FEE-046, FEE-051). Not wired yet: `server` registers `RECONCILE_KIND` and queues the first run once a Stripe client implementing `ReconcileStripe` exists (T132), as for `RETRY_KIND`. Source: `docs/spec/fees.md:33`–`docs/spec/fees.md:36`, `docs/spec/fees.md:55`, `docs/brains/payments-stripe.md` sections 6, 7 and 14 (proposal 5), `docs/brains/postgres-operations.md` P5.

**What T068 built (D-1557 to D-1560).** `app/payments/src/receipts.rs`: `queue_receipt` raises one IDs-only `payments.receipt` task per charged pick and `receipt_for` reads a business's `ChargeReceipt` (fee, tier, job, time) from `charge_evidence` (FEE-031, FEE-053); `picks::api::run_receipt_notice` (`money_notices.rs`, registered in `server`) resolves the money recipients and queues `messaging.charge_receipt`; `messaging::api::run_receipt_email` (`receipt_email.rs`) stores one receipt per recipient in T094's email outbox: "Ask Hava introduction service, job <n>: $<fee>.", "Tax included." and the "Why was I charged?" link; `portals::api::why_charged_page` (`receipts_page.rs`, `templates/why_charged.html`) is that page. `app/payments/src/flags.rs` is the pure FEE-033 tier-edge flag over a business's latest fee bases. The receipt's tier is the charged fee's (`None`, and still a receipt, for an amount that is no tier's fee); the page's `Basis` (`OnSite`, `Quote`, `FourMonths`) is checked against that tier, so a capped recurring basis says the fee is set at the capped tier; a business with no money recipient gets one `picks.receipt_no_recipient` alert and no email (D-1561). Not wired yet: the `queue_receipt` call in `payments::charge::write_evidence`, `RECEIPT_EMAIL_KIND`'s registration, the page's route, the stored basis the flag reads and the flag's caller (D-1557, D-1558, D-1561).

**What T069 built (D-1562, D-1563).** `migrations/0106_picks_release.sql`: `picks.release` (one row per released pick: job, business, basis `paid`/`no_fee`, `released_at`; the primary key makes the release once) and `picks.address_wait` (each emergency acceptance that came before the address); both append-only for the app role. `app/picks/src/release.rs`: `picks::api::release(&pool, &vault, (ReleaseProof, email_chosen), at)` plans with the pure `plan_release` (released before: nothing; ended, or a status other than `paid` for a paid proof or `free` for a $0 proof: nothing, D-1564; emergency with no address: the pick's one wait row and one `picks.address_needed` event so `intake` asks again, TD-INT-34), and reads the vault on a bare connection with `RELEASE_FIELDS` (name, address) or `EMAIL_CHOSEN_FIELDS` (adds the email the homeowner chose to share, PICK-018), never the phone. A regular job claims first (one transaction stores the release row and queues one `messaging.business_notice` to `notice_addresses(PicksAndRelease)` with the `release_notice` email and `release_text` templates and the job number only, PICK-024, D-454, or raises one High `picks.release_no_recipient` alert when there is no recipient) and reads after; an emergency job reads first, to learn whether the address is in. The caller gets a `ReleaseView` (first name, address, chosen email; no phone field) once; it is stored nowhere. Not wired yet: the `ReleaseSink` in `server` that calls it (and moves the pick to `paid` or `free` first), the `picks.address_needed` handler and the re-release once the address arrives, `messaging`'s business-notice sender with the signed `{link}`, the portal's own read of a released pick, and a check of the proof's contact against the pick (D-1563, D-1565).

**What T070 built (D-1572 to D-1576).** `app/picks/src/aftermath.rs`: `picks::api::lost_notices(&mut conn, pick, tier, LossSettings)` runs once the pick is `paid` or `free` and queues one `messaging.business_notice` per other quoter (deduped per pick and business) to `notice_addresses(Cards)`, with the job number and quote count only; the pure `compare` (PICK-038: firm amount, range or hourly midpoint, the tier picked, recurring figure of one period, visit fee; otherwise a different type) and `loss_template` (first sentence only under 3 quotes; "Several businesses quoted" when the card went to 4 or fewer) pick one of the `pick_lost*` templates. `no_longer_active(&mut conn, job)` (final status only) and `refused_after_confirm(&mut conn, job, business)` share one dedupe key per job and business for `job_no_longer_active`, both skipping a business whose pick is `paid` or `free` now (a pick moved on to no-show is not connected, D-1576); `no_longer_active` tells withdrawn quoters too, and `lost_notices` gives the first sentence only when the picked quote or the receiver's own does not read back (D-1576). `live_pick_reply(&mut conn, job, business)` gives the `pick_live_reply` template, job number and pick (the Confirm link's subject) for the business whose pick waits (PICK-027). `quotes::api::quoted_prices` reads each confirmed quote as a `QuotedPrice` (business, `Option<Price>`, `withdrawn`), unavailable and withdrawn ones included (D-1576). Not wired yet: the callers (the pick's move to `paid`/`free`, `jobs`' final moves, the refusal after Confirm, the business inbound-text route), the stored tier picked, and the `LossSettings` keys (D-1575).

**What T247 built (D-1597 to D-1599).** The first-quote move (JOB-009, JOB-011, TXT-016, D-1517). `jobs::api::first_quote(db, job, at)` moves a job whose status `JobStatus::moves_on_first_quote` (Open, No quotes yet) to Quotes ready through the version-fenced `apply` with the `first_quote` homeowner-text event, inside the caller's transaction (a savepoint); losing the guarded update to another move it re-reads, at most 3 times, and any other status moves and writes nothing. `quotes::intake::submit_quote` calls it for a quote confirmed at once (not `ai_read`) after the window, status and edit checks and before the quote row (job lock first, D-1051); `confirm_quote_at(db, (job, business), now)` (and `confirm_quote`, which takes the system clock's now) calls it when an AI-read quote is confirmed. The move bumps the version, so the old Open or No-quotes-yet timer and a held no-quotes-yet text do nothing (JOB-006), and Quotes ready's 3-day wait counts from the quote (JOB-012, JOB-025). `intake::job_texts` sends `first_quote` by its existing steps with a quote-page link, as for `all_quotes_in` (SEC-037). Tests: `tests/integration/tests/first_quote_move.rs` (TD-JOB-11, TD-PICK-40's path).

**What T246 built (D-1516, D-1602 to D-1606).** The page pick end to end. `jobs::api::JobStatus::takes_pick` (Quotes ready, Awaiting confirm) is the one pick rule: the quote page's Pick buttons, `portals::pick_flow`'s review step and final Pick, and `picks::api::page_pick` read it, and a unit test pins `picks`' text stage (Ready or Waiting) equal to it for all twelve statuses. `picks`' end-move-insert core (`service::place`) serves both channels: a waiting pick is ended `replaced` with its `picks.pick_withdrawn` event, Quotes ready moves to Awaiting confirm (a job already there stays, so the Confirm window does not restart), and the new pick is stored with its audit row and `picks.pick_made` event, with its inbound text for a text pick and none (channel `page`) on the page. `page_pick(tx, (job, paused), quote, actor, at)` answers `Picked`, `Same` or `Taken` (not `takes_pick`, a homeowner pause, or a quote not on the page or unavailable); `waiting_quote` lets the page skip the code for the waiting quote. `portals::api::pick_flow_routes(pool, clock, Option<PickCodeKeys<V>>)` is generic over the Verify adapter; `server::pick_code_keys(settings, pairs)` builds `PickCodeKeys<TwilioVerify>` from the vault keys, `ASKHAVA_ID_SECRET`, `TwilioConfig` and the compiled-in `docs/legal/homeowner-terms.md` (its "Version" line is the terms version), `None` in dev and CI without vault keys (the page keeps its "Nothing was picked" lines) and an error in the test and live projects; `build_router(pool, control, (webhooks, pick_keys))`. With keys the final Pick is the code step: the waiting quote shows the result page with no code; a paused job, a job no open request opened, or a number `messaging::api::send_window` calls opted out or untextable gets one line and no form; otherwise the code is sent and the code step shows the code-sent, cooldown, cap or failed-send line. "Finish my pick" checks the fields first (no try counted), then the code (`CodeCheck` as the code field's error), then one transaction: `intake::api::paused_under_lock` (the homeowner lock the CANCEL pause and the text pick take, then the pause again, CON-029), `page_pick` and `intake::api::record_terms_acceptance` (channel `quote_page`, the English line shown, the published version and text, the open request), then `result_page`. From Payment pending on both POSTs show the confirmed line and store nothing. The contact choice and email are checked and not stored (D-1264). Not built here: the PICK-045 homeowner text and the acceptance end time on the result page, the Spanish page, the one-quote result wording, a code that survives a failed store, a code tied to one quote, the voice-call code and the confirmed line's how-to-close (D-1606).

**What T095 built (D-1612 to D-1614).** `app/picks/src/email_code.rs` (PICK-029, PICK-030, D-050): `code_route(Texting, Option<EmailFrom>)` sends a pick code by text while texting is open and, when she opted out or texting is off or suspended, by email only to an address from her own text or web-chat conversation (`EmailFrom::Conversation`); a quote-page address (`EmailFrom::QuotePage`) gets nothing. `ConversationEmail::new` refuses an address the caller labels `EmailFrom::QuotePage`; the label is a caller rule until `privacy` stores a source flag for each address (D-1612), so `send_email_code` and `check_email_code` send only where the caller says the address came from her conversation; the emailed code uses Verify's email channel with the texted code's limits and caps on the same `picks.code_attempt` rows (stored with channel `sms`, a non-voice send, until a migration adds `email`, D-1613), counted by vault contact, never the address. `opted_out_yes` takes an opted-out homeowner's "<number> YES" through the text-pick rule and the page pick's core with no text queued: `TermsFirst` before the PICK-016 Terms line was sent, `Picked` or `Same` after it, `Nothing` otherwise (the pick stored with channel `page`, D-1614). No caller yet: the conversation address needs a vault grant and a source flag, the TermsFirst page line and emailed Pick button and the email confirmation need `messaging` emails, and `intake` must hand an opted-out YES here; the TXT-018 channel choice, the approved card and license lines on the quote page are not built (D-1612).

**What T191 built (D-1814 to D-1819).** `app/picks/src/unreachable_refund.rs` (FEE-022, JOB-027, TXT-026, TXT-030, FEE-050, FEE-052, JOB-013, JOB-014; D-491): `picks` makes every decision after a release and calls `payments::api` for money and `jobs::api` for moves; `payments::refund_rules` stays pure. `call_record` reads `calls::api::LoggedCall` as the rule's `CallRecord`; `missed_call_text` (the one `caller_missed` text, only for a number `messaging::api::send_window` says may be texted; its result FEE-022's `notice_attempted`), `queue_reminders` (TXT-026 at 24 and 48 hours, cancelled by an earlier attempt) and the pure `reminder_times`, `missed_call_at`, `send_at` (quiet hours: the missed-call text is a `Kind::PickStep`, a reminder an update) and `decision_template`; `decide_at_96_hours` refunds through `request_refund` and, only once the fee is refunded (a blocked or failed refund is `AtNinetySix::RefundWaiting`, nothing changed), in one transaction moves Connected to Closed and closes the line, and emails the business the outcome template; a rerun after a refund finishes the close (D-1819); `no_call_at_72_hours` returns to Quotes ready (pick `no_show`) a call-contact job with no call in 72 hours or an email-contact job on a day-3 NO; `dispute_auto_submit` marks through `mark_auto_submit` only on release plus a call or 3DS; `fraud_warning` refunds through `early_fraud_warning` and, once refunded, ends an unreleased pick `fraud_refunded` with Payment pending to Quotes ready (nothing when the job moved on). Not built yet: JOB-027's homeowner close text and 72-hour email question (their templates need `quotes`' TXT-040 list, D-1818). Not wired yet: the worker tasks at the release's 24, 48, 72 and 96 hours and the Stripe webhook dispatch (T132).

**What T278 built (D-2928 to D-2931).** The check-in, MORE-list, feedback, unreachable-closed and emailed-question homeowner templates (en, es drafts) are in `quotes`' TXT-040 list as `Shape::Capped` and, with `problem_reply`, in `intake`'s `JOB_TEXT_TEMPLATES`, so the `jobs.homeowner_text` sender sends them (the feedback request with the `/f/<token>` link, `{business}` from the event). `picks`' `close_unreachable` queues `unreachable_closed` in the close's transaction, and the release's claim calls `check_ins_at_release`, which queues `jobs::api::schedule_check_ins` from the release and `quotes::api::earliest_visit` only for a Connected job; nothing moves a job to Connected yet (D-2015 (1)), so that move must come before the claim or call it. The 72-hour `emailed_question` send is T281's; the MORE reply's dispatch is T277's.

**What T190 built (D-1522 to D-1526).** HOLD, Away and the emergency windows as pure rules in `app/picks/src/confirm.rs`, exported from `picks::api` (PICK-043, PICK-044, BIZ-034): `hold_allowed` and `hold` (the owner or a "Confirm and billing" member, a non-emergency job, the first HOLD, a clock not delayed by Away; the new end is `add_business_minutes(due, hold_minutes)`), `away_until` and `away_for` (at most `away_max_minutes` business minutes ahead; AWAY 0 is `TooFar`), `window_start` (a pick sent while away starts at the away end; an emergency pick never waits), `emergency_plan` and `emergency_fix_by` (60 clock minutes at any hour, the reminder at 30 and `None` once it would fall at or after the end; a night pick to a business without the BIZ-007 tick is seen at 8 a.m.) and `read_time_word` for HOLD/ESPERA and AWAY/AUSENTE. The lengths are `picks::api::WindowRules` (120, 240, 60, 30 by `Default`), passed by the caller; no `ASKHAVA_*` key until a caller reads one. Not wired yet: the stored HOLD-used and away-until facts, the Hold link, the HOLD and AWAY text replies and their templates, and `messaging`'s pick-notice sender applying `window_start` and `emergency_plan` before `notice_sent` (D-1524).

**What T192 built (D-1567).** The release proof's compile-fail and once-only tests (SEC-030, TD-SEC-11): three trybuild cases in `askhava-security` (no features), `tests/security/ui/forge_release_proof_{literal,default,clone}.rs`, each trying both `ReleaseProof` and `ReleaseMint` from outside `privacy` (a struct literal, `Default`, `.clone()`), with `.stderr` files from the pinned toolchain; `app/privacy/src/tests/release_mint_once.rs` takes the mint once and checks that a second take panics. No behavior changed.

**What T071 built (D-1577).** `tests/integration/tests/ledger_1000_jobs.rs` (TD-FEE-16; FEE-028 to FEE-030, FEE-032): 1,000 simulated jobs through charges, refunds, disputes and two nightly reconciles against an in-process stateful fake of `PaymentsAdapter` and `ReconcileStripe` in stripe-mock's shapes (stripe-mock keeps no state, so a refund would not show on its charge list); the ledger and each business statement reconcile to the fake with zero drift. The stripe-mock and Stripe test-mode round trip for refunds and the charge list is T132's.
