**What T072 built (D-1582).** `tests/integration/tests/prop_prompt_injection.rs` (TD-AI-03, TD-AI-05, AI-007, AI-025): injection lines from the twin's `injector` and "ignore your instructions" openers, bare or wrapped in an early closing tag, placed before or after fake requests and quote notes; a line with no trade or hazard words leaves routing, the 911 screen, the consent and who-is-this texts and the quote unchanged, every line leaves `parse_quote`'s format and range (so the introduction fee by job size) unchanged, and the gated `read_intake` against a fake model that obeys every word after the first closing tag reads the same as the clean request. No app code changed.

**What T073 built (D-1587).** `tests/mutation/mutants.toml` gains 33 named mutants on the 4-month rule and the fee tiers (`app/payments/src/rules.rs`), the repeat customer and second free win (`free_wins.rs`), the license check at pick, the card shown and its digest, an AI summary with the setting off and a fee-free job type charged; `python3 scripts/run_named_mutants.py --all` kills all 33. `app/payments/src/rules.rs` joins `cargo_mutants_files`. "The AI major flag raising the tier" has no text to mutate, since no fee function takes an AI flag (FEE-005, D-1415).

**Stage-3 cleanup (T075, D-1607).** `portals`' "Why was I charged?" page formats money with `confirm_pay::money`, the Confirm page's formatter, instead of a copy of it.

**What T076 built (D-1679 to D-1682).** The `calls` crate (`app/calls`) and `migrations/0114_calls_pool.sql`: the `calls` schema, whose default privileges give `askhava_app` SELECT and INSERT only (D-1520); `calls.number_pool`, one row per connected job's masked line (job, homeowner and business by ID, the pool number, `shared`, `assigned_at`, `closed_at`, an identity `seq`; one open line per job; the app role may UPDATE `closed_at` only, for T079's closing); and `calls.pool_alert` (append-only: the `ops.alert` ID and the `seq` of the line that raised it). `calls::api::assign_number(db, &MaskedPool, Connected, now)` takes a number only from `adapters`' settings pool (10 by default; the app never buys one, CALL-017, D-160) under one advisory lock: the number with fewest open lines of the same homeowner or business, then fewest open lines, then settings order; a number already used by the same homeowner or business makes the `Line` `shared` when assigned (a flag that is never updated, so T077's PIN routing derives sharing from the open lines on the number) and raises one High `calls.pool_exhausted` alert unless one is open, an alert counting as open until a later assignment finds a free number (D-231, D-1680); the line, its audit row and any alert commit together, and a repeat call returns the open line, or `LineOpen` when it names another business or homeowner. `Line` carries only the pool number, so neither side is handed the other's (CALL-002); calls dial out with it as caller ID over standard programmable voice (CALL-021). `calls::api::automated_call_gate` checks the one suppression list through `messaging::api::send_window` before a safety call or a code call not asked for on screen (CON-035, CALL-014, D-1681); a number `messaging` would not text answers `NotDialable`, and for a safety call raises one High `calls.safety_call_not_dialable` alert about the contact (D-1682). Tests: `tests/integration/tests/number_pool.rs` (TD-CALL-01, TD-CON-28's T076 case).

**What T077 built (D-1684 to D-1688).** `app/calls/src/routing.rs`: the pure `route(Party, &[RoutedLine], CallTurn) -> Step` (CALL-003, CALL-004, CALL-005, CALL-025): a linked business phone or her own number with exactly one open line on the pool number connects; anyone else, or a party with two or more lines there, enters the job PIN (then `job_pin`, from the line ID, D-1684; since T262 the stored random PIN, and T326 deleted `job_pin` and `route`, leaving `route_with` with each line's PIN, D-2247), and only an exact single match connects; 3 tries per call and 6 per number per pool number per day (the counts are the caller's input until a calls migration stores them, D-1685), the last one hanging up with one High `calls.pin_tries_exceeded` alert and a used-up number refused before the prompt; after the second wrong try an A-attested call from the business holding a line on the number gets `Step::ReadBack` of its newest line (D-1686). `answer_call` finds the party (`messaging::api::sender_business`, `privacy::api::find_by_phone`), the open lines and, for a read-back, the first name and street through `privacy::api::read_for_readback` (`app/privacy/src/readback_reader.rs`, the `(Calls, CallReadback)` grant). `picks::api::release` takes `(vault, masked pool)` and, only on `Released::Shown`, opens the job's line with `assign_number`; a failed line still returns the view and raises one High `picks.release_line_failed` alert (D-1688). The who's-calling text (TXT-027) is not built: its template needs a file outside T077's paths (D-1687).

**What T078 built (D-1689 to D-1691).** `migrations/0116_calls_call_log.sql`: the append-only `calls.call_log`, one row per finished masked call (line, call SID unique so a repeated status callback logs nothing twice, who called by role only, who answered: person, machine, nobody or unknown, length in seconds, `reached_45s` for reports only, when it ended). `app/calls/src/rules.rs`: the pure `talked` (a person answered, either direction, any length; a machine never, D-141), `business_attempt` (only the business's own calls) and `answered_by` (the provider's status and machine detection; only `human` is a person, a missing or `unknown` result is `Unknown` and never talked, D-1690; T080 must feed it the dialed leg's final callback). `app/calls/src/handlers.rs`: `dial_twiml`, the one bridging TwiML, `record="do-not-record"` with machine detection (CALL-006, D-1691); `finished_call`, `log_finished_call` and `call_log` (CALL-008). `picks` maps the log into `payments`' `CallRecord` for the refund rules, as `calls` cannot call `payments` (D-1689). Tests: `app/calls/src/tests/connection.rs` (TD-CALL-04), `tests/integration/tests/no_recording.rs` (TD-CALL-03, the log).

**What T079 built (D-1694 to D-1698).** `app/calls/src/lifecycle.rs`: `close_line(db, (job, job number), CloseReason, now)` closes a job's open line on a cancel, a no-show or a complaint, and `close_expired(db, now)` every line open 30 days (`LINE_DAYS`), each setting only `closed_at` with one audit row naming the reason, safe to repeat (CALL-009); only a cancel queues one `messaging.business_notice` (`line_closed_cancelled`) to the owner and every "Confirm and billing" member, and `calls` records no strike (CALL-010, D-1694). The pure `phone_works` and `gate` hold a member-linked phone to the caller's seeded owner approval, turning it into `Party::Unlinked` until approved (CALL-012, BIZ-033); T080 applies `gate` in the voice handler and T148 stores the approval (D-1695). `request_link(db, line, blocked phone, now)` queues the `link_this_phone` notice with the last 4 digits and the line's stored PIN (T326: read with `pins::stored_pin` in its transaction; a line with no stored PIN queues nothing, D-2247), once per line: `to` the owner and every "Confirm and billing" member by email, and `text_to: "main_phone"` so the "Reply LINK" text goes only to the business's signed-up main phone when it chose text (CALL-011, D-078, D-1696). `greeting_twiml()` is all a call to the Ask Hava number hears: the CALL-020 greeting in English then Spanish, then a hang-up (D-227, D-1697). An email-preferred homeowner gets a line like anyone: the pool never reads the contact channel (CALL-018). Not here: callers of the close functions, the daily sweep, the 45-minute follow-up and the inbound-voice route (T080, T148, T191). Tests: `tests/integration/tests/line_lifecycle.rs` (TD-CALL-05, TD-CALL-07, TD-CALL-08's gate, TD-BIZ-34's removal case).

**What T080 built (D-1704 to D-1708).** `app/calls/src/routing.rs` (T077's, extended): `PinWait` (the `ASKHAVA_PIN_WAIT_SECONDS` setting, 10 to 60 seconds, default 10), `pin_prompt_twiml` (the spoken `PIN_PROMPT` in a `Gather` that waits that long, said once more, then `PIN_GOODBYE` and a hang-up; no `Play`, so no tone-only prompt) and the pure `pin_heard`, so a 711 relay operator has time to enter the digits and a relay call routes like any other (CALL-013, D-485, D-1704); the prompt and the read-back answer only the calling side's webhook, and `dial_twiml` puts no whisper `url` on the dialed number, so a bridged call plays nothing to the homeowner (CALL-023). `app/calls/src/voice.rs`: `may_code_call` (her tap, a homeowner only) and `code_call_twiml` ("This is Ask Hava LLC", slow, the code twice, the main number as the callback and never the calling number, "Press 1 to hear it again"; CALL-014, CALL-015); `SafetySwitch` (off at launch; `turn_on` needs an `Approval` with a reference and every CALL-022 `Conditions` item), `safety_script`/`safety_twiml` (under 30 seconds, no job detail, "press 9"), `place_safety_call` (the handler of intake's `calls.safety_call` `core.task` event: switch, 30 minutes, a pool number other than the main number, the suppression list, her phone under `(Calls, BridgeCall)`, a `calls.safety_call_dialing` claim on the alert committed through `ops::api::audit_once` before the one dial keyed on the alert, so a retried task dials nothing more, then one `calls.safety_call_placed` or `calls.safety_call_failed` audit row naming the message and `safety_script_v1`; every answer logs `calls.safety_call_result` and every key press `calls.safety_call_key` or `calls.safety_call_press_9`; intake's trigger is true only on a 911-list match, D-1705), `safety_call_key` ("9" lists her number, channel `owner`, `voice:press_9`), `safety_call_answered` (a person answering acknowledges Casey's critical alert through the new `ops::api::acknowledge_alert`, audited `ops.alert_acknowledged` for the weekly digest) and `ring_owner`/`owner_bridge_twiml` (Casey's phone rung from a pool number, the INT-004 words read to him, then the homeowner dialed with the Ask Hava number shown, never recorded; OPS-028, D-1706, D-1707). `app/intake/src/emergency.rs` (T039's): the narrower `safety_call_trigger` and `enqueue_safety_call`, which queues the event in the caller's transaction, deduped on the critical alert, only while the switch is on (CALL-022, D-491, D-1705). Not here: the inbound-voice route that serves this TwiML, the stored switch and its portal page and button (T108), the handler's registration in the worker and the suspension path that calls `enqueue_safety_call` (T096), the voice adapter's TwiML URL (D-1708). Tests: `tests/integration/tests/voice_calls.rs` (TD-CALL-06, TD-CALL-09, TD-TXT-09's T080 cases, OPS-028).

**What T090 built (D-1789 to D-1793).** `app/privacy/src/photos.rs` and `migrations/0125_privacy_photos.sql` (PRIV-013, PRIV-014, PRIV-023, PRIV-025, PRIV-028). `privacy::api::sniff` reads an upload's type and size from its bytes: JPEG, PNG or WebP up to `MAX_PHOTO_BYTES` (15 MiB, also the upload route's body limit); a HEIC file (by the `ftyp` box's major or any compatible brand) is `PhotoError::Heic` and is never decoded (the caller answers with the PRIV-025 reply, a template T212 adds, D-1792); anything else is `NotAPhoto`, whatever its name. `clean` waits for one of 2 decode permits and decodes in `spawn_blocking` inside one 20-second limit, so a stuck decode turns later uploads away as `Busy` instead of queueing them; from the header, before any pixel is allocated, it refuses more than 8,000 px a side, 40 megapixels, or a pixel-buffer peak (`peak_bytes`: the decoded frame plus the larger of a quarter-turn copy and the RGB8 copy) over 256 MiB (D-1793); it keeps the primary frame, applies the orientation and writes a fresh JPEG, so EXIF, GPS, XMP, IPTC, maker notes and appended bytes are gone; only its `CleanPhoto` is stored, screened, sent to AI or put on a card, and the uploaded bytes are never stored. `store_photo` seals it with `PhotoSeal` (AES-256-GCM under `VAULT_KEY`, associated data `privacy.photo|<job>|<photo>|v<version>`), writes it through the `storage` adapter as `photos/<id>`, then records `privacy.photo` (job, key version, storage name) with an audit row; a failed insert leaves an orphan ciphertext object with no row, which the photo purge (PRIV-007) must find by listing the bucket (D-1793). `PhotoSeal::new` skips `Vault::new`'s key-separation checks, so it is built from the `VAULT_KEY` value a `Vault` has accepted. `issue_link` stores the SHA256 of a `LinkKind::Photo` token in `privacy.photo_link` with a 10-minute expiry; `photo_path` is `/photos/<token>`, the token in the path; `open_link` opens a live link only, audited. Both tables are SELECT and INSERT for the app role. The workspace's `image` entry now has `default-features = false`, so only the JPEG, PNG and WebP decoders are built (D-1790). Not here: the HTTP photo route and the upload route that call these (their first caller wires them, D-1791), the photo-ask wording and the texting provider's media delete (T212), the 30-day purge (PRIV-007). Tests: `app/privacy/src/tests/photo_checks.rs` (TD-PRIV-04, TD-PRIV-08, TD-PRIV-10), `tests/integration/tests/photo_links.rs` (TD-PRIV-04, TD-PRIV-08, TD-PRIV-13 photo-link cases).

**What T091 built (D-1794, D-1795).** `app/quotes/src/photos.rs` (AI-016, CARD-013, CARD-014, INT-044). `quotes::api::card_photo(photo, answer, gate, screener)` takes a `privacy::api::CleanPhoto` (so the format and size checks, metadata stripping and re-encoding have run) and returns `CardPhotoOutcome::OnCard(CardPhoto)` or `LeftOff(reason)`: `Removed` or `NotConfirmed` unless the homeowner confirmed no person and no house number, with no AI call; before `PhotoAiGate::from_records(zero_retention_signed_on, today, photo_sentence_live)` is AI (signed on or before today and the sentence live, every environment; never from the AI switch's `live_ok`) the on-server checks suffice and the `PhotoScreener` is never called; after it, both `ScreenCheck`s (different instructions asking for faces, plates and readable text) run and any flag or refusal is `Flagged`, a failed call `NotScreened`; no blurring in V1 (D-1794). `CardPhoto` has a private field. Not here: `ai`'s screener implementation and the `ai::api` read of the zero-retention date and photo sentence (D-1795), the note wording and the photo ask (T212), the card email's links (PRIV-027, T147). Tests: `tests/integration/tests/card_photos.rs` (TD-CARD-04, TD-AI-11, TD-PRIV-08 card and AI-input cases).

**What T212 built (D-2871 to D-2875).** Photo asks, the on-server checks and preview, look pictures and the concept image (INT-044 to INT-046, PRIV-015, SEC-041, A11Y-027). `app/privacy/src/media_fetch.rs`: `take_upload` (`clean`, then a re-ask under `DEFAULT_MIN_SHORT_SIDE` (320) on the shorter side, HEIC and over-limit outcomes, then `store_photo`), `take_picture_text` (a `PictureTextMedia` fetch through `fetch_twilio_media`, the SEC-041 guard, then `take_upload`, then the provider delete, reported as `MediaState`: nothing to delete, deleted or delete pending), `ProjectFile` (from `ScrubbedText` only) and `make_concept` (a `ConceptImageAdapter` call while the switch is on, the first image and 3 change rounds, request = project file and `CONCEPT_LABEL` only). `app/intake/src/photos.rs`: the photo and design texts (`photo_body`, `photo_text`; fixed in the file until T383, D-2871), `after_photo`, `photo_answer`, `left_off_note`; `app/intake/src/design.rs`: `is_design_category`, `concept_step`; `conversation::after_questions` places steps 9 to 11 of INT-043. `app/portals/src/photo_upload.rs` with `templates/photo_upload.html`: the upload page (native file input, PRIV-015 line, error state, preview), route T306's. `quotes::api::JobPage::with_design` and `portals::api::job_details_page` show the photos, project file and labeled concept image on the business job page; the page reads the concept image off the card itself, so it is labeled even if the caller does not name it (D-2874). Not here: the Twilio media delete in `adapters` and the composition's `PictureTextMedia` (D-2872), an image adapter and the owner switch store (D-2873), the dispatcher calls (D-999). Tests: `tests/integration/tests/intake_photos.rs` (TD-INT-27, TD-INT-28, TD-SEC-20 media case, TD-CARD-04, TD-CARD-12 photo and concept cases).

**What T092 built (D-1799 to D-1803).** The retention purge (PRIV-007 to PRIV-011, D-013, D-079, D-491, D-492). `migrations/0127_privacy_retention_purge.sql`: `privacy.retention_hold` (source `dispute` or `complaint`, source ID, job, placed and released times; only `released_at` updatable) and DELETE on `privacy.photo` and `privacy.photo_link`. `jobs::api::sweep_closed` (the daily `jobs.purge_sweep` task, first queued by the worker at start) queues, for every job in a final status (no look-back limit), three `jobs.purge_due` tasks with payload `{job, stage}` due at its `status_at` plus 30 days (`photos`), 180 days (`contact`) and 1,096 days (`text`), each deduped by job and stage; the same daily task runs `intake::api::run_request_sweep`, which queues `intake.request_purge` for each request that never got a job, 180 days after it ended. The composition runs, on `jobs.purge_due` and `privacy.purge_recheck`, `businesses::api::run_complaint_holds` (queues a `businesses.complaint_hold` event per complaint on the job, open while `listed_at` is null), `payments::api::run_dispute_holds` (each dispute on the job's charges becomes a hold through `privacy::api::place_hold`, released 90 days after its evidence due date, D-1800), then `privacy::api::run_purge_due`: photos are deleted at once; the contact stage waits one hour (`privacy.purge_recheck`) so the complaint events are applied (`privacy::api::run_complaint_hold`), then re-checks daily while a hold is open (a re-check that just queued complaint events waits another hour, `run_purge_due_after`) and otherwise queues `privacy.purge`, which `intake::api::run_purge` answers by calling `privacy::api::purge_contact` for the job's homeowner (unless another request of theirs is open or closed under 180 days ago, or any of their jobs is held): name, email, address and on-site contact cleared, the email index deleted, the sealed phone and its homeowner-phone index kept for the consent records (D-079), one `contact_purged` audit row. The text stage's `privacy.purge` deletes the job's `intake.intake_answer` rows (D-1801). Nothing deletes ledger, audit, consent or terms-acceptance rows (D-1802). `privacy::api::delete_my_data(contact, jobs)` clears the vault entry, deletes those jobs' photos and returns `DELETE_CONFIRMATION`, the PRIV-011 sentence, in one transaction. A purge task's last failed attempt raises a High `privacy.purge_failed` alert (`server::alert_if_last`). T276 (D-2160, D-2161) closes D-1803's gap: `purge_photos` queues, in the rows' own transaction, one `privacy.photo_delete` task per deleted row, which deletes the sealed object through `StorageDelete` after the commit and is retried on a storage error; the daily `privacy.photo_orphan_sweep` lists `photos/` and queues the same delete a day later for every object with no row, the delete re-checking that no row names it; both are registered and first queued by the worker over the stream's store.

**What T096 built (D-1804 to D-1808).** `app/messaging/src/mode.rs` (TXT-021 to TXT-024, TXT-034, CON-033): `SendingNumber` (a checked `+1` number), `NumberState` (`Working`, `Suspended { since }`, `Restored { since }`: `suspend`, `provider_restored`, `switch_on`, which refuses `NotRestored` before the provider's restore, so only Casey turns texting back on after it, D-144), `LaunchChannel` and `Line` (`at_launch`; `sender` is the audience's one configured number while its switch is on and the number works, else none: there is no list of numbers, so no second number is ever used, TXT-022), `Lines` (homeowner and business lines apart, TXT-035), `send_on_line` (sends an outbox row through `optout::send_checked` with the `From` of the recipient audience's `sender`, and refuses `LineSendError::Paused` while that line is closed or the `ops` `texting` or `all` pause is on, OPS-003), `update_route` (`Text`, `Email`, `CallList` for a homeowner with no email whenever her line is paused, `PortalOnly` for a business with none, never a call to a business; D-1804), `emergency_plan` (`EmergencyFacts` in, `EmergencyPlan` out: while the homeowner line is paused a 911-list match gets the INT-004 text in the web chat and by email when on file, the banner, the critical alert unless the number opted out (optout's `messaging.opted_out_emergency` covers that message), and `voice_call` only while `Suspended` or `Restored` and waiting for Casey, for her own message sent since it began, with the safety switch on, intake's `safety_call_trigger` and no opt-out; D-1805, D-1808), `raise_emergency` (in the caller's transaction: claims the inbox message once through `ops::api::audit_once`, so a retry does nothing; raises the critical `messaging.paused_emergency` alert; keeps `voice_call` only for the first such message per homeowner and suspension, a second `audit_once` claim; returns `Raised`) and `TollFree` (`configure` needs a recorded `Refusal` with a reference and Casey's go and refuses a paused homeowner number; `may_carry` refuses business templates; `launch_channel` is web-and-email until `verified`; D-1806). `Raised`'s alert and `voice_call` are the `alert` and `switch_on` the composition passes to intake's `enqueue_safety_call`, whose `calls.safety_call` event T080's handler places (`messaging` may not call `intake`, D-491). Not here (D-1807): the stored lines, suspension state and launch-channel setting with their owner-portal switches, the INT-004 email notice and the banner write, and the composition that runs the plan on an inbound message. Tests: `tests/integration/tests/web_email_mode.rs` (TD-TXT-08, TD-TXT-09, TD-TXT-12, TD-TXT-16, TD-TXT-17, TD-CON-16).

**What T097 built (D-1809 to D-1812).** `app/jobs/src/followups.rs` (JOB-015, JOB-016, JOB-028, TXT-029; D-071, D-222, D-240). `jobs::api::schedule_check_ins(db, job, released, visit)` queues one `jobs.check_in` task per check-in (`check_in_times`: day 3 from the later of the release and the earliest visit, day 14 from the release, a day 3 not before day 14 dropped; payload and dedupe key carry the job's version, D-1812); `run_check_in`, registered in `server`, queues the `check_in_day3` or `check_in_day14` homeowner-text event (dedupe key with the version) only while the job is Connected at that version, so after ONGOING, OTHER, DONE, any close or a later connection no check-in of the old one goes, and a second connection gets its own; a gone job does nothing. LATER restarts no timer: JOB-026's backstop already ends 7 days after day 14. `parse_answer` reads DONE or LISTO, NO, PROBLEM or PROBLEMA (words may follow), LATER or MÁS TARDE, ONGOING or CONTINUO, OTHER or OTRO; `after_answer` says where each leads: DONE and ONGOING to Done with the one `feedback_request` text, OTHER to Closed with no no-show even under email contact, NO by `connected_move`, PROBLEM to Closed with the TXT-029 `problem_reply`, LATER nothing (day 3: the day-14 check-in asks again; day 14: JOB-026's backstop). `answer_check_in(db, job, (answer, check, contact), Inbound {inbox, safety}, (at, actor))` makes the move guarded on Connected with its text in one transaction and, for PROBLEM, raises one `ops.alert` naming the `messaging.inbox` row: `jobs.problem_report` (the weekly digest) or, when the caller's `businesses::api::has_safety_word` matched, `jobs.problem_report_safety` (the OPS-032 push); severity normal, no fee or ledger change; a job no longer Connected writes nothing. Not here (D-1811): the `check_in_day3`, `check_in_day14` and `feedback_request` templates and the sender's list (`quotes`' `NOT_UPDATE_TEXTS` and `intake`'s `JOB_TEXT_TEMPLATES`, owned by other tasks; `problem_reply` is missing from `JOB_TEXT_TEMPLATES` too, so the TXT-029 reply is queued but not sent yet), the release's call to `schedule_check_ins`, the inbound route's call to `answer_check_in`, and MORE. Tests: `tests/integration/tests/followups.rs` (TD-JOB-04, TD-JOB-08, TD-JOB-09, TD-TXT-13).

**What T230 built (D-1820 to D-1823).** The homeowner's private feedback page (JOB-017; D-083, D-466, D-493). `migrations/0139_jobs_feedback.sql`: `jobs.feedback`, one row per job: the words (1 to 2,000 characters), the share tick as sent (default false), `received_at`, and later only `removed_reason` (one of `personal_details`, `abusive`, `fake`, `unrelated`, allowed only on ticked feedback) with `removed_at`, and `author_kind` (`owner`, `owner_relative`, `business`); the app role may update only those three columns and never delete, and a trigger (`jobs.feedback_marks_stay`) keeps a removal once set and never lets an author mark be cleared (D-1823). The body is the homeowner's own unscrubbed free text. There is no rating or tone column. `jobs::api::feedback` (written in `app/jobs/src/api.rs`, since a query may name only its own module's schema): `store` (only while the job is Done, one per job, returns the new ID), `given`, `remove` (once, ticked only, with a `feedback_removed_<reason>` audit row in its transaction), `mark_author` (audited `feedback_author_marked`) and `shown` (ticked, not removed, no author mark; nothing else filters it, so a negative one is shown like a positive one). `JobStatus::takes_feedback` is Done. `portals::api::feedback_routes` serves `GET` and `POST /f/{token}` (`app/portals/src/feedback.rs`, `templates/feedback.html`), opened by the job's quote-page link token while the job is Done and within PICK-011's 30 days, else a bare 404; one text box and one unticked share box with JOB-017's label, no reward; `noindex`, `no-store`, the cross-site refusal and the pre-session CSRF token; an empty or too long send keeps the words and tick and shows a focused alert; a repeat send shows the thanks and stores nothing, and a job that left Done after the page opened is a 404 (D-1823). `/static/portals/feedback.css` (a `NOT_PAGES` line in `tests/a11y/fixtures.ts`) draws the share box with a dark edge, because a native checkbox reports no edge colour to the A11Y-007 check (D-1820). Not here (D-1821): the `feedback_request` text and its `/f/<token>` link, the askhava.com section that reads `shown` with each writer's first name and town, the owner-portal controls for `remove` and `mark_author`, and the 3-year text purge and "Delete my data" (PRIV-009, PRIV-011) of feedback words. Tests: `app/portals/src/feedback.rs` `tests` and `tests/e2e/feedback.spec.ts` (TD-JOB-10).

**What T231 built (D-2414 to D-2418).** The day-3 and day-14 check-ins by email with one button per answer (TXT-034's emailed-buttons clause, SEC-026, D-493). `messaging::api` (`app/messaging/src/checkin_email.rs`): `CHECK_IN_ANSWERS` (`done`, `ongoing`, `later`, `no`, `other`; PROBLEM has no button, the email asks her to reply with what went wrong, D-2415), `CHECK_IN_DAYS`, `check_in_code` and `parse_check_in_code` (`<day>-<answer>`, e.g. `3-done`), `answer_label` (English and draft Spanish), `CheckInSender`, `render_check_in` (the SEC-028 layout, one link per answer to `https://<domain>/c/<token>/<code>`, the same links in the plain part) and `queue_check_in_email` (one T094 email-outbox row per contact, job number and day under an `email.send` key, in the caller's transaction, the one that issues the quote-page link token, SEC-037). `portals::api::checkin_routes` serves `GET` and `POST /c/{token}/{answer}` (`app/portals/src/checkin.rs`, `templates/checkin.html`): the job's quote-page link token while it is live (PICK-011), else a bare 404, as is an unknown code; `GET` shows the answer and one Send button and changes nothing; only the `POST` with the pre-session CSRF token (`ButtonForm`) records it through `jobs::api::answer_check_in`, once (a job no longer Connected shows the closed line and records nothing); `noindex`, `no-store`, behind `refuse_cross_site`. `app/server/src/checkin.rs` gives it the system clock and `build_router` merges it. The page answers with `Contact::Call` and the system actor, since no module stores the homeowner's contact choice yet (D-2416). Not here (D-2417): the composition that, in web-and-email mode, turns a due `jobs.check_in` task into this email instead of the text (finding her contact and language through `intake`, issuing the token, calling `queue_check_in_email`) and its worker registration, which waits on the footer postal setting as `SafetySender` does (D-2186); the page in Spanish (D-1236). Tests: `checkin_email::tests` and `checkin::tests` (TD-TXT-31), `tests/e2e/checkin.spec.ts`, and `tests/security/tests/auth_matrix.rs` holds `/c/{token}/{answer}` to the link door (D-2429).

**What T147 built (D-1825 to D-1829).** Business notices by the business's chosen channels (BIZ-019, BIZ-036, BIZ-037, CARD-019, CARD-020, QH-005, TXT-035 to TXT-037, PRIV-027, PICK-024). `migrations/0141_business_notices.sql`: `businesses.notice_channel` (one row per business: `email`, `text` or `both`, `changed_at`; no row is email; no DELETE for the app role). `app/businesses/src/channel_preference.rs`: `set_notice_channel` (text or both only while the business-texting switch is on), `notice_channel`, `notice_recipients` (T220's recipients with each person's mailbox, email flag and linked phone line IDs). `app/messaging/src/business_notices.rs`: `business_notice`, the `messaging.business_notice` handler: held whole to 8 a.m. Arizona time outside the window by re-queueing itself; texts only through the business line's open sender and the composition's `SendChecks` (`may_text`: `businesses::api::may_text`, read at run time, else email; `accept_by`: `picks::api::notice_sent`, D-1828); `{time}` set at the send (a card's `quote_hours` from the send, so a held card counts from 8 a.m.; a pick's "Accept by"; a null time picks the `_untimed` template); a `{link}` notice with no job number fails; one email per person in the email outbox (subject and words from the template, a card's CARD-020 subject, card text, WORD-002 statement and one job-page link, no image); `{link}` is the per-person signed sign-in link `sign_in_link` (D-1761); `card_notice_accepted` for CARD-019. `app/quotes/src/card_notices.rs`: `queue_card_notice` (welcome gate, recipients, one task per job and business) and `sent_count`. Not wired into the worker yet, and the gaps of D-1826 remain. Tests: `tests/integration/tests/business_notices.rs`.

**What T196 built (D-1846 to D-1850).** Verified notice addresses and the address-change protections (BIZ-040, BIZ-055, SEC-024, SEC-025, SEC-038) and the identity episode (D-1712, D-1756). `migrations/0142_businesses_email_verification.sql`: `businesses.mailbox_check` (one row per mailbox to verify: member or none for the sign-up email, the change's member, replaced mailbox and requester, whether a code is out, send and try-window times, wrong tries, verified, first soft bounce and returned times; no address, no DELETE), `team_member.step_up_at`, and the episode columns on `businesses.signup`. `app/businesses/src/email_verification.rs`: the sign-up email check (a keyed 6-digit code, never stored) that `identity::settle` and `approve` wait for; `verified_by_link`; `request_change` (owner, or a member for their own address, within 5 minutes of a Verify code not in the future, audit-logged with the acting member; a newer change closes the older; the same mailbox re-verifies a returned one) and `confirm` (only while the member is current: the mailbox moves, the old address is told by a `businesses.notice_address_changed` task, one transaction; a right code clears the returned mark; wrong tries carry over resends within 30 minutes); `may_get_notices`; `mark_returned` (hard bounce, complaint, or soft bounces 14 days apart; one `businesses.notice_address_returned` task, no call). `app/businesses/src/identity.rs`: `send_code_seen` keys each Verify send and letter code by the episode, raised only when a step's destination changed; 5 wrong codes per episode, each try taken by one guarded `UPDATE` before the check. `app/portals/src/notice_settings.rs` with `templates/notice_settings.html`: the notice page and owner-only `change_channel`, audit-logged with the acting member; the route is served with the business portal. Not wired yet: the email sender's handlers for the two tasks and its `may_get_notices` check, the webhook's call to `mark_returned`, and the sign-up route's `start_signup_check` (a sign-up with no check row is not held, D-1846). Tests: `tests/integration/tests/email_verification.rs`, `tests/security/tests/notice_addresses.rs`.

**What T253 built (D-1891 to D-1895).** The released view (PICK-022 to PICK-024, PRIV-003, PRIV-005, SEC-046; D-1739): `migrations/0129_picks_release_binding.sql` (`picks.release.contact_id`, `mail_chosen`), `app/privacy/src/released_reader.rs` (`read_released`), `app/picks/src/released_view.rs` (`released_view`, `may_view`, `Seat`, `ViewFacts`, `ViewAnswer`), `BusinessSessionProof` and `SessionMint::business` in `privacy::api`, `BusinessPrincipal::session_proof` in `portals`, `server::session_minter()`; section 8 has the flow. Each shown view adds its audited read rows (and their `ops.offdb_stream` copies) and nothing else. Tests: `app/picks/src/tests/released_view.rs` (the rule, pure and on the database through the crate-private `viewable`), `privacy`'s `realm_tests` (the read with a business proof), `app/server/tests/released_view.rs` (the real mint; an unknown pick), `tests/security/tests/vault_readers.rs`, `tests/security/ui/forge_session_proof_business.rs` (TD-PICK-48). No test runs a shown view end to end with the real mint, because `server` may seed no module's schema and no API makes a paid release yet (D-1895). Not built: the portal route that renders it (T085's `job_release.rs`).

**What T085 built (D-1896 to D-1900).** The business portal home, the step-up gate and the released job's page (BIZ-013, BIZ-025, BIZ-035, CARD-022, PICK-024, SEC-038; D-480, D-491, D-492, D-1739). `migrations/0133_businesses_open_cards_times.sql`: `businesses.open_cards_time`, one row per team member who changed the default (8 a.m., noon, 5 p.m. ticks, at most two by a check; no row is 5 p.m.; no DELETE). `app/businesses/src/open_cards_times.rs`: `OpenCardsTime`, `OpenCardsTimes`, `open_cards_times` (T198's composer reads it through `businesses::api`) and `set_open_cards_times` (the person themselves, a current member only); `businesses::api::has_fresh_step_up` (the SEC-038 5-minute rule over `team_member.step_up_at`). `app/portals/src/business_portal.rs` with `templates/business_home.html`: `require_step_up` (every SEC-038 change calls it first; a refusal is one `authz_denied` security event), `SensitiveChange`, `YourNumbers` and `business_home_page` (the panel and the person's own times form), `save_open_cards_times`. `app/portals/src/job_release.rs` with `templates/job_release.html`: `job_release_page` reads `picks::api::released_view` with the principal's `BusinessSessionProof` on every load and keeps nothing; a refusal is `None` (404), a purged contact the removed line; the details line prints the first name once and ends with the PICK-024 tip; an email release adds "Homeowner chose email. Email first; calls may not be heard."; the call log is the composition's `CallAttempt` list with no number. Not wired yet (D-1897, D-1898): the routes (served with the business portal, D-1728), the ledger and jobs counts that fill `YourNumbers`, and the call-log reader joining `calls::api::call_log` with the missed-call text's state. Tests: `tests/security/tests/step_up.rs`, `app/businesses/src/tests/open_cards_times.rs`, the render tests in both portal files, `tests/e2e/business_portal.spec.ts`.

**What T195 built (D-1912 to D-1916).** The business billing and complaints pages (BIZ-015, BIZ-029, SEC-038; D-200, D-480, D-491, D-492). `app/portals/src/business_portal.rs` with `templates/business_billing.html`: `billing_page` shows a `payments::api::Statement` (the ledger only): the totals (introduction fees charged, free first wins, refunds, credits), one row per business entry (Ask Hava's own processing, countering and payout entries left out; a dispute without the $15 dispute fee, as `MoneyView` splits it, D-1912; refunds, credits and disputes negative in the rows and CSV, so the amount column sums to the net paid, D-1915), the saved cards (brand and last four, `PaymentMethod`) and the monthly limit; `statement_csv` is the only statement format (no PDF in V1). `change_fee_limit` and `change_saved_card` check the owner or "Confirm and billing" role, then `require_step_up`, then call `payments::api::set_fee_limit` or `save_card` (D-1913). `templates/business_complaints.html`: `complaints_page` over `businesses::api::complaints` (kind and date, no job and no homeowner detail; the box while `now` is before 5 days) and `reply_to_complaint` through `businesses::api::reply` for any current member (D-1914); a refused reply (`RefusedReply`) is summarized in its own words (field words for an empty or over-long reply, a closed-box line otherwise), linked to the box and repeated by it with the words kept only while the box shows (D-1916). Not wired yet: the routes `BILLING_ROUTE`, `STATEMENT_ROUTE`, `COMPLAINTS_ROUTE` (served with the business portal, D-1728), the saved-card and fee-limit readers that fill the page, and the Verify-code entry before a billing change. Tests: `business_portal::tests` (TD-BIZ-04, TD-BIZ-09, TD-SEC-17 billing cases), `tests/e2e/business_billing.spec.ts` (signed-out edge).

**What T086 built (D-1917 to D-1921).** Owner sign-in (SEC-005 to SEC-007, SEC-043, SEC-044; D-098, D-480). `app/portals/src/owner_auth.rs` with its queries in `models.rs` and migration `0135_portals_owner_auth.sql` (`portals.owner`, `owner_factor`, `owner_recovery_code`, `owner_session`, `owner_challenge`; the app role cannot insert an owner): `owner_sign_in` checks the argon2id password and a TOTP code, a passkey answer (webauthn-rs; `static/passkey.ts` runs the browser ceremony) or a recovery code (only with two factors registered), then starts a 12-hour owner session; `owner_principal` ends one idle for more than 30 minutes; `require_owner_step_up` and `owner_step_up` hold the 5-minute fresh factor for the SEC-043 actions; `owner_sign_out` and `sign_out_everywhere`; `change_owner_password` checks 15 characters and the offline list `app/portals/data/breached_passwords.txt`; `add_owner_totp` and the passkey registration functions need a fresh factor; `owner_power` is the only gate for pause, refund and release (SEC-006). `server bootstrap-owner` (in `app/server/src/main.rs`) creates the one owner once as the migration role (D-1918). Not wired yet: the owner routes and `templates/owner_signin.html`'s page (D-1921). Fix round: an owner session's `portals.session.expires_at` follows the 30-minute idle end (never past 12 hours), so `current_session` and `Session::owner_proof` follow the idle rule; a recovery code is never a fresh factor; a password change ends the other owner sessions; the passkey challenge is issued by `POST` on `OWNER_PASSKEY_ROUTE`, never by the page's `GET` (D-1917 to D-1919).

**What T087 built (D-1934 to D-1939).** The homeowner portal (SEC-008, PRIV-012, PRIV-021, PRIV-031, SEC-046, CON-035, A11Y-032). `app/portals/src/homeowner_portal.rs`: `send_sign_in_code` texts a Verify code only to a homeowner number on file (`privacy::api::find_by_phone`) and answers the same either way (a failed vault read is logged and is an error); the code page carries the typed number to `HOMEOWNER_CODE_ROUTE`; `finish_sign_in` starts the 30-day homeowner session (`HOMEOWNER_SESSION_LIFE`) on her vault contact only after Verify says the code is right, a wrong code being an `auth_failed` event with the contact's ID; `OnFile::read` opens only the phone of the contact her `HomeownerSessionProof` names (`privacy::api::read_own_contact`); `turn_texts_off` lists the number through `messaging::api::suppress` with channel `portal`, confirmed on screen by `TEXTS_OFF_LINE`, never by text; `homeowner_page` renders `templates/homeowner_home.html` (sign-in, code, home, request-code screens; one `one-time-code` field per code screen; no identity-number or file field). `app/portals/src/data_download.rs`: `download_file` (JSON with `requests`, `quotes`, `messages`, `consent`, her contact's items only), `download_file` masks phone numbers, email and web addresses in quotes and messages; `open_request` (a `portals.data_<kind>_requested` audit row whose ID is the request's and whose time is its received time, then a Verify code to the number on file), `confirm_request` (at `DATA_CODE_ROUTE`; acts only on her own stored, unanswered request within its due date and a correct code: one `portals.data_<kind>_confirmed` row per request, claimed with `ops::api::audit_once`, and for a delete `privacy::api::delete_my_data` over her own jobs from `intake::api::jobs_of_contact`, in one transaction), `DataRequest::due_at` (stored received time plus `ANSWER_WITHIN`, 30 days; D-1939). Not served yet: the `/h` routes wait on T089's Verify send caps (D-1934); address and language changes wait on a vault update function and a stored language (D-1935).

**What T088 built (D-1945 to D-1948).** The authorization matrix (TD-SEC-01, TD-BIZ-31; SEC-004 to SEC-006, SEC-008, SEC-019, SEC-048, BIZ-041), tests only: `tests/security/tests/auth_matrix.rs` reads the route table from the source (every one-line `.route(` call in `app/*/src`, constants resolved, test modules and `/__test/` left out, plus every `*_ROUTE` or `*_PATH` constant naming a path, served or not), gives each path a door (link, asset, webhook, sign-in, homeowner, business; a path with none fails the run), and tries each route as anonymous, homeowners A and B, businesses X and Y and the owner: link routes over HTTP through the merged portal routers, webhooks through `verify_webhook`, unserved homeowner and business routes through the realm guard their handler must call first. Every refused request to a served route is checked to be one `csrf_rejected` event with route template, request ID and session ID. No path, template or static file carries a push route, service worker, manifest or "Turn on job alerts". A new route family needs its door in that file before it is served, and a served `/h` or `/b` route fails the run until a `harness` task extends the matrix to probe it over HTTP (D-1945). Known limits: homeowner A versus B isolation is proven for the `/q` and `/f` tokens only, not `/h`; webhook signature failures and realm-guard refusals are not security events yet, so SEC-048 is only partly met (owners T256, T259, the route-serving tasks and T204, D-1948).

**What T348 built (D-2440, D-3089 to D-3093).** The authorization matrix's public, owner and action-link doors and the HTTP probe of every served realm row (TD-SEC-61; SEC-019, SEC-034, SEC-048, SEC-004 to SEC-008, SEC-026), tests only. `tests/security/tests/auth_matrix.rs`: the route table also records whether the `site` module serves each path; new doors `Public` (`/healthz`, `/help` and below, `/terms`, `/privacy`, `/sms-terms`, `/your-data`, `/business-data`, and any `site` route outside a realm prefix, D-3089), `ActionLink` (`/b/a/`, before the business door) and `Owner` (`/owner` and below, its sign-in paths staying `SignIn`); the served-realm failure is now a demand that every served path of the homeowner, business, owner, action-link and public doors has a `yes` row in section 13. `app/server/tests/door_matrix.rs` (D-736: a test crate may not depend on `server`) probes those `yes` rows over HTTP through `build_router` (every vendor setting fake) on a real test database, with the six actors: homeowners on their own vault contact, businesses signed in through `portals`' emailed-link sign-in, the owner a plain owner session; `{pick}`, `{complaint}`, `{kind}`, `{object}` and `{token}` filled from fixtures made through module APIs (a carded and quoted job per business, her request and page pick, a complaint, a Quote action link; D-3090), any other parameter failing the row by name. SEC-048 events are read from their `security event` log line through a capturing `tracing` subscriber, since `server` code may not name `portals.security_event` (TD-SEC-10, D-3091). Planted correct and leaking pages on a test-local router prove the judging code. Both files assert the shared case list `tests/security/door_cases.txt`. Zero rows are served under the five doors today, so the probe runs no route until a serving task flips its row to `yes`.

**What T089 built (D-1950 to D-1954).** The rate limits (SEC-014, SEC-015, SEC-020, SEC-040, PICK-055). `app/intake/src/limits.rs`: `Limit` (scope, ceiling, hour or Arizona-day `Window`), `Key` (IP, record ID, everyone), `admit` (counts each pair through `core::api::rate_limit_hit`, narrowest first, stops at the first full one, and raises one `ops` alert per scope and window on the first refusal, High except the Normal per-phone one, D-1953), `admit_new_request` (5 per phone per hour, 10 per web IP, 40 for all numbers per hour). `app/portals/src/limits.rs`: `admit_sign_in_link`, `admit_code_try` (per IP and per business, in front of T272's per-member and per-link caps, D-1837), `admit_code_send` (per IP, 5 per number on file per Arizona day, the global Verify threshold `verify_all`; an unknown number spends two `CODE_SENDS_UNKNOWN` writes instead, so both do three, D-1954), `admit_owner_password` (per IP and the account; the passkey path calls none), `admit_sign_up` (5 per IP, 50 a day), `admit_quote`. Every counter is a `core.rate_limit` row; the client IP comes from `ClientIpPolicy::client_ip`. The callers are the routes' owners: the served Twilio inbound route gets `admit_new_request` from T256, the unserved portal routes when served (D-1951).

**What T148 built (D-1955 to D-1959).** The email action links for every former text keyword and the pages they open (BIZ-028, BIZ-032, BIZ-033, BIZ-038, CALL-011, PICK-006, PICK-039, PICK-040, FEE-043, SEC-025, SEC-026, A11Y-023, A11Y-024). `app/portals/src/action_links.rs`: `ActionKind` (Quote, Confirm, Hold, Away, Pause, Resume, Busy, Link this phone, Approve, Ask about a charge; `allowed` by role, `use_by` link alone, link and code, or session, `link_text` naming the action and job), `mint_action_link` (256 random bits; only the SHA256 of `action:<kind>:<object>:<token>` is stored in `portals.login_link`, created `LINK_LIFE` back so it never counts toward the sign-in limit; a Hold link only when `picks::api::hold_allowed` passes), `open_action_link` (reads only), `use_action_link` (a Hold's pick facts re-checked by `hold_allowed` first, then one guarded update, then the member's current roles, in the caller's transaction; `ActionGrant` has private fields and no `Clone`), `session_may`, `email_reply` (never acts, BIZ-032 help and charge flags), `OPEN_IN_BROWSER_LINE`, `ACTION_ROUTE` (not served yet). `app/portals/src/business_actions.rs` over `templates/business_actions.html`: `action_page` (what it does, an end time, one CSRF button; Busy's day field with its error state; a Confirm page promises a code only without a session), `busy_days` (1 to 60), `busy_until_line`, `fee_lines` (the PICK-039 fee and basis per quote type through `payments::api::fee_for` and `period_fee_for`), `FactsForm`, `parse_facts` (every PICK-040 fact required, earliest visit with a part of day, optional minutes 1 to 600), `prefill` (all but the earliest visit), `quote_form_page` (an error summary and the same message by each field) (CARD-019 sent and quote counts, PICK-006 read-back), `action_email_text`, `ask_about_charge` (enqueues `help_request_opened` with business, charge and job IDs), `charge_answer`. Migration `0143_quotes_quote_minutes.sql` adds `quotes.quote.quote_minutes`; `quotes::api::record_quote_minutes` writes it. **What T197 built (D-1971 to D-1974).** Confirm by email alone (PICK-019, PICK-037, PICK-043 to PICK-046, BIZ-033, BIZ-039). `app/picks/src/confirm.rs` adds `EmailOutcome` and `bounce_grace` (every email of the pick notice bounced or failed, asked before the end: `bounce_grace_minutes`, 60, once; spam counts as sent; never on an emergency) and two `WindowRules` lengths, `reminder_minutes` (60) and `bounce_grace_minutes` (60). `app/picks/src/confirm_reminders.rs`: `reminders` (every `reminder_minutes` business minutes from the notice's send, strictly before the current end, the final one last; a time at 8 p.m. waits for 8 a.m.; each carries the HOLD's end while `hold_allowed` passes), `emergency_reminders` (PICK-044's one, no Hold line), `Reminder::due`, `window_open` (the end itself is lapsed), `reminder_lines` (subject and Hold line, en and es), `lapse_plan` and `lapse_text` (PICK-046: the other businesses' quotes only, at once on an emergency, else under quiet hours), `owner_told` (the confirming person is not the owner) and `member_confirmed_line` (BIZ-033; a $0 fee says none was due), and the once-records `claim_reminder` and `extend_once` over `migrations/0144_picks_confirm_reminder.sql` (`picks.confirm_reminder`, `picks.window_extension`, append-only).
