The architecture test's own helpers (a TOML reader, `regex`, `walkdir` or `cargo_metadata`) are pinned by T175 under the new-library rule and added to `kit/allowed_crates.txt` in the same decision (D-481). `Ci` caches the Cargo registry and `target/` with `Swatinem/rust-cache` pinned by commit SHA, keyed on `Cargo.lock` and the toolchain, with `CARGO_INCREMENTAL=0` and `[profile.dev] debug = "line-tables-only"`; the per-crate clippy runs are dropped in favour of the single `cargo build -p askhava-server` boundary build; the audit tools install prebuilt and pinned; coverage runs in its own parallel job (`Coverage`; `Python` runs the pytest suite, `Checks` the rest, `E2e` the Playwright journeys, and `Ci` is the gate over all four, D-694, D-1282, D-2782); every Rust build cache in `ci.yml`, `twin.yml` and `a11y.yml` and the sqlx-cli cache are restored on every run and saved only on `main`, where nightly-main calls the three workflows, so branch runs no longer evict `main`'s entries from the repository's 10 GB cache, and the plan scripts read YAML with libyaml's `CSafeLoader` when PyYAML has it (D-2782); a warm run must finish in 15 minutes or less, and the Actions-minutes budget is confirmed with Casey before T001 (D-481).

**Resolution and audit run in this session:** a scratch workspace with every crate above at its exact version, on Rust 1.99.0, resolved 401 crates and built (the full 19-crate skeleton trial of D-488 resolved 421 external packages with the same pins, `docs/audit/rust-skeleton-trial-2026-10-29.md`); `cargo clippy --workspace --all-targets -- -D warnings` passed. `cargo audit` (0.22.2, 1,381 advisories) found no vulnerabilities and one unmaintained warning: RUSTSEC-2026-0374, `basic-toml`, used only at compile time by `askama_derive` to read Askama's config. `cargo deny check licenses bans sources advisories` (0.20.2) passed with the license list above, crates.io as the only source, wildcard versions denied except workspace paths, and that one advisory ignored with its reason; duplicate versions are warnings only. T009 writes this `deny.toml` and re-runs both (cargo-audit reads the whole `Cargo.lock`, optional crates included, so `.cargo/audit.toml` ignores RUSTSEC-2026-0459 for `libsqlite3-sys`, which no build includes, D-2144); `Ci` runs `cargo audit` (fails on any vulnerability) and `cargo deny` on every PR. The scratch workspace is not committed. npm packages are audited when T020 writes `package-lock.json`.

No Web Push crate is pinned: the installable business app with push is a later, optional phase (BIZ-041, D-307), and the spec change that adds it picks the crate under the policy above and logs its purpose, license and audit result.

## 12. Keeping this file true

- A new module, table, cross-module call, status, timeout, adapter or pinned version changes this file in the same PR, with a `docs/decisions.md` entry when it is a choice (`BUILD_BRIEF.md:489`).
- **Build order and file ownership** live in `kit/tasks.json` (K6): each build task names the module and the files it owns (`app/<module>/src/…`, its own `migrations/0NNN_<slug>.sql`, its own test files), and `python3 scripts/validate_tasks.py` fails if two tasks that can run in either order own the same file, or if a non-`harness` task owns a protected path (D-111, D-118). The shared files are this file, `docs/decisions.md`, `docs/for-casey-review.md`, `app/server/src/main.rs`, the workspace `Cargo.toml`, `Cargo.lock`, `.sqlx/`, `package.json`, `package-lock.json` and every crate's `Cargo.toml` (D-453): a crate can only list a path dependency once that crate exists, so the task that first needs `picks` to call `payments`, or the server or a test crate to use a new module, adds that line. A new outside crate still needs its decision entry and must be in the workspace table. The first task of each module creates its crate's `Cargo.toml` and `src/lib.rs`. Stage 2 builds layers 0 to 7 far enough for job cards, the quote page and the pick (including the license imports and business records that matching reads); later stages add to each module's own files in dependency order (D-112).
- A new library needs a decisions entry with purpose, license and a cargo-audit (or npm audit) result, and must pass cargo-deny (`BUILD_BRIEF.md:122`).
- The threat model (`docs/threat-model.md`) changes with any new trust boundary or asset.
- The not-a-contractor phrase list (WORD-022) lives in one place, `NOT_A_CONTRACTOR` in `scripts/trace_spec.py`; the spec check, the catalog check (`scripts/check_catalog.py`) and the app's wording scan (T104) all read it (D-360). It also holds the WORD-023 banned descriptions ("connect you with", "referral network/service", D-377).
- The legal document drafts live in `docs/legal/` (TERMS-028, D-389). `scripts/check_legal_docs.py` keeps them true to the spec: each key phrase it lists must appear word for word in its spec requirement and in its draft, and it reuses the banned-phrase list from `scripts/trace_spec.py`; `tests/test_check_legal_docs.py` runs it in `Ci`. When the site pages are built (T111, T112), the published terms and policy come from these drafts.
- Legal holds live in the catalog and its check: pest control, when on, is for Department of Agriculture licensees only and never advertised (LIC-012, D-402); sale, purchase and refinance inspections are fee-free job types (FEE-045, D-403); and no category is a warranty or protection plan (`scripts/check_catalog.py`, LIC-015, LIC-042, D-376).
- Fix round 13 adds three catalog files, all checked by `scripts/check_catalog.py`: `docs/catalog/license_map.yaml` (which license classes receive which job types, LIC-043, D-392), `docs/catalog/question_bank.yaml` (the intake question sets, LIC-040, D-394; the trade safety prompts were withdrawn, D-436) and, written by T154, `docs/catalog/intake_golden.yaml` (golden sample requests, OPS-040, D-395). `catalog` loads them; `licensing.class_map` (T153) decides coverage from the map; `intake.question_bank` and `intake.routing_confidence` (T156) ask the questions and route (the summary step was replaced by the INT-041 card approval in `intake.summary_step`, T208, D-434); `businesses.capability` (T155) pre-ticks the checklist and re-applies the map when a class changes (T207); `picks.backup_choice` (T157) and `picks.auto_confirm` (T209) handle the backup and the optional auto-confirm charge; `portals` serves the scorecard (T158) and intake-report (T210) pages from `payments::api` and `intake::api` figures that each module computes, and `payments.money_view` (T210) is the money page; `portals.push` (T211, with the installable app shell in T160) is the optional push channel. Each category has a `launch_open` flag (LIC-046, D-396). Each R-37R and R-39R subclassification row in the license map names a `scope`, and `scope_tags` tags every job type those rows can reach; `check_catalog.py` fails a subclassification given a job type outside its scope and any enabled licensed job type no license covers (LIC-035, CARD-001, D-504). `docs/catalog/matching_examples.yaml` holds the matching worked examples M01 to M16 (test-design section 7.2), the card-eligibility oracle written before any routing code; `check_catalog.py` fails a missing, duplicated or malformed example, a job type, class or agency the catalog or license map lacks, a `map_covers` the license map disagrees with, and a `card` outcome that breaks a necessary card condition (town, ticked job type, license cover, handyman limits, LIC-013's agency), and the app's matching tests (TD-LIC-02) and the Twin gate's license table are checked against it (D-110, D-506).
- The voice guide for texts and emails is `docs/voice.md` (WORD-024, D-406); the before-build list is `docs/PREBUILD.md` (D-416).
- Fix round 14 (D-429 to D-449): `docs/catalog/trade_kb/` holds the Grok trade knowledge base as the expert brief for intake (read by `catalog`, never deciding who gets a card); `categories.yaml` names each category's `quote_format` and `design` flag (PICK-053, D-433, D-441); `ai.writer` and `ai.output_checks` (T163) write and check the AI-reworded questions, the job card and the project file inside AI-019, falling back to the fixed templates; `quotes.job_page` and `quotes.update_texts` (T213) are the business job page and the short portal-link texts; `intake.order` (T162), `intake.photos` and `intake.design` (T212); `quotes.formats`, `quotes.notes` and `quotes.edit_withdraw` (T164), `picks.share_consent` and `picks.cant_take` (T214); `businesses.links` (T165) and `businesses.leaving` (T228, D-492), `businesses.pest_identity` and the OPS-041 coverage email, sent by `businesses` through `messaging::api` from `intake.recruiting_demand` events (T215, D-482). The concept-image provider is a new adapter chosen at the account checkpoint, receiving only the written project file; until then its switch is off. The safety-contacts file is withdrawn (D-436).
- Job-type kinds (LIC-041, D-605): the plumbing repair, heating and cooling (`hvac_repair` and `hvac_replacement`), electrical, roof repair and painting categories in `categories.yaml` map each job type to `repair`, `replace` or `install` under `job_type_kinds`, beside the `job_types` list; `scripts/check_catalog.py` fails a job type of those categories without a kind and any of the five without a repair job type. The kinds are what lets a business untick some job types of those trades at sign-up or later (BIZ-004).
- Fix round 15 (D-455 to D-467): no new module. A `g` source code on askhava.com links is recorded by `businesses` at sign-up with the optional "Where did you hear about Ask Hava?" answer (BIZ-053, T081) and by the web chat with the request (INT-047, T103), never reaching `catalog` routing or a card; `ops.scorecard` (T158) adds the D-458 measures; `jobs` timers (T032) send the TXT-042 all-quotes-in text and the JOB-012 closing note; `businesses.leaving` (T228, D-492) asks the BIZ-054 reason; the home-page numbers (WEB-028, T166) are computed from the ledger and audit log; the contact card and after-Done line (PICK-054) belong to `picks` (T055). Every enabled category is launch-open (D-459).
- Design (D-451): `docs/design/DESIGN_SPEC.md` sets the look for `site`, `portals` and emails; `site` serves the self-hosted fonts (T166) and the plaster, brass and paper textures (T216) from `app/site/static/`, and `tests/a11y/design_tokens.spec.ts` checks every color pair against the spec's contrast values.
- AI smell (D-452): `docs/ai_smell.yaml` lists the words, patterns and page habits that read as machine-made; `scripts/check_ai_smell.py` scans the spec's quoted copy, the voice guide, the question bank, the design reference and everything customer-facing under `app/` (templates, message files, stylesheets), and `tests/test_check_ai_smell.py` runs it on every push, so a hit fails `Ci`. Every task prompt adds a cold read of changed copy (WORD-028).
- Code documentation (D-473): `docs/code-docs.md` is the standard a session that has never seen the code relies on. Every crate keeps a `CLAUDE.md` (purpose, what it owns, rules with requirement IDs, never-break invariants with their tests, change recipes, tests) as a shared path; every Rust file opens with a `//!` comment; every public item has a `///` comment (the workspace lints deny `missing_docs`, T009); every finished build task names its requirements in its own files. `scripts/check_code_docs.py` checks this and `tests/test_check_code_docs.py` runs it in `Ci`. `docs/README.md` (T141) is the start-here map, and T171's cold-start drill tests the docs with a fresh session before "Finished".
- Copy quality (D-474): `docs/copy/copy-panel.md` is the copy panel (eight writers' methods, the ten-test scorecard and the reviewer persona every task's cold read runs); `docs/copy/site-copy.md` holds the approved site wording, block by block; `docs/copy/voice-of-customer.md` holds real customers' phrases; `scripts/check_copy.py` checks sentence length, reading grade and superlatives in the site copy and the page templates, and `tests/test_check_copy.py` runs it in `Ci` (WORD-029, T161).
- Smoke test (D-476): `scripts/smoke.sh` is the smoke test every task runs first and before hand-in: the sources fingerprint, Ci's gitleaks scan over the files Ci sees when `gitleaks` is on the path (the backup runner installs the pinned release; elsewhere it says it skipped, D-582), every plan and wording check, the Python tests and, once the workspace exists, `cargo fmt`, clippy and `cargo test` against a local PostgreSQL 16 it starts with a fresh local-only password (`--full` adds cargo-audit and cargo-deny). `.gitignore` keeps `/target/` and `node_modules/` out of commits and out of the scanners. The pre-hand-in gate (D-1300): `scripts/prehandin.sh` runs on a task branch the `Ci` "Checks" steps smoke does not (the sources file count, every plan, wording and source-rule check, the skip guard, `check_plan_lock.py` and `check_pr_change.py --base origin/main --branch <branch>`), and without `--quick` eslint and `npm audit --audit-level=low` when the npm tree changed and cargo-audit and cargo-deny when Rust changed and both are on the path, by `Ci`'s own path rules; also without `--quick`, when the diff touches a crate with a floor in `tests/coverage_floor.json`, `cargo llvm-cov` on `Ci`'s pinned nightly over that crate and every workspace crate depending on it, judged on those floors by `scripts/coverage_floor.py`, the comparison `Ci`'s Coverage job now calls too (`select` and `compare`; a floor whose path has no file is never selected; a missing measurement is red: an empty report, a floor on disk the report does not measure or measures with zero lines, an absent `--only` prefix, and a floor not on disk unless the floor file's `future` list gives a reason, D-2096; skipped without the tools or `DATABASE_URL`, and past 360 s; `backup-runner.yml` keeps the instrumented build on `/mnt` or `/tmp`, so it is cold every job, D-1440, and installs cargo-llvm-cov and the nightly best effort, so a failed download there leaves the gate saying it skipped, while its gitleaks and `Ci`'s own downloads stay required and every release download in both workflows retries 5 times; gitleaks in both falls back to building the same pinned version from proxy.golang.org, checked by sum.golang.org and the pinned module hash `GITLEAKS_MOD_H1`, and must report that version, and `Ci`'s cargo-audit, cargo-deny and Coverage cargo-llvm-cov fall back to `cargo install --locked` of the same pinned versions from crates.io and must report them, D-1986); it does not run pytest, cargo or tsc (smoke does) or `Ci`'s workspace-total coverage. The worker runs smoke (polled every 4 minutes at most, D-1317), commits, then the gate. At `python3 -m runner --after`, `GitHubEnv.collect_result` runs main's copy of the gate with `--quick` (`prehandin`, `PREHANDIN_ROOT` pointing at a detached worktree of the submitted head, `PREHANDIN_TIMEOUT` 240 s) after the decision entries are put in order and before any review record or push; exit 1 turns the hand-in into `tests_failed` with the gate's last 30 lines, redacted and capped at 3,000 characters (`PREHANDIN_RED`), and any other exit (3 is the gate's own "cannot check": no base or no merge base), a timeout, a missing gate on main or a worktree git cannot make is logged and lets the hand-in through; a check script the head lacks is skipped, and the output is also redacted with `scripts/pii_scan.py`'s key, phone and email patterns (`GATE_REDACT`). `runner/__main__.py` `hold_red_gate` then holds a red hand-in for up to 2 fix rounds per hand-off (`PREHANDIN_HOLDS`, counted in `.git/runner_handoff.json` with the last gate output): `cycle.after_handoff` writes nothing, the lease stays held and the task IN_PROGRESS, `--after` prints `runner: prehandin_red` and the output, and `backup-runner.yml` step 5b gives it to a new worker on the same branch; the third red fails the attempt with that output as `last_failure`, and a session that ends without handing in leaves the kept output in the safety step's failure. The hand-off carries `hand_in_by` (the lease's expiry less 30 minutes, `runner/__main__.py` `hand_in_by`), which `backup-runner.yml` step 3 passes to the worker as its deadline (D-1440).
- Compiled-in files (D-1867, D-2248): `Ci` builds Rust only when the diff matches its path rules (the Checks and Coverage rules in `.github/workflows/ci.yml`, `RUST_PATHS` in `scripts/prehandin.sh`, and the E2e rule), so the files the Rust code pulls in by path are named there: `docs/legal/`, `docs/catalog/`, `schemas/`, `docs/architecture.md`, `docs/copy/mail-app-steps.md` and `scripts/pii_scan.py` (read by `tests/fixtures`), never all of `docs/`. `scripts/check_include_paths.py` runs in Checks on every push and in `prehandin.sh --quick`: every `include_str!`, `include_bytes!`, `include!` and `concat!(env!("CARGO_MANIFEST_DIR"), ...)` path in `app/` and the Rust test crates must resolve to an existing file inside the repository that all three rules match; a non-literal argument fails, and a path naming the repository root (a run-time base) is printed as a note. A new compiled-in folder therefore joins the rules in the same pull request.
- Governance (D-479): the layer table of section 4 is also `kit/layers.json` (protected), which T175's architecture test reads; the approved crates of section 11 are also `kit/allowed_crates.txt` (protected), which `scripts/check_crates.py` enforces in `Ci`; sections 3 to 6 of this file are fingerprinted in `kit/plan.lock` and change only in a harness task or a plan change with a decision entry (`docs/governance.md`). When a crate joins section 11 under the new-library rule, the same decision adds it to the allow-list.
- Context budgets (D-1036, D-1075): `scripts/gen_prompts.py` measures each prompt's `budget.context_pct` from the bytes of the prompt, its owned files and its reads (this file, read by almost every task, is the largest term; `docs/decisions.md` counts only through the entries a prompt pastes). A stored value is kept while it is within 3 points of the measurement and both are within the task's limit, so this file's growth does not rewrite every prompt; `--check --strict` fails a measurement over the limit or a stored value further off. `scripts/pii_scan.py` warns when `docs/decisions.md` passes 75% of its `MAX_BYTES`. A read of this file counts as its section 2, every section the prompt itself cites ("architecture section 8"), plus the lines that name the task's module (`<module>` in backticks or `app/<module>/`), since workers read only section 2 and line ranges; a task that owns it would count it whole (D-1350).
- STATUS.md dashboard (D-1462): `runner/status.py` renders the owner's view at the top of STATUS.md on `claude/kit-state`, which `GitHubEnv._state_files` writes with every state commit: a verdict line, build tasks done (T### ids that are not owner gates, as `scripts/progress.py`), the lowest stage with an open task and the recorded stage proofs, each held lane's task, claim time and lease expiry (or idle since the last attempt ended), the week reading and tokens left with the reset in UTC and Phoenix time, the age of the last `kit/runner_log.jsonl` line (a warning past 90 minutes), the keeper's `keeper` key ({`last_run_at`, `next_wake_at`}, optional in `kit/state_schema.json`, written by the keeper of D-1451; "unknown" while absent), then "Needs attention" (red `main`, blocks, plan gaps, deviations, the build hold, and `scripts/gen_prompts.py --check --strict` red on a task still to run, with problems on DONE tasks only counted; it runs once per runner process, which is every cycle and every `--after`, measured 1.25 s); each section that fails to render becomes one "unavailable" line, so a malformed state field never stops a cycle, a "Last 24 hours" table from each task's `history` (the retry reason is the first 120 characters of the task's `last_failure`, redacted as the pre-hand-in gate's rows are, on its latest attempt only) and the plan and harness merges on `main` in that day; the progress block and the older sections follow. It reads no homeowner data. `README.md` links it.
- Plan health (D-1350): `scripts/plan_health.py` checks a task's plan before it is claimed: (a) its measured context against its limit (`gen_prompts.context_bytes`, `validate_tasks.context_limit`); (b) every repository path its requirement sentences, test-design rows and `reads` name is owned by it, an ancestor or the shared list (`check_pr_change.task_paths_check`) or already exists, and every path a live decision it pastes names is owned by some task or exists (a superseded decision never holds); (c) each dependency is DONE or SKIPPED and each DONE dependency's owned files are on `main`, except a file the plan deleted: a DONE task that owns it lists it under the optional `kit/tasks.json` field `deletes` (checked by `validate_tasks.py` to be its own owned file) and no DONE owner that depends on that task brings it back (D-2922; the runner's owned-file check at a close, `cycle._owns`, leaves a task's own `deletes` out too). `runner/cycle.py` `plan_health_pass` runs it on every eligible candidate before the pacer picks one (dependency status left to `eligible`, which already allows a proof-waiting cleanup dependency): a task with problems stays QUEUED with `attempts` untouched, gets a `plan_gap` mark (`{at, problems}`, at most 10 problems kept, allowed by `kit/state_schema.json`) listed under "Needs attention" in STATUS.md, and the next sound task is claimed; a sound task loses a stale mark, so a plan fixed on `main` frees it with no state edit; a changed problem list keeps the first `at`; a task that stops being a candidate keeps its last mark until its next turn; a check that raises holds nothing and leaves the mark as it is, and a checker that cannot be built (`runner/__main__.py` `plan_health_checker`) leaves the runner without the check. `python3 scripts/plan_health.py --all` surveys every task not DONE, plus every DONE task's owned files. Check (d), pinned lists (D-2550), is report-only: `scripts/plan_pins.py` finds tests that pin a data file by exact count or id list, and route tests whose classifier panics on a path it does not name, and warns about a task whose goal adds rows to such a file or serves such a route when it may not edit the test (unless a dependency not yet DONE owns the test by name, or a pasted decision lets it edit it); `plan_health.py --all` or `--pinned` prints the warnings as `WARN` lines, `check_task` never returns them, so nothing is held and the exit code ignores them; D-2551 (reserved) makes them holding problems.
- Owned files at close (T285, D-1869, D-2351): when a merged pull request makes a task DONE (not an empty hand-in, not FIX-MAIN), `runner/cycle.py` `mark_owned_gap` reads `kit/tasks.json` at the merge commit and checks that every path the task owns exists there, except those it lists under `deletes` (D-2922) (a path ending in `/` must be a non-empty folder) and that no row of section 13 still reads the task in its Wired cell. A miss never un-merges and never holds the lane: the task stays DONE with an `owned_gap` mark ({`at`, `problems`}, at most `PLAN_GAP_PROBLEMS_KEPT` lines) and one outbox item (kind `blocked`, title `T###: owned file missing at close`); STATUS.md's Needs attention lists it (`runner/github_env.py` `_owned_gap_line`). Each cycle `recheck_owned_gaps` judges every marked DONE task against `main`'s head and `main`'s `kit/tasks.json`: the mark clears once nothing is missing (the file arrived, a plan change moved it or listed it under the task's `deletes`, the row reads `yes` or another task) and narrows to what remains; a git error leaves it as it is. The commit reads are the optional `Env.text_at` and `Env.files_at`; an `Env` without them skips the check.
- Keeper (D-1451): the backup runner wakes itself instead of waiting on GitHub's cron (24 firings in 123 hours, median gap 5 h). The last step of every `backup` job in `backup-runner.yml` (`if: always()`) asks `python3 -m runner.keeper arm` (`runner/keeper.py`, run from a detached worktree of `main` made at job start, so a task branch the session left checked out cannot supply an older runner) for `wake_after` over the state the cycle left and dispatches the workflow with `wake_after` and `lane`, then records the `keeper` key ({`last_run_at`, `next_wake_at`}, STATUS.md's keeper line, D-1462) with `python3 -m runner --keeper-armed <minutes>`. Minutes: a live lease's end + 2; the pacer's `waiting_until` + 5; the week's `usage.session_reading` at 97% or more: its `resets_at` + 5; 20 when a task is eligible or MERGE_PENDING and the cycle did not itself report `idle` or `fix_main`; else the soonest `retry_after` + 5, else 340 (this covers Gate P's `build_hold`, which holds the T### tasks); a failed read of the state or the lease other than a 404 is marked `{"unreadable": true}` and gives 20, never "nothing live"; every value capped at 340 (5 h 40 m, so a sleep fits GitHub's 6-hour job limit; the job's `timeout-minutes` is 350) and floored at 10; no keeper at all while `backup_runner` is off or `main` is red with FIX-MAIN escalated to a person (D-1099). A dispatched run with `wake_after` skips the gate (so no cycle) and runs only the `keeper` job (`concurrency: backup-runner-keeper`, `cancel-in-progress: true`, so one sleeper; a newer keeper replaces it): it sleeps, re-reads `claude/kit-state`, `claude/lease` and the workflow's runs and decides with `python3 -m runner.keeper wake`: any other run queued or in progress, a live lease, a pacer wait, the weekly stop or nothing to claim re-arms a new keeper (chained sleeps reach a wait longer than 340 minutes, the weekly reset among them); otherwise it dispatches an ordinary run, which goes through the gate, the lane pick, the backup job's concurrency group and the lease like any start; an unreadable run list stops it (the hourly cron at minute 37 stays the backstop). The keeper holds no lock. The smoke run's own target (D-1890, replacing D-1451's clean): `GitHubEnv.smoke_main` builds `main` in a detached worktree at a fixed path (`smoke_tree_dir`, `askhava-smoke` under `RUNNER_TEMP`) into its own target dir (`smoke_target_dir`, `target/smoke`, inside the `target` the Rust cache saves, D-1178; `CARGO_INCREMENTAL=0` as in `Ci`), so the worker's builds in the checkout's `target` never meet the smoke's member artifacts, which carry the smoke tree's path through `env!("CARGO_MANIFEST_DIR")` (T057's `fake_data`); no `cargo clean` runs after the smoke. Before the script runs, `pin_smoke_mtimes` gives every tracked file and every folder holding one the time recorded for its content in `target/smoke/smoke-mtimes.json` (a file's blob, a folder's entry names), or now when the content is new, so Cargo's mtime check rebuilds only the members a commit changed instead of all of them after every fresh checkout. A failed pin deletes the record and stamps the whole tree now (`stamp_tree_now`); a changed existing `.sqlx/` file makes every path new (an added or removed one does not, D-1911). `backup-runner.yml` caches `target/smoke` as its own entry, restored newest-first from the same commit then the same lock ("Smoke build cache, restore") and saved only when `smoke_main` left `target/smoke-ran` ("Smoke build cache, save", key with the run id, D-1911); the main entry lists `target/debug` only; the runner logs the manifest path and how many entries it loaded; when it has no dependency builds, `_seed_smoke_target` copies (never hard-links) the worker build's dependency artifacts into it first. Before the session, `backup-runner.yml`'s step "Warm the smoke build before the session" (D-2007; after the smoke cache restore and the prerequisites, `continue-on-error`, 25 minutes) runs `python3 -m runner --warm-smoke` (`GitHubEnv.warm_smoke`): the same tree at the checkout's `origin/main`, the same seed and pin, then only smoke.sh's compile steps (`warm_commands`: its toolchain install, clippy, `cargo build -p askhava-server` and `cargo test --workspace --no-run`, the flags held to smoke.sh's lines by `tests/test_runner_keeper.py`, `SQLX_OFFLINE=true`) within `WARM_TIMEOUT` (1,200 s, its process group killed past it), so the session's smoke re-pins the same content to the same times and finds the artifacts fresh; it leaves `target/smoke-ran` only when its cargo commands ran at least `WARM_SAVE_SECONDS` (60 s) and one exited 0 or timed out (so a job on a head already built saves no new cache entry), prints its result and writes no state, lease or log. The next step, "Run the start-routine smoke before the session" (D-2028; `continue-on-error`, 32 minutes), runs `python3 -m runner --pre-smoke` (`GitHubEnv.pre_smoke`): the same tree, seed and pin, then `bash scripts/smoke.sh` itself in the smoke's environment within `PRE_SMOKE_TIMEOUT` (1,500 s, its process group killed past it), with no 600-second Bash call, writing `target/smoke-pre.json` (`{sha, ok, timed_out, exit, seconds, at, job, last}`, `job` the run id and attempt) and leaving `target/smoke-ran` only on a timeout; in the session, `smoke_main` takes that result as its smoke when its `job` is this job and its `sha` is main's head as just fetched, else runs smoke.sh as before, so a red one is a red smoke and a timed-out one a smoke timeout (D-1970's chain unchanged), and the run's `smoke_runs` entry says `why` "<why> (pre-session)" with `seconds`. A green smoke already recorded on main's head stands for later start routines (D-2560): `state.reusable_smoke` allows it only while `main` is not red, when `head` was never the SHA of a red `main` (`main_red_history`), when the last `smoke_runs` entry for `head` is green and not timed out, no finished run on `head` was red and no `smoke_env_problems` entry names it, and when the green run it stands on is at most `SMOKE_REUSE_MAX_AGE` (6 hours) old; `cycle._start_smoke` never reuses while the job has its own smoke for the head (`own_smoke_for`: its pre-session or background result for the head, or its background smoke still running), and adds that kit/nightly.json is not red on `head` and `Ci`, `Twin` and `Ally` are not red on it (`_head_doubted`), records the reuse as its own entry (`why` "start routine (reused <at>)", `reused` the original run's time, no `seconds`) and otherwise smokes as before; clearing a red `main` (`clear_main_red`, governance 5.3) never reuses. Ancestry reuse (D-3058): with no own smoke and no same-head green, `cycle._ancestry_reuse` takes `main`'s first-parent chain from the head (`main_first_parents`, `git rev-list --first-parent`) and `state.ancestry_smoke` lets the nearest ancestor at most kit/runner.json `smoke_ancestry_reuse` commits back (3; 0 off; `Config.smoke_ancestry`) stand when it has a real green run (never a reuse of any kind) that passes `reusable_smoke` and is at most `SMOKE_REUSE_MAX_AGE` old, and every commit after it, the head included, is a DONE task's `merged_sha` (`done_merges`: not FIX-MAIN, not an empty hand-in) with no red or timed-out run, no `smoke_env_problems` entry and no `main_red_history` entry, and `_head_doubted` holds for none of them nor the base; never while `main` is red, so a real smoke runs at least every N+1 merges; any error runs the smoke. It is recorded as its own entry (`why` "start routine (ancestry reuse of <base>, <at>)", `reused` the base run's time, `base` the ancestor's SHA, allowed by `kit/state_schema.json`); `reusable_smoke` never reuses such an entry as is, so the chain is judged again. An own background smoke of the head still running with no result yields (D-3058) to a green on the head that passes the same-head reuse in full: `GitHubEnv.yield_own_smoke` stops it (`bg_stop`) and drops its record, and refuses when a result exists, also one written while it was being stopped, so a finished own result is always judged. The step "Read the recorded smoke runs for the pre-session smoke (D-2560)" saves kit/state.json from `claude/kit-state` (job token, read-only) to `$RUNNER_TEMP/pre_smoke_state.json`, and `--pre-smoke` (env `RUNNER_PRE_SMOKE_STATE`) runs nothing when that state already allows the reuse on the checkout's head, or an ancestry reuse (`_ancestry_line`, `smoke_ancestry` set from kit/runner.json, D-3058). When main moved after the pre-session smoke (the cause of every in-session timeout on November 10: a 540 s cap against a smoke of 326 to 573 s), the session's smoke runs detached (`RUNNER_SMOKE_BACKGROUND=1` on the session step; `GitHubEnv._background_smoke`): `start_background_smoke` starts `python3 -m runner --pre-smoke` for the new head (`RUNNER_PRE_SMOKE_HEAD`) in its own session, recorded in `target/smoke-bg.json` ({sha, job, pid, at}, output in `target/smoke-bg.log`), within `PRE_SMOKE_TIMEOUT`; each runner call waits for its result until `SMOKE_WAIT` (450 s) after the call began (`wait_from`, retries included) and, while it still runs, the cycle returns `smoke_pending` (nothing recorded or claimed, no strike, `main` untouched), which the session's prompt answers by running the runner again; a result is used like a pre-session one (`source` "background", `why` "<why> (background)"), and one that ended without a result is a timeout; one still running for an older head is stopped before the start routine judges a reuse (`settle_background_smoke`, `stop_background_smoke`: SIGTERM to its group and its smoke.sh's group, `target/smoke-bg-child`, then SIGKILL to every recorded group), and the step "Stop a background smoke before the cache save" stops one left at the session's end and skips that job's save. The checkout's own `target` still rebuilds every member once per job (fresh checkout mtimes).
- Model window (D-2225): `kit/runner.json` `model_window` = {`model` (the full ID the transcripts record, `claude-fable-5-1`), `alias` (the worker call's name, `fable`), `from`, `until` (null: open), `run_to_limit`, `end_on_limit`}, read at run time by `runner/model_window.py`; `kit/tasks.json` is never changed. Inside [from, until) a claim marks the task's entry `window_model` = {model, alias}; that attempt's worker, fix-round workers and reviewer run on it (the hand-off's `model_window` block, backup-runner.yml step 3d; `RUNNER_MODEL_ALIAS` for a worker command), `--after` checks the evidence against it (`cycle.worker_model`, `reviewer_model`, `known_models`) and the history and usage rows keep it; a retry outside the window drops the mark. The orchestrating session's `--model` comes from the step "Pick the session's model (D-2225)" (`python3 -m runner --session-model`, falling back to `claude-sonnet-5-5`). With `run_to_limit`, `scripts/pacing.py` (`assess(run_to_limit=True)`, `at_limit`) and `runner/keeper.py` (`week_stop(to_limit=True)`) drop the headroom, the ledger and the 97% stop and hold only for a usage-limit hit or a fresh real reading at 100%, until the earlier of its reset and its expiry. With `end_on_limit` the window closes at the first session reading taken inside it with the week at 100% or rejected (a `limit_hit` alone never closes it): `model_window.close_on_limit` records `model_window_ended` {model, from, at, why: "limit"}, drops the pacer's ledger `usage.windows` and writes the outbox item `model-window-ended`, before a claim (`cycle.close_window_on_limit`) and at `--usage-reading`; after that the window changes no model and `run_to_limit` stops, so pacing is back to its normal rules, and the keeper cuts its week stop and the pacer's wait at the reading's 6-hour expiry while the window is recorded ended. A session the limit stops mid-task is handed in by the safety step as `usage_limit` (`python3 -m runner.usage_reading --at-limit`), at no attempt cost. A refused model (the session's `--after --model-fallback`, or `--model-refused` over the execution file of a session that used no tool) records `model_window_refused` {model, from, at, why} and an outbox item; that window then changes no model and costs no attempt. Outside the window every path is as before.
- Twin gate (T172, D-107, D-516): `twin/` holds the twin copied from `sources/01_spec/hhq_twin/` with only the brand changed (Ask Hava, `askhava.example`). `twin/gate.py` runs every `_fixed` scenario, seeds 1 to 5, and fails `Twin` on any instance not waived by `twin/waivers.json`; it also requires the 57 frozen check keys and the `normal` negative control. `tests/twin/test_gate.py` holds the gate's self-tests, including deliberate breaks of the twin's rules run on a temporary copy. Since T007 (D-541 to D-544) the waiver file has no pinned entry, only class waivers with a committed evidence predicate and per-seed ceilings; the twin's matching checks and the simulated businesses' ticks read the license oracle `twin/oracle/license_scope.json`, generated from `docs/catalog/` by `twin/oracle/gen_license_scope.py` through the crosswalk in `twin/twin_config.json`, and the gate exits 2 on a stale or hand-edited oracle (the committed file differs from a fresh generation), a twin that reads the app's own license table, or an oracle that disagrees with `docs/catalog/matching_examples.yaml`; the twin charges the introduction fee by job size from the chosen quote (FEE-001, REC-002). Since T074 (D-1592 to D-1596) `twin.yml` builds `rules_bridge` from the pushed commit and runs the gate with `--bridge`: the twin's fee, status arrows and card matching are then the app's own (`twin/rules_bridge.py`), the app's quiet hours are checked at every texting decision (the twin's 9 p.m. close is the one known difference), and a fee probe compares the app's fee with the oracle's, so a wrong tier or a 2-month rule in `payments::rules` turns `Twin` red; `python3 -m twin chaos --baseline twin/chaos_baseline.json` runs the fault scenarios through the bridge and fails on a problem not in the committed baseline. T005 (D-517) changed the twin's intake to D-102 to D-105: keyword job types survive a trade answer, re-asks repeat the numbered options, the trade question offers up to three trades, license-boundary keyword job types stay candidates and a picked job type sets the trade; its C1 to C2e pins went then, and T007 removed the rest, so no pinned entry remains.
- Job timers (T031, D-805 to D-807): `jobs` queues one backstop timer per waiting status of section 6, counted from the entry (`app/jobs/src/rules.rs`, values in `JobStatus::wait`), as a `core.task` of kind `jobs.timeout` carrying the job ID, status and `jobs.job.version`; the handler, registered in `register_handlers()`, does nothing once the version has moved on. A wait the spec counts from a later event or extends or pauses (INT-019's last reply, JOB-014's decline, JOB-015's visit date, FEE-018, FEE-038, PICK-043, BIZ-034) is re-timed by the task owning that event through `jobs::api::restart_timer`, which bumps `jobs.job.timer_version` and moves `timer_from`, so the superseded timer does nothing (D-807). The other finer timers (the Intake reminder, the Connected call check and check-ins) are later tasks', each moving the job through `jobs::api::move_to`. T032 (D-871 to D-875) adds the timeout texts: Open's 6 p.m. move, No quotes yet's and Quotes ready's closes queue a `jobs.homeowner_text` `core.task` event (job ID, version, template ID and numbers only) in the move's transaction, as does `jobs::api::all_windows_ended`, which `quotes` (T046) calls once every card's window has ended: it closes a Quotes ready job with no choosable quote left, or queues the one TXT-042 text. `jobs` holds no homeowner contact, so the event's sender is `intake`'s (events go up, section 4 rule 2): it fills the quote-page link, applies quiet hours and calls `jobs::api::text_due` before sending; T240 built and registered it (`intake::api::run_job_text`, D-1301 to D-1303). `jobs::api::connected_reply` moves a Connected job on DONE, NO or PROBLEM (JOB-015).
- Stage proofs and the war-games gate (D-1473): `runner/proof.py` maps each clause of a stage's exit row (`docs/build-readiness.md` section 3) to the test files, marks (a test title, a mutant group, the axe widths) and nightly-main jobs (`kit/nightly.json` `results`) that prove it, for stages 1, 2 and 4 (`MACHINE_PROOFS`); stages 3, 5 and 6 stay hand-recorded with the reason (`HAND_RECORDED`). `scripts/validate_tasks.py` holds an eighth gate kind, `war-games-go` (T243), after the pre-war-games review (the task owning `docs/reviews/pre-war-games-review.md`) and before every task owning a `wargames/` path; it is the one `exact` gate, which `runner/outbox.py` clears only on a reply that is its phrase and nothing else.
- Integration-closure gate (T265, D-1760, D-2101, SEC-022): `scripts/stage_exit.py` has a fifth condition read from `docs/integration-closure.md`'s table. A `security`, `money` or `privacy` row that is not `closed` is judged at stage N only when every owner it names has a numeric `stage` at or below N in `kit/tasks.json` (otherwise it prints a `note:` line, so a later owner's merge never closes an earlier stage the runner re-checks on each claim); a judged row fails when every owner is DONE or SKIPPED; a row with no owner or an unknown task id fails at every stage; a `closed` row of those areas needs a commit SHA or `#PR` and a test name (`test_…` or a `TD-` id) in Evidence; other areas only print; a missing register fails. `evaluate`/`passed_stages` take the register path as an optional last argument and judge it only when given; the command line always passes `<root>/docs/integration-closure.md`. The runner's gate (`runner/__main__.py` `build.gate`) does not pass it yet (D-2101). Tests: `tests/test_stage_exit.py` (TD-OPS-31).

## 13. Routes

Every HTTP route the `web` process serves or a queued task's goal says it will serve, one row per method and path (T284, D-1868, D-2269). `Wired` is `yes` for a route `server::build_router` serves on `main` today, and otherwise the ID of the queued task that wires it; that task marks its own row `yes` in the same pull request, and a task that serves a route not listed adds its row as `yes`. `app/server/tests/route_wiring.rs` (TD-SEC-41) compiles this table in and probes the router `build_router` builds with every optional part configured: a `yes` row must reach its route, a task row must not, and a malformed row, a duplicate row or a task ID not in `kit/tasks.json` fails with the row quoted, and a header other than `| Method | Path | Module | Wired |` or a missing separator fails with that line quoted. T285's runner check reads the same table at each task's close. `Module` is the crate that owns the handler or page. A path parameter is written `{name}`, as in the code. The test-control routes (`/__test/...`, compiled only with the `test-control` feature, SEC-035) are not app routes and are not listed. Routes whose path no constant or goal fixes yet (T271's call status callback, T133's signed inbound email, T303's sign-out and step-up, the owner pages of T304, T305 and T310, T306's notice log, T309's help forms, the first photo task's `GET /photos/{token}` (D-1791 names no one task), and the public site pages no goal fixes a path for: home and how it works (T102), web chat (T103), the accessibility statement (T115), services and "What we cover now" (T201), the Spanish pages including the Spanish data pages (T202, T221)) get their row from the task that wires them (D-2270). The site's legal pages (T221's goal: `/terms`, `/privacy`, `/sms-terms`, `/your-data`, `/business-data`) and T201's help page (`askhava.com/help`, D-289) and ROC page (`askhava.com/help/roc-complaint`, WEB-011) are listed. The T116 Stage 0.5 static set is not served by the `web` process and is not listed. The test checks only the rows listed: an axum router cannot list its routes, so a route served with no row passes, and the task that merges a route adds its row.
